     1→# Chores-5
     2→
     3→## 20260216 - Future: config_key_value and rustc field migration
     4→
     5→### Context
     6→
     7→`[cargo.config_key_value]` is now implemented, providing a general-purpose mechanism for
     8→passing `--config KEY=VALUE` args to cargo. This enables per-package profile overrides
     9→without bleeding settings through RUSTFLAGS.
    10→
    11→### Open questions
    12→
    13→- **Auto-scoping:** Should tspec automatically scope config keys with
    14→  `profile.*.package.<name>` so users don't have to write the full path? Currently users
    15→  write exact cargo config keys — no magic.
    16→- **`[rustc]` field migration:** Several `[rustc]` fields (`opt_level`, `lto`,
    17→  `codegen_units`) duplicate what cargo profiles express. These could migrate to
    18→  `config_key_value` entries, but that's a separate follow-up. The existing fields work
    19→  fine via RUSTFLAGS for now.
    20→- **High-level fields (`panic`, `strip`):** These expand to both cargo and rustc flags.

> AGENT

The most recent commit `a98ebad` is "Update todo about adding -v passthrough to cargo" — so this is a known follow-up. The current `-v`/`-vv` shows tspec's own debug info but doesn't pass verbosity to cargo itself.

The top todo item is about **passing `--verbose` through to cargo** so you can see what `rustc` and the linker are actually doing. The question is about the verbosity mapping — should `-vvv` or `-vV` trigger cargo's verbose mode.

Would you like me to implement this? The approach would be:
- tspec `-v` = tspec verbose (current behavior) 
- tspec `-vv` = tspec verbose + spec details (current behavior)
- tspec `-vvv` = all the above + pass `-v` to cargo (shows rustc commands)

Or alternatively, a separate flag like `--cargo-verbose` / `-V` to independently control cargo's verbosity. What's your preference?

> DEVELOPER

I suggest -v -> tspec + cargo and -vv -> tspec + cargo

> AGENT

Makes sense — both levels pass `-v` to cargo, so you always see rustc/linker invocations when you ask for verbosity. Let me look at the current code to plan the implementation.

    12→pub use build::BuildCmd;
    13→pub use clean::CleanCmd;
    14→pub use clippy::ClippyCmd;
    15→pub use compare::CompareCmd;

    24→use std::ffi::OsString;
    25→use std::path::Path;
    26→use std::process::ExitCode;
    27→
    28→use crate::find_paths::{find_package_dir, find_project_root, get_package_name, is_pop};
    29→use crate::types::Verbosity;
    30→
    31→/// Trait for command execution.
    32→pub trait Execute {
    33→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    34→}
    35→
    36→/// Resolve a `-p` argument (path or name) to the actual cargo package name.
    37→/// Returns Some(name) for a package, None if it resolves to a workspace root
    38→/// with no `[package]` section (meaning "operate on all packages").
    39→pub(crate) fn resolve_package_arg(pkg: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    40→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    41→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg)?;
    42→    match get_package_name(&pkg_dir) {
    43→        Ok(name) => Ok(Some(name)),
    44→        Err(_) => Ok(None),
    45→    }
    46→}
    47→
    48→/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.
    49→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(
    50→    subcommand: &str,
    51→    args: &[OsString],
    52→    project_root: &Path,
    53→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    54→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    55→    cmd.arg(subcommand);
    56→    cmd.args(args);
    57→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
    58→    let status = cmd
    59→        .status()
    60→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", subcommand))?;
    61→    if status.success() {
    62→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    63→    } else {
    64→        bail!("cargo {} failed", subcommand);
    65→    }
    66→}
    67→
    68→/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package]).
    69→/// Returns None at a workspace root so that commands default to all-packages mode.
    70→pub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {
    71→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
    72→    // At a workspace root, don't treat it as a single package
    73→    if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() && !is_pop(&cwd) {
    74→        return None;
    75→    }
    76→    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()
    77→}
    78→

   410→
   411→/// Check if spec uses an abort-like panic mode that needs `-Zpanic_abort_tests` for testing.
   412→fn needs_panic_abort_tests(spec: &Spec) -> bool {
   413→    spec.panic
   414→        .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())
   415→        .unwrap_or(false)
   416→}
   417→
   418→/// Print command details at verbose level.
   419→fn print_verbose_command(cmd: &Command, verbosity: Verbosity) {
   420→    if verbosity >= Verbosity::Verbose {
   421→        let prog = cmd.get_program().to_string_lossy();
   422→        let args: Vec<String> = cmd
   423→            .get_args()
   424→            .map(|a| a.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   425→            .collect();
   426→        println!("[verbose] {} {}", prog, args.join(" "));
   427→        for (key, val) in cmd.get_envs() {
   428→            if let Some(v) = val {
   429→                println!(
   430→                    "[verbose] {}={}",
   431→                    key.to_string_lossy(),
   432→                    v.to_string_lossy()
   433→                );
   434→            }
   435→        }
   436→    }
   437→}
   438→
   439→/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)
   440→fn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> Result<Command> {
   441→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   442→
   443→    if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
   444→        cmd.arg(format!("+{}", tc));
   445→    } else if requires_nightly(spec, mode) {
   446→        cmd.arg("+nightly");
   447→    }
   448→
   449→    Ok(cmd)

1. Update the `Verbosity` doc comments to reflect the new semantics
2. Add `cargo -v` to the commands at both `-v` and `-vv` levels in `run_cargo`
3. Also pass `-v` to cargo in the no-spec path

    10→/// Clean build artifacts
    11→#[derive(Args)]
    12→pub struct CleanCmd {
    13→    /// Package to clean (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    14→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    15→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    16→    /// Package to clean (defaults to entire workspace/project)
    17→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    18→    pub package: Option<String>,
    19→    /// Only clean release artifacts
    20→    #[arg(short, long)]
    21→    pub release: bool,
    22→}
    23→
    24→impl Execute for CleanCmd {
    25→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, _verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    26→        let mut args: Vec<OsString> = Vec::new();
    27→        let pkg_arg = self.positional.as_ref().or(self.package.as_ref());
    28→        if let Some(pkg) = pkg_arg
    29→            && let Some(name) = resolve_package_arg(pkg)?
    30→        {
    31→            args.push("-p".into());
    32→            args.push(name.into());
    33→        }
    34→        if self.release {
    35→            args.push("--release".into());
    36→        }
    37→        execute_cargo_subcommand("clean", &args, project_root)
    38→    }
    39→}

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::Execute;
     7→use crate::find_paths::get_package_name;
     8→use crate::types::Verbosity;
     9→
    10→/// Install a package from a local path
    11→#[derive(Args)]
    12→pub struct InstallCmd {
    13→    /// Path to package (relative or absolute)
    14→    #[arg(long)]
    15→    pub path: PathBuf,
    16→    /// Force reinstall even if already installed
    17→    #[arg(short, long)]
    18→    pub force: bool,
    19→}
    20→
    21→impl Execute for InstallCmd {
    22→    fn execute(&self, _project_root: &Path, _verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    23→        let resolved = self
    24→            .path
    25→            .canonicalize()
    26→            .with_context(|| format!("path not found: {}", self.path.display()))?;
    27→
    28→        let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    29→        cmd.arg("install").arg("--path").arg(&resolved);
    30→
    31→        // Pass package name if we can determine it (needed for workspaces)
    32→        if let Ok(name) = get_package_name(&resolved) {
    33→            cmd.arg(&name);
    34→        }
    35→
    36→        if self.force {
    37→            cmd.arg("--force");
    38→        }
    39→
    40→        let status = cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo install")?;
    41→        if !status.success() {
    42→            bail!("cargo install failed");
    43→        }
    44→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    45→    }
    46→}
    47→

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::Path;

     6→use super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};

     9→use crate::cargo_build::build_package;
    10→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    11→use crate::types::Verbosity;
    12→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    13→
    14→/// Build package(s) with a translation spec
    15→#[derive(Args)]
    16→pub struct BuildCmd {
    17→    /// Package to build (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    18→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    19→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    20→    /// Package to build (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    21→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    22→    pub package: Option<String>,
    23→    /// Build all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    24→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    25→    pub workspace: bool,
    26→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to build with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    27→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    28→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    29→    /// Release build
    30→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    31→    pub release: bool,
    32→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    33→    #[arg(long)]
    34→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    35→    /// Strip symbols from binary after build
    36→    #[arg(short, long)]
    37→    pub strip: bool,
    38→    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    39→    #[arg(short, long)]
    40→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    41→}
    42→
    43→impl BuildCmd {
    44→    /// Resolve the effective profile from --release / --profile flags.
    45→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    46→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    47→            Some(p)
    48→        } else if self.release {
    49→            Some("release")
    50→        } else {
    51→            None
    52→        }
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→impl Execute for BuildCmd {
    57→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    58→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    59→
    60→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    61→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    62→            None
    63→        } else {
    64→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    65→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    66→                None => current_package_name(),
    67→            }
    68→        };
    69→
    70→        match resolved {
    71→            None => {
    72→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    73→                let results = build_all(
    74→                    &workspace,
    75→                    &self.tspec,
    76→                    cli_profile,
    77→                    self.strip,
    78→                    self.fail_fast,
    79→                    verbosity,
    80→                );
    81→                Ok(print_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    82→            }
    83→            Some(name) => {
    84→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    85→                    let result = build_package(&name, None, cli_profile, verbosity)?;
    86→                    if self.strip {
    87→                        strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    88→                    }
    89→                } else {
    90→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    91→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    92→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    93→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    94→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    95→                        let result =
    96→                            build_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, verbosity)?;
    97→                        if self.strip {
    98→                            strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    99→                        }
   100→                    }
   101→                }
   102→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   103→            }
   104→        }
   105→    }
   106→}

Good. Now I have the full picture. The changes needed:

1. **`types.rs`**: Update `Verbosity` doc comments to reflect `-v` = tspec + cargo verbose, `-vv` = tspec + cargo verbose + spec details
2. **`cargo_build.rs`**: Add `cmd.arg("-v")` before running cargo at both verbosity levels
3. **`cli.rs`**: Update the help text

The passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt) currently ignore verbosity. I should also pass `-v` to cargo for those. Let me also check the `run` and `compare` commands.

    28→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    29→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    30→    /// Release build

    34→    #[arg(long)]
    35→    pub profile: Option<String>,

    60→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    61→        let resolved = if self.workspace {

    66→                None => current_package_name(),
    67→            }
    68→        };

    81→                    if self.strip {

   105→        }

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::ffi::OsString;
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→use std::process::ExitCode;
     6→
     7→use super::{Execute, execute_cargo_subcommand, resolve_package_arg};
     8→use crate::types::Verbosity;
     9→
    10→/// Run clippy lints
    11→#[derive(Args)]
    12→pub struct ClippyCmd {
    13→    /// Package to check (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    14→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    15→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    16→    /// Package to check (defaults to entire workspace)
    17→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    18→    pub package: Option<String>,
    19→    /// Check all workspace packages
    20→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    21→    pub workspace: bool,
    22→}
    23→
    24→impl Execute for ClippyCmd {
    25→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, _verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    26→        let mut args: Vec<OsString> = Vec::new();
    27→        let pkg_arg = self.positional.as_ref().or(self.package.as_ref());
    28→        if let Some(pkg) = pkg_arg
    29→            && let Some(name) = resolve_package_arg(pkg)?
    30→        {
    31→            args.push("-p".into());
    32→            args.push(name.into());
    33→        }
    34→        if self.workspace {
    35→            args.push("--workspace".into());
    36→        }
    37→        execute_cargo_subcommand("clippy", &args, project_root)
    38→    }
    39→}
    40→

   210→### Changes
   211→
   212→1. **types.rs:** Move `build_std` to `CargoConfig`, add `rustflags` to `Spec`, delete `RustcConfig`
   213→2. **cargo_build.rs:** Update all `spec.rustc` references to new locations

   220→
   221→### Status
   222→
   223→Done — released in v0.11.6. Also renamed `config_key_value` to `config` with nested table support (dev2).
   224→
   225→## 20260218 - Add custom profile support
   226→
   227→### Context
   228→
   229→Currently `cargo.profile` only accepts `"debug"` or `"release"` (a Rust enum). Cargo supports
   230→custom profiles defined in `Cargo.toml` (e.g., `[profile.release-small]` with `inherits = "release"`),
   231→selected via `cargo build --profile <name>`. tspec should support selecting and overriding these
   232→custom profiles.
   233→
   234→Key insight: Custom profile **definitions** must live in `Cargo.toml` (cargo requirement — they
   235→need `inherits`). tspec's role is **selecting** a profile and **overriding** its settings via
   236→`cargo.config`.
   237→
   238→Cargo's profile-to-directory mapping: `dev` → `target/debug/`, `release` → `target/release/`,
   239→custom → `target/<name>/`.
   240→
   241→### Plan
   242→
   243→Multi-step (`-devN` series), version `0.12.0`.
   244→
   245→**Step 1 (dev1):** Core type change — replace `Profile` enum with `String`, update command
   246→generation (`"debug"`/`"dev"` → no flag, anything else → `--profile <name>`), update path
   247→resolution (`profile_dir_name()` helper), relax validation.
   248→
   249→**Step 2 (dev2):** CLI `--profile <name>` flag on build/run/test commands, thread through
   250→call chain, replace `release: bool` with `Option<String>`.
   251→
   252→**Step 3:** Final release — remove `-dev` suffix.
   253→
   254→### Changes
   255→
   256→**Step 1 (dev1):** Replaced `enum Profile { Debug, Release }` with `Option<String>`.
   257→Updated `apply_spec_to_command()` to emit `--profile <name>` for any non-debug/dev profile.
   258→Added `profile_dir_name()` helper for `dev` → `debug` directory mapping.
   259→Removed `validate_config_profiles()` — custom profiles in `[cargo.config]` are now accepted.
   260→Relaxed `validate_value("cargo.profile", ...)` to accept any string.
   261→
   262→**Step 2 (dev2):** Added `--profile <name>` flag to build/run/test commands (`conflicts_with = "release"`).
   263→Replaced `release: bool` with `cli_profile: Option<&str>` throughout the call chain:
   264→`build_package()`, `test_package()`, `apply_spec_to_command()`, `get_binary_path()`,
   265→`get_binary_path_simple()`, `build_all()`, `test_all()`, `run_all()`.
   266→Simplified `plain_cargo_build_release()` to a thin wrapper: `build_package(name, None, Some("release"))`.
   267→Added `validate_profile()` to catch undefined custom profiles early with a clear error
   268→message instead of cargo's confusing "missing inherits" error.
   269→Added `[profile.other]` to `Cargo.toml` and `tspec.other.ts.toml` for real custom profile testing.
   270→
   271→### Status
   272→
   273→Done — released in v0.12.0.
   274→
   275→## 20260218 - Allow -t glob patterns in all-packages mode
   276→
   277→### Context
   278→
   279→Currently `-t`/`--tspec` only works in single-package mode. In all-packages mode (pure workspace
   280→or `--workspace`), using `-t` bails with "requires a package target". The user wants `-t tspec*`
   281→to apply to all sub-packages in a workspace, resolving the glob per-package and skipping packages
   282→with no matches.
   283→
   284→### Plan
   285→
   286→**Step 1 (v0.12.5-dev1):** Update `all.rs` batch functions
   287→- Add `resolve_specs_for_member()` helper that calls `find_tspecs` per package, returns empty vec on no match
   288→- Change `build_all`, `test_all`, `run_all` signatures from `tspec: Option<&str>` to `tspec_patterns: &[String]`
   289→- Add `tspec_patterns: &[String]` parameter to `compare_all`
   290→- When patterns are empty: current behavior (each package uses its default spec)
   291→- When patterns are non-empty: resolve per-package, skip packages with no matches
   292→
   293→**Step 2 (v0.12.5-dev2):** Update command files
   294→- Remove the `bail!` for `-t` in all-packages mode from build, run, test, compare commands
   295→- Pass `&self.tspec` to the `_all` functions
   296→- Remove the `resolve_package_arg(".")` fallback hack (no longer needed — all-packages mode handles `-t` natively)
   297→
   298→**Step 3 (v0.12.5):** Final release
   299→
   300→### Status
   301→
   302→Done — released in v0.12.5.
   303→
   304→## 20260218 - Refactor summary printers
   305→
   306→### Context
   307→
   308→The 4 summary printers (`print_summary`, `print_test_summary`, `print_run_summary`,
   309→`print_compare_summary`) share nearly identical structure: compute column widths,
   310→conditionally show a Spec column, print header/rows/footer. The duplication grew
   311→with the 3-column Spec support in v0.12.6 and workspace name headers in v0.12.7.
   312→
   313→### Plan
   314→
   315→Refactor into a shared `print_summary_table(name, cmd, ...)` that handles the
   316→common pattern (header, column widths, has_spec logic, row printing). Each caller
   317→provides:
   318→- `name` — workspace/package name for the header
   319→- `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   320→- Column 3 label and per-row formatting (Status, Exit, Status+Size)
   321→- Footer text (passed/failed, ok/failed, errors)
   322→
   323→**Step 1 (v0.12.8-dev1):** Extract shared table printer, update all 4 callers
   324→**Step 2 (v0.12.8):** Final release
   325→
   326→### Status
   327→
   328→Done — released in v0.12.8.
   329→
   330→## 20260219 - Add toolchain field
   331→
   332→### Context
   333→
   334→When testing packages in rlibc-x that use `-Z build-std` (nightly-only), tspec auto-detects
   335→the need for nightly and hardcodes `+nightly`. A `toolchain` field lets specs explicitly
   336→declare which toolchain to use (e.g., `nightly`, `stable`, `nightly-2025-01-15`, `1.75`),
   337→giving users control and making specs self-documenting. When set, it overrides auto-detection;
   338→when absent, current behavior is preserved.
   339→
   340→### Changes
   341→
   342→- `src/types.rs` — Added `pub toolchain: Option<String>` to `Spec`
   343→- `src/cargo_build.rs` — `build_cargo_command()` checks `spec.toolchain` first, falls back to `requires_nightly()`
   344→- `src/testing.rs` — `test_package()` checks `spec.toolchain` first, falls back to `requires_nightly_for_test()`
   345→- `src/ts_cmd/edit.rs` — Added `("toolchain", FieldKind::Scalar)` to `FIELD_REGISTRY`
   346→- `README.md` — Added `toolchain` to example spec and field reference table
   347→
   348→### Status
   349→
   350→Done — released in v0.13.0.
   351→
   352→## 20260219 - Unified cargo runner + verbose
   353→
   354→### Context
   355→
   356→`build_package()` (cargo_build.rs) and `test_package()` (testing.rs) share ~80% of their code.
   357→The long-standing todo item "Refactor `build_package`, `test_package`, `plain_cargo_build_release`
   358→into a unified cargo runner" addresses this. Adding `--verbose` on top of the unified runner
   359→means verbose printing lives in one place instead of three.
   360→
   361→### Plan
   362→
   363→**Commit 2:** Unify `build_package`/`test_package` into `run_cargo()` with a `CargoMode` enum.
   364→Delete `testing.rs`, merge duplicate `requires_nightly` functions, keep thin wrappers.
   365→
   366→**Commit 3:** Add `--verbose`/`-v`/`-vv` support. `Verbosity` enum in types.rs, threaded through
   367→`Execute` trait and all commands. `-v` prints command line + env vars, `-vv` adds spec resolution
   368→details.
   369→
   370→### Status
   371→
   372→Done — released in v0.14.0.
   373→
```

  "new_string": "/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.\npub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(\n    subcommand: &str,\n    args: &[OsString],\n    project_root: &Path,\n    verbosity: Verbosity,\n) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n    cmd.arg(subcommand);\n    cmd.args(args);\n    if verbosity >= Verbosity::Verbose {\n        cmd.arg(\"-v\");\n    }\n    cmd.current_dir(project_root);"

  "old_string": "    fn execute(&self, _project_root: &Path, _verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n        let resolved = self\n            .path\n            .canonicalize()\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"path not found: {}\", self.path.display()))?;\n\n        let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n        cmd.arg(\"install\").arg(\"--path\").arg(&resolved);\n\n        // Pass package name if we can determine it (needed for workspaces)\n        if let Ok(name) = get_package_name(&resolved) {\n            cmd.arg(&name);\n        }\n\n        if self.force {\n            cmd.arg(\"--force\");\n        }",
  "new_string": "    fn execute(&self, _project_root: &Path, verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n        let resolved = self\n            .path\n            .canonicalize()\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"path not found: {}\", self.path.display()))?;\n\n        let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n        cmd.arg(\"install\").arg(\"--path\").arg(&resolved);\n\n        // Pass package name if we can determine it (needed for workspaces)\n        if let Ok(name) = get_package_name(&resolved) {\n            cmd.arg(&name);\n        }\n\n        if self.force {\n            cmd.arg(\"--force\");\n        }\n\n        if verbosity >= Verbosity::Verbose {\n            cmd.arg(\"-v\");\n        }"
}

 ### Status
 
 Done — released in v0.14.0.
+
+## 20260219 - Pass -v through to cargo
+
+### Context
+
+The `-v`/`-vv` flags added in v0.14.0 show tspec's own debug info (command line, env vars,
+spec resolution) but don't pass `-v` to cargo itself. Without cargo `-v`, users can't see
+the actual `rustc` and linker invocations — the whole reason they want verbose mode.
+
+### Changes
+
+Both `-v` and `-vv` now pass `-v` to cargo, so rustc/linker commands are always visible
+when verbosity is enabled:
+- `-v` = tspec command/env printing + cargo `-v`
+- `-vv` = tspec command/env + spec resolution details + cargo `-v`
+
+Updated:
+- `cargo_build.rs` — `run_cargo()` adds `-v` to cargo in both spec and no-spec paths
+- `cmd/mod.rs` — `execute_cargo_subcommand()` accepts `Verbosity`, adds `-v`
+- `cmd/clean.rs`, `cmd/clippy.rs`, `cmd/fmt.rs` — pass verbosity to `execute_cargo_subcommand`
+- `cmd/install.rs` — adds `-v` to `cargo install` when verbose
+- `cli.rs` — updated help text
+- `types.rs` — updated doc comments
+
+### Status
+
+Done — released in v0.14.1.

}

> DEVELOPER

Now I'd like to passthrough `-j N` i.e. jobs and in the future maybe others, is the code setup so adding to the passthrough commands is trivial

> AGENT

Not really — right now verbosity is threaded through as a separate parameter everywhere. Adding `-j N` the same way would mean another parameter in `run_cargo()`, `execute_cargo_subcommand()`, every `Execute::execute()` signature, and `apply_cargo_verbosity()`. It'd work but it doesn't scale.

A cleaner approach would be a `CargoFlags` struct that collects all passthrough flags in one place:

```rust
pub struct CargoFlags {
    pub verbosity: Verbosity,
    pub jobs: Option<u16>,
    // future: more flags here
}
```

Then `Execute::execute()`, `run_cargo()`, `execute_cargo_subcommand()`, etc. all take `&CargoFlags` instead of `Verbosity`. One parameter, one `apply_to_command(&self, cmd)` method, and adding a new flag is just:
1. Add the field to `CargoFlags`
2. Add the CLI arg to `Cli`
3. Handle it in `apply_to_command()`

No signature changes anywhere else. Want me to refactor to that pattern before committing?

> DEVELOPER

So just change this version to the new schema?

> AGENT

Yes, let me refactor the current verbosity-only passthrough into the `CargoFlags` pattern and add `-j N` at the same time. Let me check the remaining files I need to update.

     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace packages
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→
     5→use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
     6→use std::process::ExitCode;
     7→
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
    11→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, test_package};
    12→use crate::compare::{SpecResult, compare_specs, print_comparison};
    13→use crate::find_paths::find_tspecs;
    14→use crate::run::run_binary;
    15→use crate::tspec::spec_name_from_path;
    16→use crate::types::Verbosity;
    17→use crate::workspace::{PackageKind, WorkspaceInfo};
    18→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
    19→
    20→/// Normalize tspec patterns for per-package matching in all-packages mode.
    21→///
    22→/// Strips directory components so shell-expanded paths from the workspace root
    23→/// don't leak into sub-packages. Filters out non-tspec shell expansions
    24→/// (e.g. `target/`, `tools/`) — only `.ts.toml` files and glob patterns are kept.
    25→///
    26→/// Returns `None` if all patterns were filtered out (likely shell expansion of a
    27→/// glob that matched non-tspec entries). The caller should warn about quoting.
    28→fn normalize_tspec_patterns(patterns: &[String]) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    29→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    30→        return Some(Vec::new());
    31→    }
    32→    let filenames: Vec<String> = patterns
    33→        .iter()
    34→        .filter_map(|p| {
    35→            let name = Path::new(p)
    36→                .file_name()
    37→                .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
    38→                .unwrap_or_else(|| p.clone());
    39→            // Keep glob patterns (unexpanded) and actual tspec files
    40→            let is_glob = name.contains('*') || name.contains('?') || name.contains('[');
    41→            let is_tspec = name.ends_with(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    42→            if is_glob || is_tspec {
    43→                Some(name)
    44→            } else {
    45→                None
    46→            }
    47→        })
    48→        .collect();
    49→    if filenames.is_empty() && !patterns.is_empty() {
    50→        None // all patterns were filtered — likely shell expansion
    51→    } else {
    52→        Some(filenames)
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Warn that shell glob expansion likely ate the tspec pattern.
    57→pub fn warn_shell_glob_expansion(patterns: &[String]) {
    58→    eprintln!(
    59→        "Warning: -t arguments ({}) don't look like tspec files.",
    60→        patterns.join(", ")
    61→    );
    62→    eprintln!("  The shell likely expanded your glob before tspec could see it.");
    63→    eprintln!("  Quote the pattern to prevent shell expansion: -t 'pattern*'");
    64→}
    65→
    66→/// Resolve tspec patterns for a workspace member.
    67→///
    68→/// Returns the matching spec paths, or an empty vec if no patterns match.
    69→/// When `patterns` is empty, returns an empty vec (caller should use default behavior).
    70→fn resolve_specs_for_member(member_path: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    71→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    72→        return Vec::new();
    73→    }
    74→    find_tspecs(member_path, patterns).unwrap_or_default()
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
    78→fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    79→    match tspec {
    80→        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
    81→        None => String::new(),
    82→    }
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
    86→pub struct OpResult {
    87→    pub name: String,
    88→    pub spec: String,
    89→    pub success: bool,
    90→    pub message: String,
    91→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    92→}
    93→
    94→/// Build all workspace packages (excluding build tools)
    95→///
    96→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
    97→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
    98→pub fn build_all(
    99→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   100→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   101→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   102→    strip: bool,
   103→    fail_fast: bool,
   104→    verbosity: Verbosity,
   105→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   106→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   107→        Some(n) => n,
   108→        None => {
   109→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   110→            return Vec::new();
   111→        }
   112→    };
   113→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   114→
   115→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   116→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   117→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   118→            continue;
   119→        }
   120→
   121→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   122→
   123→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   124→            vec![None]
   125→        } else {
   126→            specs
   127→                .into_iter()
   128→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   129→                .collect()
   130→        };
   131→
   132→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   133→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   134→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, verbosity)
   135→            {
   136→                Ok(build_result) => {
   137→                    if strip
   138→                        && member.has_binary
   139→                        && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
   140→                    {
   141→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   142→                    }
   143→                    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
   144→                    OpResult {
   145→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   146→                        spec,
   147→                        success: true,
   148→                        message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
   149→                        size,
   150→                    }
   151→                }
   152→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   153→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   154→                    spec,
   155→                    success: false,
   156→                    message: e.to_string(),
   157→                    size: None,
   158→                },
   159→            };
   160→
   161→            let failed = !result.success;
   162→            results.push(result);
   163→
   164→            if failed && fail_fast {
   165→                return results;
   166→            }
   167→        }
   168→    }
   169→
   170→    results
   171→}
   172→
   173→/// Run all app packages sequentially
   174→///
   175→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   176→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   177→pub fn run_all(
   178→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   179→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   180→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   181→    strip: bool,
   182→    verbosity: Verbosity,
   183→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   184→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   185→        Some(n) => n,
   186→        None => {
   187→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   188→            return Vec::new();
   189→        }
   190→    };
   191→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   192→
   193→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
   194→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   195→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   196→            continue;
   197→        }
   198→
   199→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   200→
   201→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   202→            vec![None]
   203→        } else {
   204→            specs
   205→                .into_iter()
   206→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   207→                .collect()
   208→        };
   209→
   210→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   211→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   212→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, verbosity)
   213→            {
   214→                Ok(build_result) => {
   215→                    if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
   216→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   217→                    }
   218→                    match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
   219→                        Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
   220→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   221→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   222→                            success: true,
   223→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   224→                            size: None,
   225→                        },
   226→                        Err(e) => OpResult {
   227→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   228→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   229→                            success: false,
   230→                            message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   231→                            size: None,
   232→                        },
   233→                    }
   234→                }
   235→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   236→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   237→                    spec,
   238→                    success: false,
   239→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   240→                    size: None,
   241→                },
   242→            };
   243→
   244→            results.push(result);
   245→        }
   246→    }
   247→
   248→    results
   249→}

   251→/// Test all workspace packages
   252→///
   253→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   254→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   255→pub fn test_all(
   256→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   257→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   258→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   259→    fail_fast: bool,
   260→    verbosity: Verbosity,

   262→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   263→        Some(n) => n,
   264→        None => {
   265→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   266→            return Vec::new();

   268→    };
   269→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   270→
   271→    // Test regular packages (excluding Test kind which needs special handling)
   272→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   273→        if member.kind == PackageKind::Test {
   274→            continue; // Handle test packages separately
   275→        }
   276→
   277→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   278→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   279→            continue;

   281→

   292→
   293→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   294→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   295→            let result = match test_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, verbosity)
   296→            {

   313→            let failed = !result.success;

   315→
   316→            if failed && fail_fast {

   322→    // Handle test packages (like rlibc-x2-tests) - build and run all test binaries
   323→    for member in workspace.test_members() {
   324→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   325→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   326→            continue;
   327→        }
   328→
   329→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   330→
   331→        // Build the test package (builds all binaries)
   332→        let build_tspec = specs.first().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
   333→        let spec = spec_label(&build_tspec);
   334→        let build_result =
   335→            match build_package(&member.name, build_tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, verbosity) {
   336→                Ok(r) => r,

   351→
   352→        // Find and run all test binaries in the target directory
   353→        let profile_dir = crate::types::profile_dir_name(cli_profile.unwrap_or("debug"));
   354→        let target_dir = build_result.target_base.join(profile_dir);
   355→
   356→        // Look for binaries that end with "-tests" in the target directory
   357→        let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
   358→            .into_iter()
   359→            .flatten()
   360→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())

   368→                    .unwrap_or(false);

   373→        if test_binaries.is_empty() {

   400→                        }
   401→                    } else {

   403→                        OpResult {
   404→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   405→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   406→                            success: false,

   408→                            size: None,

   423→
   424→            let failed = !result.success;
   425→            results.push(result);
   426→
   427→            if failed && fail_fast {
   428→                return results;
   429→            }
   430→        }
   431→    }
   432→
   433→    results
   434→}
   435→
   436→/// A row for the summary table: package name, spec, and pre-formatted detail string.
   437→struct SummaryRow {
   438→    name: String,
   439→    spec: String,
   440→    detail: String,
   441→}
   442→
   443→/// Print a summary table with consistent formatting across all operations.
   444→///
   445→/// - `ws_name` — workspace/package name for the header
   446→/// - `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   447→/// - `detail_header` — column header for the detail column (e.g. "Status", "Status    Size", "Exit")
   448→/// - `rows` — pre-formatted rows
   449→/// - `footer` — footer line (e.g. "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed")
   450→fn print_summary_table(
   451→    ws_name: &str,
   452→    cmd: &str,
   453→    detail_header: &str,
   454→    rows: &[SummaryRow],
   455→    footer: &str,
   456→) {
   457→    let max_name_len = rows.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(7).max(7);
   458→    let has_spec = rows.iter().any(|r| !r.spec.is_empty());
   459→    let max_spec_len = if has_spec {
   460→        rows.iter().map(|r| r.spec.len()).max().unwrap_or(4).max(4)
   461→    } else {
   462→        0
   463→    };
   464→
   465→    println!();
   466→    print_header!(format!("{ws_name} {cmd} SUMMARY"));
   467→    if has_spec {
   468→        println!(
   469→            "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {detail_header}",
   470→            "Package",
   471→            "Spec",
   472→            nw = max_name_len,
   473→            sw = max_spec_len
   474→        );
   475→    } else {
   476→        println!(
   477→            "  {:width$}  {detail_header}",
   478→            "Package",
   479→            width = max_name_len
   480→        );
   481→    }
   482→
   483→    for row in rows {
   484→        if has_spec {
   485→            println!(
   486→                "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {}",
   487→                row.name,
   488→                row.spec,
   489→                row.detail,
   490→                nw = max_name_len,
   491→                sw = max_spec_len
   492→            );
   493→        } else {
   494→            println!(
   495→                "  {:width$}  {}",
   496→                row.name,
   497→                row.detail,
   498→                width = max_name_len
   499→            );
   500→        }
   501→    }
   502→
   503→    println!();
   504→    if !footer.is_empty() {
   505→        println!("  {footer}");
   506→    }
   507→    print_hline!();
   508→    println!();
   509→}
   510→
   511→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   512→pub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   513→    let mut passed = 0;
   514→    let mut failed = 0;
   515→
   516→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   517→        .iter()
   518→        .map(|r| {
   519→            let detail = if r.success {
   520→                passed += 1;
   521→                "[PASS]".to_string()
   522→            } else {
   523→                failed += 1;
   524→                "[FAIL]".to_string()
   525→            };
   526→            SummaryRow {
   527→                name: r.name.clone(),
   528→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   529→                detail,
   530→            }
   531→        })
   532→        .collect();
   533→
   534→    print_summary_table(
   535→        name,
   536→        "TEST",
   537→        "Status",
   538→        &rows,
   539→        &format!("Test: {passed} passed, {failed} failed"),
   540→    );
   541→
   542→    if failed > 0 {
   543→        ExitCode::from(1)
   544→    } else {
   545→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   546→    }
   547→}
   548→
   549→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   550→pub fn print_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   551→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   552→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   553→
   554→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   555→        .iter()
   556→        .map(|r| {
   557→            let status = if r.success {
   558→                ok_count += 1;
   559→                "[ OK ]"
   560→            } else {
   561→                failed_count += 1;
   562→                "[FAIL]"
   563→            };
   564→            let size_str = r.size.map(format_size).unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   565→            SummaryRow {
   566→                name: r.name.clone(),
   567→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   568→                detail: format!("{status}  {size_str:>6}"),
   569→            }
   570→        })
   571→        .collect();
   572→
   573→    print_summary_table(
   574→        name,
   575→        "BUILD",
   576→        "Status    Size",
   577→        &rows,
   578→        &format!("Build: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   579→    );
   580→
   581→    if failed_count > 0 {
   582→        ExitCode::from(1)
   583→    } else {
   584→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   585→    }
   586→}
   587→
   588→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   589→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   590→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   591→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   592→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   593→    } else {
   594→        format!("{}", bytes)
   595→    }
   596→}
   597→
   598→/// Result of a compare operation on a single package
   599→pub struct CompareResult {
   600→    pub op: OpResult,
   601→    pub specs: Vec<SpecResult>,
   602→}
   603→
   604→/// Compare all workspace packages that have binaries
   605→///
   606→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package discovers its own specs via default glob.
   607→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   608→pub fn compare_all(
   609→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   610→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   611→    fail_fast: bool,
   612→    verbosity: Verbosity,
   613→) -> Vec<CompareResult> {
   614→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   615→        Some(n) => n,
   616→        None => {
   617→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   618→            return Vec::new();
   619→        }
   620→    };
   621→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   622→
   623→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   624→        if !member.has_binary {
   625→            continue;
   626→        }
   627→
   628→        let spec_paths = if normalized.is_empty() {
   629→            find_tspecs(&member.path, &[]).unwrap_or_default()
   630→        } else {
   631→            let resolved = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   632→            if resolved.is_empty() {
   633→                continue;
   634→            }
   635→            resolved
   636→        };
   637→
   638→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   639→
   640→        let (op, specs) = match compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths, verbosity) {
   641→            Ok(spec_results) => (
   642→                OpResult {
   643→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   644→                    spec: String::new(),
   645→                    success: true,
   646→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   647→                    size: None,
   648→                },
   649→                spec_results,
   650→            ),
   651→            Err(e) => (
   652→                OpResult {
   653→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   654→                    spec: String::new(),
   655→                    success: false,
   656→                    message: e.to_string(),
   657→                    size: None,
   658→                },
   659→                Vec::new(),
   660→            ),
   661→        };
   662→
   663→        let failed = !op.success;
   664→        results.push(CompareResult { op, specs });
   665→
   666→        if failed && fail_fast {
   667→            break;
   668→        }
   669→    }
   670→
   671→    results
   672→}
   673→
   674→/// Print a summary for compare operations
   675→///
   676→/// With a single package, just prints its comparison table.
   677→/// With multiple packages, reprints all per-package tables then an overall OK/FAIL summary.
   678→pub fn print_compare_summary(name: &str, results: &[CompareResult]) -> ExitCode {
   679→    let has_failure = results.iter().any(|r| !r.op.success);
   680→
   681→    // Reprint per-package comparison tables together
   682→    for result in results {
   683→        if !result.specs.is_empty() {
   684→            print_comparison(&result.op.name, &result.specs);
   685→        }
   686→    }
   687→
   688→    // Only show overall COMPARE SUMMARY when there are multiple packages
   689→    if results.len() > 1 {
   690→        let mut ok_count = 0;
   691→        let mut failed_count = 0;
   692→
   693→        let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   694→            .iter()
   695→            .map(|r| {
   696→                let detail = if r.op.success {
   697→                    ok_count += 1;
   698→                    "[ OK ]".to_string()
   699→                } else {
   700→                    failed_count += 1;
   701→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   702→                };
   703→                SummaryRow {
   704→                    name: r.op.name.clone(),
   705→                    spec: r.op.spec.clone(),
   706→                    detail,
   707→                }
   708→            })
   709→            .collect();
   710→
   711→        print_summary_table(
   712→            name,
   713→            "COMPARE",
   714→            "Status",
   715→            &rows,
   716→            &format!("Compare: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   717→        );
   718→    }
   719→
   720→    if has_failure {
   721→        ExitCode::from(1)
   722→    } else {
   723→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   724→    }
   725→}
   726→
   727→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   728→pub fn print_run_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   729→    let mut error_count = 0;
   730→
   731→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   732→        .iter()
   733→        .map(|r| {
   734→            let detail = if r.success {
   735→                let code = r.message.strip_prefix("exit code: ").unwrap_or(&r.message);
   736→                format!("{code:>4}")
   737→            } else {
   738→                error_count += 1;
   739→                format!("ERROR: {}", r.message)
   740→            };
   741→            SummaryRow {
   742→                name: r.name.clone(),
   743→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   744→                detail,
   745→            }
   746→        })
   747→        .collect();
   748→
   749→    let footer = if error_count > 0 {
   750→        format!("Run: {error_count} error(s)")
   751→    } else {
   752→        String::new()
   753→    };
   754→
   755→    print_summary_table(name, "RUN", "Exit", &rows, &footer);
   756→
   757→    if error_count > 0 {
   758→        ExitCode::from(1)
   759→    } else {
   760→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   761→    }
   762→}
   763→

    13→/// Test package(s) with a translation spec
    14→#[derive(Args)]
    15→pub struct TestCmd {
    16→    /// Package to test (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    17→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    18→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    19→    /// Package to test (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    20→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    21→    pub package: Option<String>,
    22→    /// Test all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]

    25→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to test with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    26→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    27→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    28→    /// Release build
    29→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]

    31→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    32→    #[arg(long)]

    34→    /// Stop on first failure
    35→    #[arg(short, long)]
    36→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    37→}
    38→
    39→impl TestCmd {
    40→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    41→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    42→            Some(p)
    43→        } else if self.release {
    44→            Some("release")
    45→        } else {
    46→            None
    47→        }
    48→    }
    49→}
    50→
    51→impl Execute for TestCmd {
    52→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    53→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    54→
    55→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    56→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    57→            None
    58→        } else {
    59→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    60→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    61→                None => current_package_name(),
    62→            }
    63→        };
    64→
    65→        match resolved {
    66→            None => {
    67→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    68→                let results = test_all(
    69→                    &workspace,
    70→                    &self.tspec,
    71→                    cli_profile,
    72→                    self.fail_fast,
    73→                    verbosity,
    74→                );
    75→                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    76→            }
    77→            Some(name) => {
    78→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    79→                    test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, verbosity)?;
    80→                } else {
    81→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    82→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    83→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    84→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    85→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    86→                        test_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, verbosity)?;
    87→                    }
    88→                }
    89→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    90→            }
    91→        }
    92→    }
    93→}

    80→        }

     1→use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
     2→use std::collections::BTreeMap;
     3→use std::fmt;
     4→use std::path::PathBuf;
     5→
     6→use crate::options::{PanicMode, StripMode};
     7→
     8→/// Verbosity level for command output.
     9→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    10→pub enum Verbosity {
    11→    #[default]
    12→    Normal,
    13→    /// -v: print command line + env vars, pass -v to cargo
    14→    Verbose,
    15→    /// -vv: also print spec resolution details, pass -vv to cargo
    16→    Debug,
    17→}
    18→
    19→impl Verbosity {
    20→    pub fn from_count(count: u8) -> Self {
    21→        match count {
    22→            0 => Verbosity::Normal,
    23→            1 => Verbosity::Verbose,
    24→            _ => Verbosity::Debug,
    25→        }
    26→    }
    27→}
    28→
    29→/// A value in the `[cargo.config]` table.
    30→/// Uses `#[serde(untagged)]` so TOML bools/ints/strings/tables are deserialized naturally.
    31→/// We avoid `toml::Value` because it contains `Float(f64)` which doesn't implement `Eq`.
    32→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    33→#[serde(untagged)]
    34→pub enum ConfigValue {
    35→    Bool(bool),
    36→    Integer(i64),
    37→    String(String),
    38→    Table(BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>),
    39→}
    40→
    41→impl fmt::Display for ConfigValue {
    42→    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
    43→        match self {
    44→            ConfigValue::Bool(b) => write!(f, "{}", b),
    45→            ConfigValue::Integer(n) => write!(f, "{}", n),
    46→            ConfigValue::String(s) => write!(f, "\"{}\"", s),
    47→            ConfigValue::Table(map) => write!(f, "{:?}", map),
    48→        }
    49→    }
    50→}
    51→
    52→/// Flatten nested config into dotted key-value pairs for --config args.
    53→pub fn flatten_config(config: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    54→    let mut result = Vec::new();
    55→    flatten_inner(config, String::new(), &mut result);
    56→    result
    57→}
    58→
    59→fn flatten_inner(
    60→    map: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>,
    61→    prefix: String,
    62→    result: &mut Vec<(String, String)>,
    63→) {
    64→    for (key, value) in map {
    65→        let full_key = if prefix.is_empty() {
    66→            key.clone()
    67→        } else {
    68→            format!("{}.{}", prefix, key)
    69→        };
    70→        match value {
    71→            ConfigValue::Table(inner) => flatten_inner(inner, full_key, result),
    72→            other => result.push((full_key, other.to_string())),
    73→        }
    74→    }
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Map a profile name to the directory cargo uses in target/.
    78→/// `"dev"` → `"debug"`, `"release"` → `"release"`, custom → as-is.
    79→pub fn profile_dir_name(profile: &str) -> &str {
    80→    match profile {
    81→        "dev" => "debug",
    82→        other => other,
    83→    }
    84→}
    85→
    86→/// Cargo-specific configuration (flat struct)
    87→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    88→pub struct CargoConfig {
    89→    /// Build profile name (e.g., "debug", "release", "release-small", or any custom profile)
    90→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    91→    /// Target triple (e.g., "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl")
    92→    pub target_triple: Option<String>,
    93→    /// Custom target JSON file path
    94→    pub target_json: Option<PathBuf>,
    95→    /// Nightly-only -Z flags (e.g., ["panic-immediate-abort"])
    96→    #[serde(default)]
    97→    pub unstable: Vec<String>,
    98→    /// Custom target directory subdirectory for per-spec isolation.
    99→    /// Supports `{name}` (spec filename sans .ts.toml) and `{hash}` (8-char content hash).
   100→    pub target_dir: Option<String>,
   101→    /// Config values passed as `--config 'KEY=VALUE'` to cargo.
   102→    /// Supports both flat dotted keys and nested tables.
   103→    /// Each leaf entry becomes a separate `--config` arg.
   104→    #[serde(default)]
   105→    pub config: BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>,
   106→    /// Crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly only)
   107→    #[serde(default)]
   108→    pub build_std: Vec<String>,
   109→}
   110→
   111→/// Version script configuration for symbol visibility
   112→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
   113→pub struct VersionScript {
   114→    /// Symbols to keep global (e.g., ["_start"])
   115→    pub global: Vec<String>,
   116→    /// Pattern for local symbols (typically "*")
   117→    #[serde(default = "default_local")]
   118→    pub local: String,
   119→}
   120→
   121→fn default_local() -> String {
   122→    "*".to_string()
   123→}
   124→
   125→/// Linker configuration (flat struct)
   126→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
   127→pub struct LinkerConfig {
   128→    /// Linker arguments (e.g., ["-static", "-nostdlib"])
   129→    #[serde(default)]
   130→    pub args: Vec<String>,
   131→    /// Version script for symbol visibility (enables --gc-sections optimization)
   132→    pub version_script: Option<VersionScript>,
   133→}
   134→
   135→/// A translation spec
   136→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
   137→pub struct Spec {
   138→    /// High-level panic mode (sets both cargo -Z and rustc -C flags)
   139→    pub panic: Option<PanicMode>,
   140→
   141→    /// High-level strip mode (sets rustc -C strip=)
   142→    pub strip: Option<StripMode>,
   143→
   144→    /// Rust toolchain override (e.g., "nightly", "stable", "nightly-2025-01-15", "1.75")
   145→    pub toolchain: Option<String>,
   146→
   147→    #[serde(default)]
   148→    pub cargo: CargoConfig,
   149→    /// Raw flags passed through to RUSTFLAGS

   151→    pub rustflags: Vec<String>,
   152→    #[serde(default)]
   153→    pub linker: LinkerConfig,
   154→}

    10→/// Manage translation specs
    11→#[derive(Args)]
    12→pub struct TsCmd {
    13→    #[command(subcommand)]
    14→    command: TsCommands,
    15→}
    16→
    17→#[derive(Subcommand)]
    18→pub enum TsCommands {
    19→    /// List tspec files in workspace or for a specific package
    20→    List {
    21→        /// Package to list specs for (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    22→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    23→        package: Option<String>,
    24→        /// List all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    25→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    26→        all: bool,
    27→    },
    28→    /// Show a tspec's contents
    29→    Show {
    30→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    31→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    32→        package: Option<String>,
    33→        /// Show all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    34→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    35→        all: bool,
    36→        /// Tspec name (defaults to all tspec files)
    37→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    38→        tspec: Option<String>,
    39→    },
    40→    /// Show the content hash of a tspec
    41→    Hash {
    42→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    43→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    44→        package: Option<String>,
    45→        /// Hash all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    46→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    47→        all: bool,
    48→        /// Tspec name (defaults to package's tspec file)
    49→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    50→        tspec: Option<String>,
    51→    },
    52→    /// Create a new tspec file
    53→    New {
    54→        /// Name for the new tspec (default: "tspec")
    55→        #[arg(default_value = "tspec")]
    56→        name: String,
    57→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    58→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    59→        package: Option<String>,
    60→        /// Copy from existing tspec (package/spec or just spec name in same package)
    61→        #[arg(short = 'f', long = "from")]
    62→        from: Option<String>,
    63→    },
    64→    /// Set a field in a tspec (scalar value or replace entire array)
    65→    ///
    66→    /// For scalars: tspec ts set key value
    67→    /// For arrays: tspec ts set key val1 val2 ...
    68→    Set {
    69→        /// Field key (e.g., "cargo.profile", "linker.args")
    70→        key: String,
    71→        /// Value(s). For scalars, one value. For arrays, each arg is an element.
    72→        #[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
    73→        value: Vec<String>,
    74→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    75→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    76→        package: Option<String>,
    77→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    78→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    79→        tspec: Option<String>,
    80→    },
    81→    /// Remove a field from a tspec (preserves comments)
    82→    Unset {
    83→        /// Key to remove (e.g., "cargo.profile", "panic", "linker.args")
    84→        key: String,
    85→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    86→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    87→        package: Option<String>,
    88→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    89→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    90→        tspec: Option<String>,
    91→    },
    92→    /// Add items to an array field (append by default, or insert at position)
    93→    Add {
    94→        /// Field key (must be an array field, e.g., "linker.args")
    95→        key: String,
    96→        /// Items to add
    97→        #[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
    98→        value: Vec<String>,
    99→        /// Insert at this index instead of appending
   100→        #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   101→        index: Option<usize>,
   102→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   103→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   104→        package: Option<String>,
   105→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   106→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   107→        tspec: Option<String>,
   108→    },
   109→    /// Remove items from an array field (by value or by index)
   110→    Remove {
   111→        /// Field key (must be an array field, e.g., "linker.args")
   112→        key: String,
   113→        /// Items to remove by value (not used with --index)
   114→        #[arg(allow_hyphen_values = true)]
   115→        value: Vec<String>,
   116→        /// Remove item at this index instead of by value
   117→        #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   118→        index: Option<usize>,
   119→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   120→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   121→        package: Option<String>,
   122→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   123→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   124→        tspec: Option<String>,
   125→    },
   126→    /// Create a versioned backup of a tspec
   127→    Backup {
   128→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   129→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   130→        package: Option<String>,
   131→        /// Tspec to backup (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   132→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   133→        tspec: Option<String>,
   134→    },
   135→    /// Restore a tspec from a versioned backup
   136→    Restore {
   137→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   138→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   139→        package: Option<String>,
   140→        /// Backup tspec to restore from (required, e.g., "t2-001-abcd1234.ts.toml")
   141→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   142→        tspec: String,
   143→    },
   144→}
   145→
   146→impl Execute for TsCmd {
   147→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, _verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   148→        match &self.command {
   149→            TsCommands::List { package, all } => {
   150→                ts_cmd::list_tspecs(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all)?;
   151→            }
   152→            TsCommands::Show {
   153→                package,
   154→                all,
   155→                tspec,
   156→            } => {
   157→                ts_cmd::show_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   158→            }
   159→            TsCommands::Hash {
   160→                package,
   161→                all,
   162→                tspec,
   163→            } => {
   164→                ts_cmd::hash_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   165→            }
   166→            TsCommands::New {
   167→                name,
   168→                package,
   169→                from,
   170→            } => {
   171→                ts_cmd::new_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), name, from.as_deref())?;
   172→            }
   173→            TsCommands::Set {
   174→                key,
   175→                value,
   176→                package,
   177→                tspec,
   178→            } => {
   179→                ts_cmd::set_value(
   180→                    project_root,
   181→                    package.as_deref(),
   182→                    key,
   183→                    value,
   184→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   185→                )?;
   186→            }
   187→            TsCommands::Unset {
   188→                key,
   189→                package,
   190→                tspec,
   191→            } => {
   192→                ts_cmd::unset_value(project_root, package.as_deref(), key, tspec.as_deref())?;
   193→            }
   194→            TsCommands::Add {
   195→                key,
   196→                value,
   197→                index,
   198→                package,
   199→                tspec,
   200→            } => {
   201→                ts_cmd::add_value(
   202→                    project_root,
   203→                    package.as_deref(),
   204→                    key,
   205→                    value,
   206→                    *index,
   207→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   208→                )?;
   209→            }
   210→            TsCommands::Remove {
   211→                key,
   212→                value,
   213→                index,
   214→                package,
   215→                tspec,
   216→            } => {
   217→                ts_cmd::remove_value(
   218→                    project_root,
   219→                    package.as_deref(),
   220→                    key,
   221→                    value,
   222→                    *index,
   223→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   224→                )?;
   225→            }
   226→            TsCommands::Backup { package, tspec } => {
   227→                ts_cmd::backup_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec.as_deref())?;
   228→            }
   229→            TsCommands::Restore { package, tspec } => {
   230→                ts_cmd::restore_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec)?;
   231→            }
   232→        }
   233→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   234→    }
   235→}
   236→
```

{

  "old_string": "use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse std::collections::BTreeMap;\nuse std::fmt;\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\n\nuse crate::options::{PanicMode, StripMode};\n\n/// Verbosity level for command output.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]\npub enum Verbosity {\n    #[default]\n    Normal,\n    /// -v: print command line + env vars, pass -v to cargo\n    Verbose,\n    /// -vv: also print spec resolution details, pass -vv to cargo\n    Debug,\n}\n\nimpl Verbosity {\n    pub fn from_count(count: u8) -> Self {\n        match count {\n            0 => Verbosity::Normal,\n            1 => Verbosity::Verbose,\n            _ => Verbosity::Debug,\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse std::collections::BTreeMap;\nuse std::fmt;\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\nuse std::process::Command;\n\nuse crate::options::{PanicMode, StripMode};\n\n/// Verbosity level for command output.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]\npub enum Verbosity {\n    #[default]\n    Normal,\n    /// -v: print command line + env vars, pass -v to cargo\n    Verbose,\n    /// -vv: also print spec resolution details, pass -vv to cargo\n    Debug,\n}\n\nimpl Verbosity {\n    pub fn from_count(count: u8) -> Self {\n        match count {\n            0 => Verbosity::Normal,\n            1 => Verbosity::Verbose,\n            _ => Verbosity::Debug,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Global flags passed through to cargo commands.\n///\n/// Collected once from the CLI and threaded through all command execution.\n/// Adding a new passthrough flag only requires adding a field here and\n/// handling it in `apply_to_command()`.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]\npub struct CargoFlags {\n    pub verbosity: Verbosity,\n    /// Number of parallel jobs (-j N)\n    pub jobs: Option<u16>,\n}\n\nimpl CargoFlags {\n    /// Apply these flags to a cargo command.\n    pub fn apply_to_command(&self, cmd: &mut Command) {\n        match self.verbosity {\n            Verbosity::Verbose => {\n                cmd.arg(\"-v\");\n            }\n            Verbosity::Debug => {\n                cmd.arg(\"-vv\");\n            }\n            Verbosity::Normal => {}\n        }\n        if let Some(j) = self.jobs {\n            cmd.arg(\"-j\").arg(j.to_string());\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

    15→
    16→/// Built-in cargo profiles that don't require a `[profile.<name>]` definition.
    17→const BUILTIN_PROFILES: &[&str] = &["dev", "debug", "release", "test", "bench"];
    18→
    19→/// Validate that a custom profile is defined in the workspace Cargo.toml.
    20→/// Built-in profiles (dev, release, test, bench) are always valid.
    21→/// Custom profiles must have a `[profile.<name>]` section with `inherits`.
    22→pub fn validate_profile(profile: &str, workspace: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    23→    if BUILTIN_PROFILES.contains(&profile) {
    24→        return Ok(());
    25→    }
    26→    let cargo_toml_path = workspace.join("Cargo.toml");
    27→    let content = fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml_path)
    28→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml_path.display()))?;
    29→    let doc: toml::Value =
    30→        toml::from_str(&content).context("failed to parse workspace Cargo.toml")?;
