```
     1→use std::path::Path;
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→
     5→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
     6→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, plain_cargo_build_release};
     7→use crate::types::Verbosity;
     8→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
     9→
    10→/// Result of building a spec
    11→pub struct SpecResult {
    12→    pub name: String,
    13→    pub size: u64,
    14→}
    15→
    16→/// Compare multiple specs for a package
    17→pub fn compare_specs(
    18→    pkg_name: &str,
    19→    spec_paths: &[impl AsRef<Path> + std::fmt::Debug],
    20→    verbosity: Verbosity,
    21→) -> Result<Vec<SpecResult>> {
    22→    println!("Comparing {} builds:\n", pkg_name);
    23→
    24→    let mut results = Vec::new();
    25→
    26→    // Always build cargo --release baseline first (unstripped + stripped)
    27→    match build_baseline(pkg_name, verbosity) {
    28→        Ok((size, stripped_size)) => {
    29→            results.push(SpecResult {
    30→                name: "cargo --release".to_string(),
    31→                size,
    32→            });
    33→            results.push(SpecResult {
    34→                name: "cargo --release-strip".to_string(),
    35→                size: stripped_size,
    36→            });
    37→        }
    38→        Err(_) => {
    39→            println!("    baseline build failed, skipping");
    40→        }
    41→    }
    42→    println!();
    43→
    44→    for spec_path in spec_paths {
    45→        let spec_path = spec_path.as_ref();
    46→        let name = spec_path
    47→            .file_name()
    48→            .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
    49→            .unwrap_or_else(|| spec_path.display().to_string());
    50→
    51→        let size = build_spec(pkg_name, spec_path, verbosity)?;
    52→        results.push(SpecResult { name, size });
    53→        println!();
    54→    }
    55→
    56→    // Sort by size (smallest first)
    57→    results.sort_by_key(|r| r.size);
    58→
    59→    Ok(results)
    60→}
    61→
    62→/// Build baseline and return (unstripped_size, stripped_size)
    63→fn build_baseline(pkg_name: &str, verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<(u64, u64)> {
    64→    println!("  cargo --release:");
    65→
    66→    let build_result = plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name, verbosity)?;
    67→
    68→    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    69→    println!("    size: {} bytes", format_size(size));
    70→
    71→    strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    72→    let stripped_size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    73→
    74→    Ok((size, stripped_size))
    75→}
    76→
    77→fn build_spec(pkg_name: &str, spec_path: &Path, verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<u64> {
    78→    let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    79→    println!(
    80→        "  {}:",
    81→        spec_path.file_name().unwrap_or_default().to_string_lossy()
    82→    );
    83→
    84→    // Build using spec settings (profile, strip, etc. are all in the spec)
    85→    let build_result = build_package(pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), None, verbosity)?;
    86→
    87→    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    88→    println!("    size: {} bytes", format_size(size));
    89→
    90→    Ok(size)
    91→}
    92→
    93→pub fn print_comparison(pkg_name: &str, results: &[SpecResult]) {
    94→    let largest_size = results.iter().map(|r| r.size).max().unwrap_or(0);
    95→    let max_name_len = results.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(4);
    96→
    97→    // Format percent change: show reduction with minus sign, baseline as 0.0%
    98→    let fmt_pct = |size: u64| -> String {
    99→        if largest_size == 0 {
   100→            return "   0.0%".to_string();
   101→        }
   102→        let pct = ((largest_size as f64 - size as f64) / largest_size as f64) * 100.0;
   103→        if pct > 0.0 {
   104→            format!("{:>7.1}%", -pct)
   105→        } else {
   106→            "   0.0%".to_string()
   107→        }
   108→    };
   109→
   110→    println!();
   111→    print_header!(format!("{} COMPARE SUMMARY", pkg_name));
   112→    println!(
   113→        "  {:width$}  {:>10}  {:>8}",
   114→        "Spec",
   115→        "Size",
   116→        "Change",
   117→        width = max_name_len
   118→    );
   119→    for result in results {
   120→        println!(
   121→            "  {:width$}  {:>10}  {}",
   122→            result.name,
   123→            format_size(result.size),
   124→            fmt_pct(result.size),
   125→            width = max_name_len
   126→        );
   127→    }
   128→    print_hline!();
   129→    println!();
   130→}
   131→
   132→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   133→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   134→        format!("{:.3}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   135→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   136→        format!("{:.3}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   137→    } else {
   138→        format!("{}", bytes)
   139→    }
   140→}
   141→
```

  "old_string": "## 20260219 - Pass -v through to cargo\n\n### Context\n\nThe `-v`/`-vv` flags added in v0.14.0 show tspec's own debug info (command line, env vars,\nspec resolution) but don't pass `-v` to cargo itself. Without cargo `-v`, users can't see\nthe actual `rustc` and linker invocations — the whole reason they want verbose mode.\n\n### Changes\n\nBoth levels now pass matching verbosity to cargo:\n- `-v` = tspec command/env printing + cargo `-v`\n- `-vv` = tspec command/env + spec resolution details + cargo `-vv`\n\nUpdated:\n- `cargo_build.rs` — `run_cargo()` adds `-v` to cargo in both spec and no-spec paths\n- `cmd/mod.rs` — `execute_cargo_subcommand()` accepts `Verbosity`, adds `-v`\n- `cmd/clean.rs`, `cmd/clippy.rs`, `cmd/fmt.rs` — pass verbosity to `execute_cargo_subcommand`\n- `cmd/install.rs` — adds `-v` to `cargo install` when verbose\n- `cli.rs` — updated help text\n- `types.rs` — updated doc comments\n\n### Status\n\nDone — released in v0.14.1.",
  "new_string": "## 20260219 - CargoFlags: pass -v/-vv/-j through to cargo\n\n### Context\n\nThe `-v`/`-vv` flags added in v0.14.0 show tspec's own debug info (command line, env vars,\nspec resolution) but don't pass `-v` to cargo itself. Without cargo `-v`, users can't see\nthe actual `rustc` and linker invocations — the whole reason they want verbose mode.\n\nAlso needed: `-j N` for parallel jobs. Rather than threading each flag individually, introduced\na `CargoFlags` struct so future passthrough flags only need: add a field, add a CLI arg, handle\nit in `apply_to_command()`. No signature changes anywhere else.\n\n### Changes\n\nIntroduced `CargoFlags` struct in `types.rs` with `verbosity` and `jobs` fields, plus\n`apply_to_command()` method. Replaced `verbosity: Verbosity` parameter with `flags: &CargoFlags`\nthroughout the entire call chain:\n\n- `types.rs` — `CargoFlags` struct with `apply_to_command()`\n- `cli.rs` — added `-j`/`--jobs` global arg\n- `main.rs` — constructs `CargoFlags` from CLI\n- `cmd/mod.rs` — `Execute` trait takes `&CargoFlags`, `execute_cargo_subcommand()` updated\n- All command files — updated `execute()` signatures\n- `cargo_build.rs` — `run_cargo()`, `build_package()`, `test_package()`, `plain_cargo_build_release()`\n- `all.rs` — `build_all()`, `run_all()`, `test_all()`, `compare_all()`\n- `compare.rs` — `compare_specs()`, `build_baseline()`, `build_spec()`\n\n### Status\n\nDone — released in v0.14.1."
}

```
     1→# tspec - Translation Spec Build System
     2→
     3→A spec-driven build system for Rust that wraps cargo with configurable target triples, compiler flags, and linker options. Each tspec applies to a Cargo **package** — the unit with a `Cargo.toml`. If different crates need different compilation settings, they should be in separate packages.
     4→
     5→**Status:** Active development. Works with both workspaces and single-package projects (i.e. Plain Old Packages, POPs).
     6→
     7→## Installation
     8→
     9→```bash
    10→cargo install --path .
    11→```
    12→
    13→Or run directly:
    14→```bash
    15→cargo run -- build
    16→```
    17→
    18→## Contributing
    19→
    20→**All non-trivial changes must be on a feature branch** — never commit directly to `main`. This applies even for solo development.
    21→
    22→```bash
    23→git checkout -b feat-my-feature    # Create a branch
    24→# ... make changes, commit ...
    25→git checkout main                  # Switch back
    26→git merge --no-ff feat-my-feature  # Merge with merge commit (or squash-merge)
    27→git push
    28→```
    29→
    30→Branch naming: `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`.
    31→
    32→**Protect main on GitHub:** Enable branch protection (Settings > Branches) to block force pushes and optionally require PRs.
    33→
    34→For the full feature workflow (version bumps, notes, merge steps, Claude Code conventions), see [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md#feature-workflow).
    35→
    36→## Usage
    37→
    38→```bash
    39→tspec build                                # Build current package (or all in workspace) with tspec.ts.toml if present
    40→tspec build -p myapp                       # Build specific package with tspec.ts.toml if present
    41→tspec build myapp                          # Same, using positional argument
    42→tspec build .                              # Build the package in current directory
    43→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt          # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    44→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt.ts.toml  # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    45→tspec build -p myapp -r -s                 # Build release, strip symbols with tspec.ts.toml if present
    46→tspec run -p myapp                         # Build and run with tspec.ts.toml if present
    47→tspec test -p myapp                        # Build and test with tspec.ts.toml if present
    48→tspec build -w                             # Build all packages (even from inside a package dir)
    49→tspec build -t 'tspec*'                    # Build all packages using matching specs (quote the glob!)
    50→tspec build -t 'tspec-opt*'               # Build all packages with tspec-opt specs only
    51→tspec compare -p myapp                     # Compare binary sizes for a package
    52→tspec compare -p myapp -t *.ts.toml        # Compare using shell-expanded glob
    53→tspec compare                              # Compare all packages (workspace mode)
    54→tspec compare -w                           # Force workspace mode from inside a package
    55→tspec compare -w -f                        # Workspace mode, stop on first failure
    56→tspec -v build .                           # Verbose: show cargo command and env vars
    57→tspec -vv build .                          # Debug: also show spec resolution details
    58→```
    59→
    60→The `-p` flag or positional argument specifies a package by name or path (defaults to current directory if in a package, otherwise all packages). Paths like `.` are resolved to the actual cargo package name. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` means "all packages."
    61→Use `-w, --workspace` to force all-packages mode even when inside a package directory.
    62→The `-t` flag selects a tspec file; if omitted and `tspec.ts.toml` exists, it's used automatically. In workspace mode (no `-p`, or with `-w`), `-t` patterns are resolved per-package — each sub-package is searched for matching specs, and packages with no matches are skipped.
    63→
    64→**Important: quote glob patterns with `-t`.** The shell expands unquoted globs *before* tspec sees them. At a workspace root, `tspec build -t tspec*` may expand to literal filenames from the root directory (or worse, match `target/`), rather than being matched per-package. Always quote: `-t 'tspec*'`. tspec will warn if it detects shell-expanded non-tspec arguments.
    65→
    66→**Note on `cmd` vs `cmd .`:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), while `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata files. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    67→
    68→## tspec Files
    69→
    70→Translation specs are TOML files (conventionally `*.ts.toml`) that configure builds:
    71→
    72→```toml
    73→# tspec.ts.toml - all supported fields shown
    74→
    75→# Top-level high-level options (expand to lower-level cargo/rustc flags)
    76→panic = "abort"                            # "unwind" (default), "abort", "immediate-abort" (nightly)
    77→strip = "symbols"                          # "none" (default), "debuginfo", "symbols"
    78→toolchain = "nightly"                      # override toolchain (e.g., "nightly", "stable", "1.75")
    79→rustflags = ["-C", "relocation-model=static"]  # raw flags passed through to RUSTFLAGS
    80→
    81→[cargo]
    82→profile = "release"                        # "debug" (default), "release", or custom profile
    83→target_triple = "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl"
    84→target_json = "path/to/custom-target.json" # custom target spec (auto-adds -Z json-target-spec)
    85→unstable = ["panic-immediate-abort"]       # -Z flags (nightly only)
    86→target_dir = "{name}"                      # per-spec target dir; supports {name} and {hash} placeholders
    87→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]              # crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly)
    88→
    89→[cargo.config.profile.release]             # nested tables under [cargo.config] become individual
    90→opt-level = "z"                            # --config KEY=VALUE args to cargo; any valid cargo
    91→lto = true                                 # config key works (profile, build, target, etc.)
    92→codegen-units = 1
    93→
    94→[linker]
    95→args = ["-static", "-nostartfiles"]
    96→
    97→[linker.version_script]                    # symbol visibility control (enables --gc-sections)
    98→global = ["_start"]                        # symbols to keep
    99→local = "*"                                # default: "*" (hide everything else)
   100→```
   101→
   102→The top-level `panic` sets both cargo `-Z` and rustc `-C` flags automatically.
   103→
   104→### Key Concepts
   105→
   106→- **One tspec per package** - A tspec applies to all crates (targets) within a Cargo package. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
   107→- **tspec.ts.toml is optional** - Packages without one get plain `cargo build/test`
   108→- **Generated build.rs** - tspec generates temporary build.rs for scoped linker flags
   109→- **Spec comparison** - Compare binary sizes across different specs; always shows both `cargo --release` and `cargo --release-strip` baselines. Supports `-w` for all-packages mode — per-package tables are collected and printed together at the end. With a single package, only its comparison table is shown
   110→
   111→### How Linker Flags Work
   112→
   113→tspec generates a temporary `build.rs` to apply linker flags with binary-scoped directives:
   114→
   115→```rust
   116→// Generated by tspec - deleted after build
   117→fn main() {
   118→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-static");
   119→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-nostdlib");
   120→}
   121→```
   122→
   123→The only other alternative is to add these linker flags to RUSTFLAGS, but that
   124→affects ALL compilations (including dependency build scripts), causing crashes
   125→with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated build.rs scopes flags to just
   126→the binary.
   127→
   128→### Cargo Config Passthrough (`cargo.config`)
   129→
   130→The `[cargo.config]` section is a general-purpose passthrough to cargo's `--config KEY=VALUE` mechanism. Any nested tables under `[cargo.config]` are flattened into dotted keys and passed as `--config` args. This lets you override any [cargo configuration](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html) value at the command line.
   131→
   132→**Profile settings** are the most common use case:
   133→
   134→```toml
   135→[cargo.config.profile.release]
   136→opt-level = "z"
   137→codegen-units = 1
   138→lto = true
   139→```
   140→
   141→This produces: `cargo --config profile.release.opt-level="z" --config profile.release.codegen-units=1 --config profile.release.lto=true`
   142→
   143→**Other cargo config keys** work too:
   144→
   145→```toml
   146→[cargo.config.target.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu]
   147→linker = "clang"
   148→runner = "qemu-x86_64"
   149→```
   150→
   151→**Note:** Do not use `build.rustflags` in `[cargo.config]`. tspec sets the `RUSTFLAGS` env var (from top-level `rustflags`, `panic`, and `strip`), which takes precedence over `--config build.rustflags` per cargo's precedence rules. Use the top-level `rustflags` array instead.
   152→
   153→**Custom profiles:** Any profile defined in your workspace `Cargo.toml` (with `inherits`) can be used. tspec validates that custom profiles exist before invoking cargo. Use `cargo.profile` in the spec or `--profile` on the CLI.
   154→
   155→**Flat dotted keys** also work — TOML treats them equivalently:
   156→
   157→```toml
   158→[cargo.config]
   159→"profile.release.opt-level" = "z"
   160→```
   161→
   162→## tspec Management (ts subcommand)
   163→
   164→Manage spec files without manual TOML editing. All commands that modify files preserve comments and formatting.
   165→
   166→| Command | Description |
   167→|---------|-------------|
   168→| `ts list` | List tspec files found in the workspace or a specific package |
   169→| `ts show` | Display the TOML contents of a tspec file |
   170→| `ts hash` | Print the content hash (used in backup filenames) of a tspec |
   171→| `ts new` | Create a new tspec with defaults, or copy an existing one with `-f` (byte-for-byte) |
   172→| `ts set` | Set a scalar value or replace an entire array |
   173→| `ts unset` | Remove a field (scalar or array) from a tspec entirely |
   174→| `ts add` | Add items to an array field (append or insert at position) |
   175→| `ts remove` | Remove items from an array field (by value or by index) |
   176→| `ts backup` | Create a versioned backup copy (`name-NNN-hash.ts.toml`) |
   177→| `ts restore` | Restore a tspec by copying a versioned backup back to its original name |
   178→
   179→```bash
   180→# List / show / hash
   181→tspec ts list                            # List all tspec files
   182→tspec ts list -p myapp                   # List tspec files for a package
   183→tspec ts show -p myapp                   # Show all tspec contents
   184→tspec ts show -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show specific tspec-opt.ts.toml
   185→tspec ts hash -p myapp                   # Show content hash of tspec.ts.toml
   186→
   187→# Create new tspec files
   188→tspec ts new -p myapp                    # Create tspec.ts.toml with defaults
   189→tspec ts new experiment -p myapp         # Create experiment.ts.toml
   190→tspec ts new opt2 -p myapp -f tspec-opt  # Copy from existing spec (byte-for-byte)
   191→
   192→# Set scalar values
   193→tspec ts set strip symbols -p myapp              # Set a scalar field
   194→tspec ts set cargo.profile release               # No quoting needed
   195→
   196→# Set (replace) entire arrays — each element is a separate arg
   197→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib        # Replace array
   198→tspec ts set cargo.build_std core alloc           # Replace array
   199→tspec ts set rustflags -C relocation-model=static # Replace array
   200→
   201→# Add items to arrays (append by default)
   202→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections        # Append one item
   203→tspec ts add linker.args -nostdlib -pie            # Append multiple items
   204→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles        # Insert at position 0
   205→
   206→# Remove items from arrays
   207→tspec ts remove linker.args -nostdlib              # Remove by value
   208→tspec ts remove linker.args -static -pie           # Remove multiple by value
   209→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args                   # Remove by index
   210→
   211→# Remove a field entirely
   212→tspec ts unset strip -p myapp                      # Remove scalar field
   213→tspec ts unset linker.args -t tspec-opt            # Remove array field
   214→
   215→# Backup and restore (byte-for-byte copies)
   216→tspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Backup tspec.ts.toml → tspec-001-abcd1234.ts.toml
   217→tspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup tspec-opt.ts.toml
   218→tspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup
   219→```
   220→
   221→Backups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.
   222→
   223→`ts set`, `ts unset`, `ts add`, and `ts remove` use `toml_edit` for surgical editing — comments and formatting are preserved.
   224→`ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` use byte-for-byte file copy — comments are preserved exactly.
   225→
   226→### Supported keys
   227→
   228→Keys use two forms: `FIELD` for top-level fields (`panic`, `strip`, `rustflags`) or `TABLE.FIELD` for nested fields (`cargo.profile`, `linker.args`).
   229→
   230→| Key | Type | Values |
   231→|-----|------|--------|
   232→| `panic` | scalar | `unwind`, `abort`, `immediate-abort` |
   233→| `strip` | scalar | `none`, `debuginfo`, `symbols` |
   234→| `toolchain` | scalar | e.g. `nightly`, `stable`, `nightly-2025-01-15`, `1.75` |
   235→| `rustflags` | array | e.g. `-C relocation-model=static` |
   236→| `cargo.profile` | scalar | `debug`, `release`, or any custom profile defined in `Cargo.toml` |
   237→| `cargo.target_triple` | scalar | any string |
   238→| `cargo.target_json` | scalar | any path |
   239→| `cargo.target_dir` | scalar | any string (supports `{name}`, `{hash}`) |
   240→| `cargo.unstable` | array | e.g. `panic-immediate-abort` |
   241→| `cargo.config` | table | arbitrary `--config KEY=VALUE` passthrough; supports nested tables (e.g. `[cargo.config.profile.release]`) |
   242→| `cargo.build_std` | array | e.g. `core alloc` |
   243→| `linker.args` | array | e.g. `-static -nostdlib` |
   244→
   245→### Array operations
   246→
   247→Four orthogonal commands operate on arrays at two levels:
   248→
   249→| Level | Command | Purpose |
   250→|-------|---------|---------|
   251→| Field-level | `ts set` | Replace entire array with new values |
   252→| Field-level | `ts unset` | Remove the array field entirely |
   253→| Item-level | `ts add` | Append items (or insert at position with `-i`) |
   254→| Item-level | `ts remove` | Remove items by value (or by index with `-i`) |
   255→
   256→```bash
   257→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib   # replace all with these
   258→tspec ts unset linker.args                   # clear the field
   259→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections   # append (skips duplicates)
   260→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles  # insert at position
   261→tspec ts remove linker.args -static          # remove by value
   262→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args             # remove by index
   263→```
   264→
   265→Values starting with `-` work without quoting thanks to `allow_hyphen_values`.
   266→Use `--` as an escape hatch if a value collides with a flag like `-p`, `-t`, or `-i`.
   267→
   268→Note: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for "tspec management").
   269→
   270→## Testing
   271→
   272→```bash
   273→tspec test -p tspec           # Run tspec tests
   274→tspec test -p tspec-build     # Run tspec-build tests
   275→```
   276→
   277→## Project Structure
   278→
   279→```
   280→tspec/                  # Workspace root (also the main tspec package)
   281→  Cargo.toml            # [workspace] + [package] — workspace with root package
   282→  src/
   283→    lib.rs              # Library root, exposes modules
   284→    main.rs             # Entry point, dispatch
   285→    cli.rs              # Clap CLI definitions
   286→    types.rs            # Spec parameter types
   287→    tspec.rs            # Spec loading/saving/hashing
   288→    find_paths.rs       # Project/package/tspec/binary path discovery
   289→    workspace.rs        # Workspace package discovery
   290→    cargo_build.rs      # Unified cargo runner (build + test) + generated build.rs
   291→    run.rs              # Run command implementation
   292→    compare.rs          # Compare command (size comparison)
   293→    all.rs              # Batch operations (build_all, run_all, test_all, compare_all)
   294→    cmd/                # Command implementations (one file per command)
   295→    ts_cmd/             # Tspec management subcommands
   296→    binary.rs           # Binary operations (strip, size)
   297→  tests/
   298→    data/               # Test fixtures (TOML specs)
   299→    tspec_test.rs       # Integration tests
   300→  tspec-build/          # Library crate for build.rs integration
   301→    src/lib.rs          # Reads TSPEC_SPEC_FILE env var at build time
   302→  notes/                # Design docs and todo tracking
   303→```
   304→
   305→## Development
   306→
   307→See [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md) for current tasks.
   308→
   309→### Verification
   310→
   311→```bash
   312→tspec test -p tspec
   313→tspec test -p tspec-build
   314→tspec clippy
   315→tspec fmt --check
   316→```
   317→
   318→## Origin
   319→
   320→Originally developed as `xt` within the [rlibc-x](https://github.com/user/rlibc-x) project, then extracted to a standalone tool. The git history was preserved using `git subtree split`.

QQ: I used `-j=1` but `-j1` and `-j 1` seem to all work, I assume clap is giving us that?

> AGENT

Yes, clap handles all three forms automatically for short args with values. `-j 1`, `-j1`, and `-j=1` are all standard short option syntax that clap supports out of the box. The long form `--jobs=1` and `--jobs 1` also both work.

> DEVELOPER

but --jobs1 doesn't?

> AGENT

Correct — `--jobs1` doesn't work. For long options, the value must be separated by `=` or a space (`--jobs=1`, `--jobs 1`). For short options, all three work (`-j1`, `-j 1`, `-j=1`) because the single-character flag makes it unambiguous where the flag ends and the value begins. With `--jobs1`, clap sees the whole thing as the flag name `jobs1`, which doesn't exist.

This is standard POSIX/GNU option convention, not clap-specific.

> DEVELOPER

is `--verbose --verbose` == `-vv`?

> AGENT

Yes — with `ArgAction::Count`, each occurrence increments the counter. So these are all equivalent (count=2):

- `-vv`
- `-v -v`
- `--verbose --verbose`
- `--verbose -v`

> DEVELOPER

what about `--jobs --jobs`, does that increment too?

> AGENT

No — `jobs` uses a different action. It's declared as `Option<u16>`, so clap expects a *value* after it. `--jobs --jobs` would try to parse the second `--jobs` as the numeric value for the first one, and fail.

`ArgAction::Count` is specifically what makes `-v` stackable. `jobs` uses the default `ArgAction::Set` — it takes a value, and if repeated (`--jobs 2 --jobs 4`), the last one wins.

> DEVELOPER

Which actaully leads to the next question, I executed:
tspec clean ; tspec -v -j 1 build -p tspec -t tspec.3.ts.toml > 3-v.txt 2>&1
In Cargo.toml I have `profile.other.codegen-units=1` and `profile.other.package.proc-macro2.codegen-units=5`
In tspec.3.ts.toml I have `cargo.config.profile.other.codegen-units=3`

In 3-v.txt:
we see the `--config profile.other.codegen-units=3` in the [verboase] command line, great!
and we see for proc-macro2 `Running sccache .. -C codegen-units=5`, great again!!
but at the end of the proc-macro2 `Running sccache .. -C codegen-units=16`, Unepxected but "somebody" overrode our codegen-units!!!
This is actually good, it means controlling parameters per package can and is being done. My guess it's build.rs, thoughts?

> AGENT

Not build.rs — it's cargo compiling `proc-macro2` **twice** with different profiles:

1. **As a proc-macro dependency** (for `serde_derive`, `syn`, etc. that run on the host at compile time) — this uses the **build-override** profile, where `codegen-units=16` is the default for release-inheriting profiles
2. **As a regular dependency** (if anything in the target code depends on it) — this uses your profile settings, hence `codegen-units=5`

Proc-macro crates and their dependencies are compiled for the host, not the target, so cargo applies a separate profile to them. You can control it with:

```toml
[profile.other.build-override]
codegen-units = 1
```

This affects all build scripts and proc-macro dependencies. The `-v` output should show different `--crate-type` or metadata hashes for the two compilations if you look closely.

> DEVELOPER

Explain "compiled for the host, not the target". We are compiling our tspec app on this host for this target, so I'm confused?

> AGENT

Good point — in your case host and target are the same machine, so the distinction is invisible. But cargo still treats them as separate compilation contexts.

The distinction matters when **cross-compiling** — say building for `aarch64-unknown-linux-musl` on your `x86_64` machine. Proc macros like `serde_derive` are compiler plugins — they run *during* compilation to generate code. So they must run on your x86_64 build machine, not on the aarch64 target. Cargo compiles them for the host even when everything else targets aarch64.

Cargo maintains this host vs target separation *always*, even when they're the same triple. That's why you see two compilations of `proc-macro2` with different profiles — cargo treats the "build tools that run during compilation" pipeline as fundamentally separate from the "code that ends up in the final binary" pipeline, regardless of whether they happen to target the same architecture.

> DEVELOPER

Ok, is it correct to say "code compiled and run at compile time on the host while compiling for a target?"

> AGENT

Yes, that's exactly right. Proc macros, build scripts (`build.rs`), and their dependencies all fall into that category — code that runs on the host at compile time to produce or transform the code that ends up in the target binary.

> DEVELOPER

So the first line informs us that cargo is "Compiling proc-macro2 v1.0.106" and the next line runs `rustc` on crate `build_script_build` compiling `build.rs`:
Running `sccache /home/wink/.rustup/toolchains/stable-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/bin/rustc --crate-name build_script_build --edition=2021 /home/wink/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/proc-macro2-1.0.106/build.rs
a few lines below that we see "Compiling quote v1.0.44" and in that we see the only `--crate-name proc_macro2` so is the `Compiling proc-macro2 v1.0.106`, the first thing compiled, is actually a dependency of `quote v1.0.44` and that why it ends up being the first thing compiled, i.e. it's the first dependency leaf in the dependency tree that gets compiled?

> AGENT

Yes, that's right. Cargo does a topological sort of the dependency graph and starts with the leaves. `proc-macro2` depends only on `unicode-ident`, making it one of the shallowest leaves. The compilation order under "Compiling proc-macro2 v1.0.106" is:

1. Compile `build_script_build` (proc-macro2's `build.rs`)
2. Run the build script (produces cfg flags for the actual lib)
3. Compile the `proc_macro2` library crate — which shows up later in the output, near quote, because cargo parallelizes and interleaves work

So "Compiling proc-macro2" doesn't mean the library is compiled right then — it means cargo *started processing* that package, beginning with its build script. The actual `--crate-name proc_macro2` invocation comes after the build script finishes, and by that time cargo may already be printing "Compiling quote" because it's working on multiple things concurrently (unless you pass `-j1`, which would make the ordering strictly sequential).

> DEVELOPER

I did pass `-j 1`!

> AGENT

Then the ordering is strictly sequential and your reading is exactly right — `proc-macro2` is the first dependency leaf compiled, and the `--crate-name proc_macro2` library compilation appears later because cargo processes it as: build script compile → build script run → library compile. With `-j1` there's no interleaving, so wherever you see `--crate-name proc_macro2` in the output, that's exactly when it happened in the sequence.

> DEVELOPER

Who build the dependency tree cargo or rustc? (We see many crates being built are these recursive invocations from rustc or "linear (-j 1)" invocations from cargo?

> AGENT

Cargo. It resolves the full dependency graph from `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` upfront, topologically sorts it, then invokes `rustc` once per crate in the right order. Each `rustc` invocation is a flat, independent process — rustc compiles exactly the one crate it's told to and exits. It doesn't recurse or invoke itself.

With `-j1`, it's a simple linear sequence of `rustc` calls driven by cargo. With `-j4`, cargo launches up to 4 `rustc` processes in parallel, but only for crates whose dependencies have already finished.

> DEVELOPER

Where is that documented and can we get cargo to emit the dependency graph as we'll need it if/when we process tspec files on a per package basis?

> AGENT

Cargo has a few ways to get dependency info:

**`cargo tree`** — human-readable dependency tree:
```bash
cargo tree -p tspec
cargo tree -p tspec --edges all    # includes build deps and dev deps
cargo tree -p tspec -i proc-macro2 # invert: show what depends on proc-macro2
```

**`cargo metadata`** — machine-readable JSON with the full resolved dependency graph:
```bash
cargo metadata --format-version 1
```
This is what tspec already uses (via the `cargo_metadata` crate) for workspace discovery. The `resolve` section in the output contains the full dependency graph with `deps` and `dep_kinds` (normal, build, dev) for each package.

**Documentation:** The build process is documented in [The Cargo Book - Build Scripts](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/build-scripts.html) and the resolution algorithm in [Resolver](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/resolver.html). The `cargo metadata` output format is documented in [External Tools](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/external-tools.html).

For per-package tspec processing, `cargo metadata`'s `resolve` graph is what you'd want — it gives you the exact dependency edges and kinds, so you could walk the tree and apply different specs to different packages.

     5→**Question:** Should tspec augment or replace cargo?
     6→
     7→**Decision:** Augment cargo (Option 1)
     8→
     9→### Context
    10→
    11→Currently `xt` is designed to *augment* cargo:
    12→- It wraps `cargo build/test/run` with extra configuration (tspec files)
    13→- It generates temporary `build.rs` files for scoped linker flags
    14→- It requires an existing workspace structure
    15→
    16→Running `xt build` outside a workspace fails with "could not find workspace root".
    17→
    18→### Options Considered
    19→
    20→1. **Augment cargo (chosen)** - Stay as a build orchestrator that sits on top of cargo. Users still use cargo directly for simple builds, use tspec when they need spec-driven configuration.
    21→
    22→2. **Replace cargo** - Become a standalone build tool that doesn't require cargo's workspace structure. Much larger scope - would need to handle dependency resolution, compilation, etc.
    23→
    24→3. **Hybrid** - Work standalone for simple cases (single crate) but leverage workspace features when available.
    25→
    26→### Rationale
    27→
    28→Option 1 (augment) is the practical choice. Replacing cargo is a massive undertaking. The "workspace required" limitation can be relaxed by treating a single `Cargo.toml` (Plain Old Package / POP) as a trivial workspace.
    29→
    30→### First Task
    31→
    32→Make tspec work with both workspaces and POPs (Plain Old Packages - single crate projects without a workspace).(**done**)
    33→
    34→## 20260209 - Crate, Package, Cargo.toml
    35→
    36→**Conclusion, tspec provides a mechanism to describe how crates are compiled and linked.**
    37→
    38→### Rust Compilation unit
    39→
    40→General question about rust compilation units[[1]]
    41→
    42→[1]:https://claude.ai/share/c621b72e-bebe-494a-b9f8-639cbaf10f07
    43→
    44→Crate is the compilation unit of rust.
    45→* Binary crate: produces an executable
    46→* Library crate: produces a library
    47→
    48→Q: a binary crate uses multiple library crates can each library crate have a different tspec?
    49→* yes
    50→* but the final link step needs compatible link settings
    51→  * panic setting must be compatible
    52→  * abi affecting options must be compatible
    53→  * if not the linker should/will produce errors
    54→
    55→Q: does rustc or cargo enforce the definition of a crate?
    56→* rustc
    57→* cargo is just an orchestrator
    58→
    59→Q: So I cannot give rustc a file to compile?
    60→* No, rustc is always given a single file as the root of the crate.
    61→  see formal definition of crate in the rust reference below [[2]]
    62→
    63→  
    64→Q: If foo.rs is only `fn f() -> i8 { 1 }` it is a crate and can have a tspec.ts.toml
    65→associated with it to control its compilation?
    66→* yes, but as defined it would be private, it should be `pub fn f() -> i8 { 1 }`
    67→
    68→Q: sym tables (me free thinking wild possibilities here)
    69→* We may want capabilities to control symbol visibility/availability in tspec.
    70→  Symbols are tricky and if we did this it probably would use glob/regex patterns
    71→  as matching individual symbols would be fragile?
    72→  In any case we wouldn't want to disallow either case.
    73→
    74→
    75→### Package, Crates and Modules
    76→
    77→[book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-00-managing-growing-projects-with-packages-crates-and-modules.html):
    78→Rust has a number of features that allow you to manage your
    79→code’s organization, including which details are exposed,
    80→which details are private, and what names are in each scope
    81→in your programs. These features, sometimes collectively
    82→referred to as the module system, include:
    83→
    84→* Packages: A Cargo feature that lets you build, test, and share crates
    85→* Crates: A tree of modules that produces a library or executable
    86→* Modules and use: Let you control the organization, scope, and privacy of paths
    87→* Paths: A way of naming an item, such as a struct, function, or module
    88→
    89→#### Package
    90→
    91→[book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-01-packages-and-crates.html)
    92→A package is a bundle of one or more crates that provides a set of functionality. A package contains a Cargo.toml file that describes how to build those crates.
    93→
    94→#### Crate
    95→A rust crate, as stated by [[2]] says; "The Rust compiler is always invoked with a single source file as input, and always produces a single output crate." Thus, a crate is defined by the .rs file passed to the compiler and all of its dependencies.
    96→
    97→[2]:https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/crates-and-source-files.html
    98→
    99→Crates are imported using `extern crate` [3],
   100→"An extern crate declaration specifies a dependency on an external crate."
   101→
   102→In [The Rust Reference](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference) the term "package" is only found in extern-crates[3] and specifically [here](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/items/extern-crates.html?search=#r-items.extern-crate.name-restrictions)
   103→
   104→So from a language PoV there are crates but no packages. A package is a Cargo concept, not a Rust language concept.
   105→[3](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/items/extern-crates.html)
   106→
   107→#### Modules [ref](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html), [book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-00-managing-growing-projects-with-packages-crates-and-modules.html)
   108→
   109→* [intro](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html#r-items.mod.intro)
   110→  A module is zero or more items.
   111→* [def](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html#r-items.mod.def)
   112→  A module item is a module, surrounded in braces, named, and prefixed
   113→  with the keyword mod. A module item introduces a new, named module
   114→  into the tree of modules making up a crate.
   115→
   116→#### Cargo terminology
   117→
   118→* **Targets** — `[lib]`, `[[bin]]`, `[[example]]`, `[[test]]`, `[[bench]]`
   119→  sections in Cargo.toml. These are the crates within a package.
   120→* **Members** — packages listed in `[workspace] members` in the root Cargo.toml.
   121→
   122→Within a package you have targets, within a workspace you have members.
   123→
   124→### Package vs Crate granularity in tspec
   125→
   126→**Question:** tspec currently operates at the package level, but conceptually
   127→it describes crate compilation. How do we handle packages with multiple crates?
   128→
   129→**Decision:** One package = one tspec. If you need per-crate control, split into
   130→separate packages.
   131→
   132→#### Current behavior
   133→
   134→tspec operates at Cargo's package granularity:
   135→- `build_all` discovers workspace packages via `cargo metadata` and iterates,
   136→  calling `build_package()` for each
   137→- Single package: user passes `-p <name>`, `build_package()` runs `cargo build -p <name>`
   138→- `RUSTFLAGS` is set per invocation, applying to all crates within the package
   139→
   140→A package can contain multiple crates (targets). For example, tspec itself has two:
   141→- `src/lib.rs` → library crate (`--crate-type lib`)
   142→- `src/main.rs` → binary crate (`--crate-type bin`)
   143→
   144→Both get the same `RUSTFLAGS` from a single `cargo build -p tspec` invocation.
   145→
   146→#### Options considered for per-crate tspecs
   147→
   148→**1. Per-target tspecs within a package** — Use Cargo.toml `[lib]`/`[[bin]]`
   149→target names to find `<target-name>.ts.toml`, then invoke `cargo rustc`
   150→per target instead of `cargo build -p`.
   151→
   152→Problems:
   153→- `cargo rustc --lib -- <flags>` applies extra flags only to the named target,
   154→  not its dependencies, but `RUSTFLAGS` (which tspec currently uses) hits everything
   155→- Would need to stop using `RUSTFLAGS` and pass everything via `cargo rustc -- <flags>`
   156→- Would need to manage build ordering (lib before bin) — becoming a mini build
   157→  orchestrator on top of Cargo's orchestrator
   158→- Fighting against Cargo's design
   159→
   160→**2. Separate packages (chosen)** — If you need different compilation settings
   161→for lib vs bin, split them into separate packages. Each gets its own directory,
   162→`Cargo.toml`, and `tspec.ts.toml`. Workspace `members` ties them together.
   163→
   164→This works today with zero changes to tspec. It's idiomatic Rust — this is
   165→exactly why workspaces exist.
   166→
   167→#### Rationale
   168→
   169→Approach 2 keeps the model clean: **one package = one primary crate = one tspec**.
   170→Approach 1 fights Cargo's design for marginal benefit. For the common case
   171→(tspec itself: thin main.rs calling into lib.rs), there's no reason to compile
   172→the lib and bin differently. When you truly need different settings, the Rust
   173→answer is "make them separate packages."
   174→
   175→## 20260216 - Build mechanisms and per-package scoping
   176→
   177→### Current mechanisms (as of 0.10.9)
   178→
   179→tspec passes settings to cargo/rustc through five mechanisms, each with different
   180→scoping behavior:
   181→
   182→| Mechanism | tspec source | How it's passed | Scope |
   183→|---|---|---|---|
   184→| Top-level fields (`panic`, `strip`) | Global fields in spec | RUSTFLAGS `-C` + cargo `-Z` | Package + all deps |
   185→| `[cargo]` fields | `profile`, `target_triple`, `target_json`, `unstable`, `target_dir` | Cargo CLI args (`--release`, `--target`, `-Z`, `--target-dir`) | Package-scoped |
   186→| `[cargo.config_key_value]` | Arbitrary key-value pairs | `cargo --config 'KEY=VALUE'` | Depends on key (see below) |
   187→| `[rustc]` fields | `opt_level`, `lto`, `codegen_units`, `build_std`, `flags` | RUSTFLAGS `-C` | Package + all deps |
   188→| `[linker]` fields | `args`, `version_script` | Generated `build.rs` or `tspec-build` library | Per-binary |
   189→
   190→### The config_key_value scoping spectrum
   191→
   192→`[cargo.config_key_value]` is the most flexible mechanism because the scoping is
   193→determined by the key the user writes:
   194→
   195→```toml
   196→# Global — applies to the package and all its dependencies
   197→[cargo.config_key_value]
   198→"profile.release.opt-level" = "z"
   199→"profile.release.codegen-units" = 1
   200→
   201→# Per-package — applies ONLY to the named package
   202→[cargo.config_key_value]
   203→"profile.release.package.serde.opt-level" = 2
   204→"profile.release.package.tspec.codegen-units" = 1
   205→```
   206→
   207→This maps to cargo's `[profile.release.package.<name>]` syntax. The supported
   208→per-package profile fields are: `opt-level`, `codegen-units`, `overflow-checks`,
   209→`debug`, `debug-assertions`, `strip`, `lto`. Notably excluded: `panic` (must be
   210→consistent across the dependency graph) and `rpath` (whole-build concern).
   211→
   212→### Per-package status
   213→
   214→We now have the **mechanism** for per-package settings — users can manually write
   215→`profile.release.package.<name>.<field>` keys in `config_key_value` today. What's
   216→missing for full per-dependency tspec support:
   217→
   218→1. **Dependency tspec discovery** — walking the dep tree to find each dependency's
   219→   tspec file (known viable: `cargo package` preserves `*.ts.toml` files)
   220→2. **Automatic key scoping** — translating a dependency's tspec fields into
   221→   `--config 'profile.release.package.<dep>.<field>=...'` args
   222→3. **Conflict detection** — ensuring `panic` strategy is consistent across the
   223→   dependency graph
   224→
   225→None of these are implemented yet. The manual path works for experimentation.
   226→
   227→### The [rustc] field overlap
   228→
   229→Several `[rustc]` fields duplicate what `[cargo.config_key_value]` can express:
   230→
   231→| `[rustc]` field | Equivalent `config_key_value` key | Mechanism |
   232→|---|---|---|
   233→| `opt_level = "z"` | `"profile.release.opt-level" = "z"` | RUSTFLAGS vs `--config` |
   234→| `lto = true` | `"profile.release.lto" = true` | RUSTFLAGS vs `--config` |
   235→| `codegen_units = 1` | `"profile.release.codegen-units" = 1` | RUSTFLAGS vs `--config` |
   236→
   237→Tested: both paths produce identical binary sizes (confirmed with tspec-build test
   238→runner: 1,117,880 bytes both ways). Binaries are not bit-identical (~6.5% byte
   239→difference) but the optimization effect is the same. See chores-5 for details.
   240→
   241→The `[rustc]` fields that have NO `config_key_value` equivalent and must stay:
   242→- `build_std` — cargo `-Z build-std`, not a profile setting
   243→- `flags` — raw rustc flags with no profile equivalent
   244→
   245→Migration of the overlapping fields is possible but not urgent. Both paths work.
   246→The advantage of migrating: `config_key_value` supports per-package scoping via
   247→`profile.*.package.<name>`, while RUSTFLAGS hits everything uniformly.
   248→
   249→### Relationship to earlier design discussions
   250→
   251→- [chores-4: Profile support and section scoping][25] — design analysis
   252→- [chores-4: build.rs vs cargo --config][26] — confirmed `--config` is the right path
   253→- [chores-4: config_key_value implementation][27] — the implementation
   254→- [chores-5: RUSTFLAGS vs --config binary comparison][28] — same size, different bytes
   255→
   256→[25]: chores-4.md#20260215---design-profile-support-and-tspec-section-scoping
   257→[26]: chores-4.md#20260216---design-passing-tspec-fields-via-buildrs-vs-cargo---config
   258→[27]: chores-4.md#20260216---implement-cargoconfig_key_value-support
   259→[28]: chores-5.md#finding-rustflags-vs---config-produce-same-size-but-non-identical-binaries
   260→
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/notes/tspec-design.md",
  "old_string": "## 20260216 - Build mechanisms and per-package scoping",
  "new_string": "#### Cargo's host vs target compilation (implications for per-crate tspecs)\n\nWhen cargo builds a package, not all crates are compiled the same way. Cargo\ndistinguishes between **target** crates (your code, running on the target) and\n**host** crates (code that runs at compile time on the build machine):\n\n- **Target crates:** Your library, binary, and their dependencies. These get\n  your profile settings (`opt-level`, `codegen-units`, etc.) and RUSTFLAGS.\n- **Host crates:** Proc-macros (like `syn`, `quote`, `proc-macro2`) and build\n  scripts (`build.rs`). These run during compilation to generate code or\n  configure the build. They are compiled for the **host** machine, not the\n  target — which matters for cross-compilation but cargo maintains the\n  separation always.\n\nIn short: host crates are **code compiled and run at compile time on the host\nwhile compiling for a target**.\n\n**Build-override profiles** control host crate compilation. When you see\n`codegen-units=16` on proc-macro2 despite setting `codegen-units=3` in your\ntspec, that's because proc-macros use `[profile.<name>.build-override]` settings,\nnot your main profile. This is intentional — optimizing proc-macros for size\nwould slow compilation for no runtime benefit.\n\n**Implication for per-crate tspecs:** A future per-dependency tspec system would\nneed to understand this host/target split. Applying size-optimization flags to\nproc-macro dependencies would be counterproductive (slower builds, no effect on\nfinal binary size). The dependency graph metadata includes this distinction.\n\n#### Dependency graph: cargo vs rustc\n\nCargo, not rustc, owns the dependency graph:\n- **Cargo** resolves dependencies (from `Cargo.toml` + `Cargo.lock`), performs\n  topological sort, and invokes `rustc` once per crate in dependency order\n- **rustc** is stateless — it compiles exactly one crate per invocation and\n  exits. With `-j 1`, you see strictly linear `rustc` invocations from cargo,\n  not recursive calls\n\nThis means per-crate tspec processing would hook into **cargo's** dependency\nresolution, not rustc's compilation.\n\n#### Tools for dependency graph access\n\nTwo approaches for getting the dependency graph:\n\n1. **`cargo tree`** — Human-readable dependency tree visualization. Useful for\n   inspection but not machine-parseable.\n\n2. **`cargo metadata --format-version 1`** — Machine-readable JSON output with\n   the full resolved dependency graph. Includes package names, versions,\n   dependency edges, features, and target information. This is the standard\n   programmatic interface.\n\nThe **`cargo_metadata`** crate provides a typed Rust API over `cargo metadata`\noutput. tspec already uses this crate for workspace discovery (`WorkspaceInfo`),\nso extending it to walk the dependency graph for per-package tspec discovery\nwould require no new dependencies.\n\nA future per-dependency tspec system would use `cargo metadata` to:\n1. Walk the resolved dependency graph\n2. Identify which dependencies have tspec files (discovery)\n3. Determine host vs target classification for each dependency\n4. Generate scoped `--config 'profile.*.package.<dep>.<field>=...'` args\n5. Detect conflicts (e.g., incompatible panic strategies)\n\n## 20260216 - Build mechanisms and per-package scoping"
}
```

## Implementation Plan: Test Fixture Workspace for tspec

### 1. Branch and Version Strategy

Per CLAUDE.md conventions, this is a multi-step feature:

- Branch name: `feat-test-fixture-workspace`
- Initial version bump: `0.15.0-dev1` in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/Cargo.toml`
- Create `notes/chores-N.md` entry with context
- Update `notes/todo.md`
- Marker commit: `chore: Start feat-test-fixture-workspace branch, v0.15.0-dev1`

### 2. Fixture Workspace Directory Structure

Location: `tests/fixtures/workspace/`

```
tests/fixtures/workspace/
  Cargo.toml              # workspace root with [workspace] and [package] for fixture-app
  Cargo.lock              # committed for reproducible builds
  src/
    main.rs               # hello world binary for the root package "fixture-app"
  fixture-lib/
    Cargo.toml            # [package] name = "fixture-lib"
    src/
      lib.rs              # pub fn add(a: i32, b: i32) -> i32 { a + b } + a test
  tspec.ts.toml           # default spec (profile=release, strip=symbols)
  tspec-opt.ts.toml       # opt-level "z" spec for compare tests
```

**Design rationale:**

- The root package `fixture-app` is a binary that depends on `fixture-lib`, so building it exercises dependency resolution.
- `fixture-lib` is a pure library with a `#[test]` so `cargo test` has something to run.
- Two `.ts.toml` files at the workspace root exercise spec discovery (`find_tspecs` default pattern) and compare workflows.
- Keep everything minimal -- one-liner `main.rs`, two-liner `lib.rs` -- for fast builds.

#### File Contents

**`tests/fixtures/workspace/Cargo.toml`**:
```toml
[workspace]
members = ["fixture-lib"]

[package]
name = "fixture-app"
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2024"

[dependencies]
fixture-lib = { path = "fixture-lib" }
```

**`tests/fixtures/workspace/src/main.rs`**:
```rust
fn main() {
    println!("sum = {}", fixture_lib::add(2, 3));
}
```

**`tests/fixtures/workspace/fixture-lib/Cargo.toml`**:
```toml
[package]
name = "fixture-lib"
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2024"
```

**`tests/fixtures/workspace/fixture-lib/src/lib.rs`**:
```rust
pub fn add(a: i32, b: i32) -> i32 {
    a + b
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn add_works() {
        assert_eq!(add(2, 3), 5);
    }
}
```

**`tests/fixtures/workspace/tspec.ts.toml`** (default spec):
```toml
# Default fixture spec
strip = "symbols"

[cargo]
profile = "release"
```

**`tests/fixtures/workspace/tspec-opt.ts.toml`** (optimized spec):
```toml
# Optimized fixture spec for compare tests
strip = "symbols"

[cargo]
profile = "release"

[cargo.config.profile.release]
opt-level = "z"
lto = true
codegen-units = 1
```

### 3. Root Cargo.toml Change

Add the fixture workspace to the `exclude` list so the root workspace does not try to include it:

```toml
[workspace]
members = ["tspec-build"]
exclude = ["tests/fixtures/workspace"]
```

The `exclude` key is currently absent. Adding it prevents cargo from treating the fixture as a workspace member. This is standard cargo behavior -- `exclude` takes precedence over `members` globs and prevents auto-discovery.

### 4. Gitignore for Fixture Target Directory

Add to `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/.gitignore`:
```
tests/fixtures/workspace/target/
```

This prevents accidentally committing build artifacts from running `cargo build` directly in the fixture during development. The Cargo.lock should **not** be ignored -- it must be committed.

### 5. Test Helper Module

Create a new test helper file at `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixture.rs` (a module, not a test binary):

```rust
use std::fs;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use tempfile::TempDir;

/// Path to the fixture workspace within the repo.
fn fixture_source() -> PathBuf {
    PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
        .join("tests/fixtures/workspace")
}

/// Recursively copy a directory tree, skipping `target/` directories.
fn copy_dir_recursive(src: &Path, dst: &Path) -> std::io::Result<()> {
    fs::create_dir_all(dst)?;
    for entry in fs::read_dir(src)? {
        let entry = entry?;
        let file_type = entry.file_type()?;
        let name = entry.file_name();

        // Skip target/ directories (build artifacts)
        if file_type.is_dir() && name == "target" {
            continue;
        }

        let src_path = entry.path();
        let dst_path = dst.join(&name);

        if file_type.is_dir() {
            copy_dir_recursive(&src_path, &dst_path)?;
        } else {
            fs::copy(&src_path, &dst_path)?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Copy the fixture workspace to a temporary directory.
/// Returns (TempDir handle, path to workspace root within it).
/// The TempDir handle must be kept alive for the duration of the test.
pub fn copy_fixture_workspace() -> (TempDir, PathBuf) {
    let tmp = TempDir::new().expect("failed to create temp dir");
    let ws_root = tmp.path().join("workspace");
    copy_dir_recursive(&fixture_source(), &ws_root)
        .expect("failed to copy fixture workspace");
    (tmp, ws_root)
}
```

**Key design decisions in the helper:**

- Skips `target/` directories during copy to avoid bloat if someone has built the fixture locally.
- Returns both `TempDir` (must stay alive to prevent cleanup) and the `PathBuf` to the workspace root.
- Uses `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")` for reliable path resolution regardless of working directory.
- This is a plain module, not `#[path]` -- it will be included via `mod fixture;` in test files.

### 6. Integration Test File

Create `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/integration_test.rs`:

```rust
mod fixture;

use std::process::Command;

/// Helper to run tspec (the installed binary) in a given directory.
fn tspec_cmd(dir: &std::path::Path) -> Command {
    let mut cmd = Command::new("tspec");
    cmd.current_dir(dir);
    cmd
}

/// Helper to run cargo directly in a given directory.
fn cargo_cmd(dir: &std::path::Path) -> Command {
    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
    cmd.current_dir(dir);
    cmd
}
```

The actual test functions below. All `#[ignore]`-tagged because they invoke real cargo builds and are slow.

#### Test Group 2: Spec Loading in Context

```rust
use tspec::tspec::load_spec;

#[test]
#[ignore]
fn fixture_default_spec_loads() {
    let (_tmp, ws) = fixture::copy_fixture_workspace();
    let spec = load_spec(&ws.join("tspec.ts.toml")).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(spec.cargo.profile, Some("release".to_string()));

#[test]
#[ignore]
fn fixture_opt_spec_loads() {
    let (_tmp, ws) = fixture::copy_fixture_workspace();
    let spec = load_spec(&ws.join("tspec-opt.ts.toml")).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(spec.cargo.profile, Some("release".to_string()));
    // Verify config has opt-level z
    let flat = tspec::types::flatten_config(&spec.cargo.config);
    assert!(flat.iter().any(|(k, v)| k == "profile.release.opt-level" && v == "\"z\""));

#### Test Group 3: Workspace Discovery

```rust
#[test]
#[ignore]
fn fixture_workspace_discovery() {
    let (_tmp, ws) = fixture::copy_fixture_workspace();
    // WorkspaceInfo::discover() uses current_dir, so we need to run
    // in a subprocess or test a lower-level function.
    // Instead, test that cargo metadata works on the fixture.
    let output = cargo_cmd(&ws)
        .args(["metadata", "--no-deps", "--format-version", "1"])
        .output()
        .expect("cargo metadata failed");
    assert!(output.status.success());
    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
    assert!(stdout.contains("fixture-app"));
    assert!(stdout.contains("fixture-lib"));
}

#### Test Group 6: Mutation Tests (tests that modify the fixture copy)

```rust
#[test]
#[ignore]
fn fixture_build_without_spec_is_debug() {
    let (_tmp, ws) = fixture::copy_fixture_workspace();
    // Remove all spec files so tspec falls back to plain cargo
    std::fs::remove_file(ws.join("tspec.ts.toml")).unwrap();
    std::fs::remove_file(ws.join("tspec-opt.ts.toml")).unwrap();
    let status = tspec_cmd(&ws)
        .args(["build", "-p", "fixture-app"])
        .status()
        .expect("tspec build failed to start");
    assert!(status.success());
    assert!(ws.join("target/debug/fixture-app").exists());
}
```

### 7. Important Architecture Constraint: `find_project_root()` and `current_dir()`

The `run_cargo()` function calls `find_project_root()` which walks up from `std::env::current_dir()`. Integration tests that call `tspec` as a subprocess handle this naturally via `Command::current_dir()`. However, tests that call library functions directly (like `build_package()`) cannot reliably change the working directory because tests run in parallel.

**Recommendation:** Integration tests should primarily use subprocess invocation (`Command::new("tspec")` or `Command::new("cargo")`), not direct function calls. This tests the real end-to-end path and avoids the `current_dir` problem. Spec-loading tests that call library functions directly are fine because `load_spec()` takes an explicit path and does not depend on `current_dir`.

### 8. Running the Integration Tests

Since integration tests are `#[ignore]`, they do not run by default:

```bash
# Normal test run (skips slow integration tests)
tspec test -p tspec

# Run only integration tests (requires tspec to be installed)
cargo test --test integration_test -- --ignored

# Or via tspec itself (though this is recursive, it works because 
# the fixture is a separate workspace):
# cargo test -p tspec --test integration_test -- --ignored
```

A note: using `tspec test -p tspec` with `-- --ignored` is not currently supported by tspec's CLI (it does not pass trailing args to cargo test). This could be a future enhancement. For now, use `cargo test` directly to run the ignored tests.

### 9. Implementation Sequence

**Commit 1** (marker): `chore: Start feat-test-fixture-workspace branch, v0.15.0-dev1`
- Create branch
- Bump version
- Create chores entry
- Update todo.md

**Commit 2** (fixture): `feat: Add test fixture workspace`
- Create `tests/fixtures/workspace/` with all files
- Add `exclude = ["tests/fixtures/workspace"]` to root `Cargo.toml`
- Add `tests/fixtures/workspace/target/` to `.gitignore`
- Run `cargo build` and `cargo test` inside the fixture to generate `Cargo.lock`
- Commit the `Cargo.lock`

**Commit 3** (helper + tests): `feat: Add integration tests using fixture workspace`
- Create `tests/fixture.rs` (helper module)
- Create `tests/integration_test.rs` (test file)
- Run `tspec test -p tspec` to verify existing tests still pass
- Run `cargo test --test integration_test -- --ignored` to verify new tests pass
- Install: `tspec install --path .`

**Commit 4** (release): `chore: Release v0.15.0`
- Remove `-dev1` from version
- Update notes

### 10. Potential Challenges

1. **Cargo.lock generation**: The fixture workspace needs its own `Cargo.lock`. This must be generated by running `cargo build` or `cargo generate-lockfile` inside `tests/fixtures/workspace/` before committing.

2. **Edition 2024 availability**: The fixture uses `edition = "2024"` matching the project convention. If the CI or local toolchain does not support edition 2024, the fixture must be adjusted. But since tspec itself uses edition 2024, this should not be an issue.

3. **Parallel test isolation**: Since each test copies the fixture to its own `TempDir`, tests are fully isolated and can run in parallel safely.

4. **tspec binary availability**: Tests in Group 4-5 require `tspec` to be installed on PATH. This is fine for local development (per dogfooding convention) but needs a `which tspec` guard or skip-logic for CI environments that might not have tspec installed.

5. **Root workspace exclude**: If `exclude` is not set correctly, `cargo metadata` from the tspec root will try to resolve `fixture-lib` as a workspace member and fail. The `exclude` field must be added in the same commit that adds the fixture.

---

### Critical Files for Implementation

- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/Cargo.toml` - Add `exclude = ["tests/fixtures/workspace"]` to [workspace] section and bump version
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixtures/workspace/Cargo.toml` - New file: fixture workspace root with [workspace] + [package]
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/integration_test.rs` - New file: all integration tests using the fixture
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixture.rs` - New file: `copy_fixture_workspace()` helper with recursive copy
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/tspec_test.rs` - Existing integration test file to reference for conventions (pattern to follow)

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→use tspec::options::PanicMode;
     3→use tspec::tspec::{hash_spec, load_spec};
     4→use tspec::types::{CargoConfig, LinkerConfig, flatten_config};
     5→
     6→fn test_data(name: &str) -> PathBuf {
     7→    PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
     8→        .join("tests/data")
     9→        .join(name)
    10→}
    11→
    12→#[test]
    13→fn load_minimal_spec() {
    14→    let spec = load_spec(&test_data("minimal.toml")).unwrap();
    15→
    16→    assert_eq!(spec.panic, Some(PanicMode::Abort));
    17→
    18→    assert_eq!(spec.cargo.profile, Some("release".to_string()));
    19→    assert!(spec.cargo.target_triple.is_none());
    20→    assert!(spec.cargo.target_json.is_none());
    21→    assert!(spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty());
    22→    let flat = flatten_config(&spec.cargo.config);
    23→    assert!(flat.contains(&("profile.release.opt-level".to_string(), "\"z\"".to_string())));
    24→
    25→    assert!(spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty());
    26→    assert!(spec.rustflags.is_empty());
    27→
    28→    assert_eq!(spec.linker, LinkerConfig::default());
    29→}
    30→

    34→    assert!(result.is_err());
    35→}
    36→
    37→#[test]

    39→    let spec = load_spec(&test_data("minimal.toml")).unwrap();
    40→    let hash1 = hash_spec(&spec).unwrap();
    41→    let hash2 = hash_spec(&spec).unwrap();
    42→    assert_eq!(hash1, hash2);
    43→}

  "content": "# Plan: Test Fixture Workspace\n\n## Context\n\ntspec has 255 unit tests but zero integration tests that actually build real packages. The todo list's top item is \"Add a permanent test workspace for integration testing.\" Currently tests use `tempfile::TempDir` with synthetic files but never invoke real cargo builds. This leaves `compare.rs`, `all.rs`, and end-to-end workflows untested.\n\n## Approach: Embedded fixture + copy to TempDir\n\n### Fixture workspace: `tests/fixtures/workspace/`\n\nA minimal compilable workspace living in the repo:\n\n```\ntests/fixtures/workspace/\n  Cargo.toml              # [workspace] + [package] name=\"fixture-app\" (binary)\n  Cargo.lock              # committed for reproducibility\n  src/main.rs             # println using fixture-lib::add()\n  fixture-lib/\n    Cargo.toml            # [package] name=\"fixture-lib\"\n    src/lib.rs            # pub fn add() + one #[test]\n  tspec.ts.toml           # default: profile=release, strip=symbols\n  tspec-opt.ts.toml       # optimized: + opt-level=z, lto, codegen-units=1\n```\n\n### Root Cargo.toml changes\n\nAdd `exclude` to `[workspace]` (not the `[package]` exclude that already exists):\n\n```toml\n[workspace]\nmembers = [\"tspec-build\"]\nexclude = [\"tests/fixtures/workspace\"]\n```\n\nAdd `tests/fixtures/workspace/target/` to `.gitignore`.\n\n### Test helper: `tests/fixture.rs`\n\n- `copy_fixture_workspace() -> (TempDir, PathBuf)` — recursively copies fixture to tmp, skips `target/`\n- Uses `env!(\"CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR\")` for reliable path resolution\n\n### Integration tests: `tests/integration_test.rs`\n\nAll tests `#[ignore]` since they invoke real cargo/tspec builds (slow).\n\n**Test groups:**\n1. **Fixture validity** — `cargo build` and `cargo test --workspace` succeed on the copy\n2. **Spec loading** — `load_spec()` on fixture specs returns expected values\n3. **Workspace discovery** — `cargo metadata` finds both packages\n4. **End-to-end tspec build** — `tspec build -p fixture-app` produces binary in `target/release/`\n5. **Compare** — `tspec compare -p fixture-app` succeeds (covers compare.rs gap)\n6. **Mutation** — remove spec files, verify fallback to debug build\n\nTests in groups 4-6 invoke `tspec` as a subprocess (requires installed binary, which we always have per dogfooding convention).\n\n### Running\n\n```bash\ntspec test -p tspec                              # normal (skips #[ignore])\ncargo test --test integration_test -- --ignored  # integration only\n```\n\n## Files to create/modify\n\n- `Cargo.toml` — add workspace `exclude`, bump version\n- `.gitignore` — add fixture target dir\n- `tests/fixtures/workspace/` — all fixture files (new)\n- `tests/fixture.rs` — copy helper (new)\n- `tests/integration_test.rs` — integration tests (new)\n- `notes/todo.md` — move item to In Progress\n- `notes/chores-6.md` — new chores file with context\n\n## Verification\n\n```bash\ntspec test -p tspec                              # existing tests still pass\ncargo test --test integration_test -- --ignored  # new integration tests pass\ntspec install --path .\ntspec clippy\ntspec fmt --check\n```\n"
}

    17→  src/main.rs             # println using fixture-lib::add()
    18→  fixture-lib/
    19→    Cargo.toml            # [package] name="fixture-lib"
    20→    src/lib.rs            # pub fn add() + one #[test]
    21→  tspec.ts.toml           # default: profile=release, strip=symbols
    22→  tspec-opt.ts.toml       # optimized: + opt-level=z, lto, codegen-units=1
    23→```
    24→
    25→### Root Cargo.toml changes
    26→
    27→Add `exclude` to `[workspace]` (not the `[package]` exclude that already exists):
    28→
    29→```toml
    30→[workspace]
    31→members = ["tspec-build"]
    32→exclude = ["tests/fixtures/workspace"]
    33→```
    34→
    35→Add `tests/fixtures/workspace/target/` to `.gitignore`.
    36→
    37→### Test helper: `tests/fixture.rs`
    38→
    39→- `copy_fixture_workspace() -> (TempDir, PathBuf)` — recursively copies fixture to tmp, skips `target/`
    40→- Uses `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")` for reliable path resolution
    41→
    42→### Integration tests: `tests/integration_test.rs`
    43→
    44→All tests `#[ignore]` since they invoke real cargo/tspec builds (slow).
    45→
    46→**Test groups:**
    47→1. **Fixture validity** — `cargo build` and `cargo test --workspace` succeed on the copy
    48→2. **Spec loading** — `load_spec()` on fixture specs returns expected values
    49→3. **Workspace discovery** — `cargo metadata` finds both packages
    50→4. **End-to-end tspec build** — `tspec build -p fixture-app` produces binary in `target/release/`
    51→5. **Compare** — `tspec compare -p fixture-app` succeeds (covers compare.rs gap)
    52→6. **Mutation** — remove spec files, verify fallback to debug build
    53→
    54→Tests in groups 4-6 invoke `tspec` as a subprocess (requires installed binary, which we always have per dogfooding convention).
    55→
    56→### Running
    57→
    58→```bash
    59→tspec test -p tspec                              # normal (skips #[ignore])
    60→cargo test --test integration_test -- --ignored  # integration only
    61→```
    62→
    63→## Files to create/modify
    64→
    65→- `Cargo.toml` — add workspace `exclude`, bump version
    66→- `.gitignore` — add fixture target dir
    67→- `tests/fixtures/workspace/` — all fixture files (new)
    68→- `tests/fixture.rs` — copy helper (new)
    69→- `tests/integration_test.rs` — integration tests (new)
    70→- `notes/todo.md` — move item to In Progress

  "content": "# Plan: Test Fixture Workspace\n\n## Context\n\ntspec has 255 unit tests but zero integration tests that actually build real packages. This leaves `compare.rs`, `all.rs`, and end-to-end workflows untested.\n\ntspec supports three project structures that all need test coverage:\n1. **POP** (Plain Old Package) — single `Cargo.toml`, no `[workspace]`\n2. **POP + embedded workspace** — root has both `[package]` and `[workspace]` (like tspec itself)\n3. **POWS** (Pure Old Workspace) — workspace root with only `[workspace]`, no `[package]`\n\n## Approach: Embedded fixtures + copy to TempDir\n\n### Phase 1 (dev1): POP fixture only\n\nStart with the simplest structure to get the infrastructure right.\n\n#### Fixture: `tests/fixtures/pop/`\n\n```\ntests/fixtures/pop/\n  Cargo.toml              # [package] name=\"fixture-pop\", a bin crate, NO [workspace]\n  Cargo.lock              # committed for reproducibility\n  src/main.rs             # fn main() { println!(\"hello\"); }\n  tspec.ts.toml           # default: profile=release, strip=symbols\n  tspec-opt.ts.toml       # optimized: + opt-level=z, lto, codegen-units=1\n```\n\n#### Root Cargo.toml changes\n\nAdd `exclude` to `[workspace]` section:\n\n```toml\n[workspace]\nmembers = [\"tspec-build\"]\nexclude = [\"tests/fixtures\"]\n```\n\nExcludes the entire `tests/fixtures/` tree so future fixtures (dev2, dev3) are covered.\n\nAdd `tests/fixtures/*/target/` to `.gitignore`.\n\n#### Test helper: `tests/fixture.rs`\n\n- `copy_fixture(name: &str) -> (TempDir, PathBuf)` — copies `tests/fixtures/<name>/` to tmp, skips `target/`\n- `copy_pop() -> (TempDir, PathBuf)` — convenience for `copy_fixture(\"pop\")`\n- Uses `env!(\"CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR\")` for reliable path resolution\n\n#### Integration tests: `tests/integration_test.rs`\n\nAll tests `#[ignore]` since they invoke real cargo/tspec builds.\n\n**POP test groups:**\n1. **Fixture validity** — `cargo build` succeeds on the copy\n2. **Spec loading** — `load_spec()` on fixture specs returns expected values\n3. **End-to-end tspec build** — `tspec build .` produces binary in `target/release/`\n4. **Compare** — `tspec compare .` succeeds\n5. **Mutation** — remove specs, verify fallback to debug build\n\n### Phase 2 (dev2): POP + embedded workspace fixture\n\n```\ntests/fixtures/pop-ws/\n  Cargo.toml              # [workspace] members=[\"pop-ws-lib\"] + [package] name=\"fixture-pop-ws\"\n  Cargo.lock\n  src/main.rs             # uses fixture_pop_ws_lib::add()\n  pop-ws-lib/\n    Cargo.toml            # [package] name=\"fixture-pop-ws-lib\"\n    src/lib.rs            # pub fn add() + #[test]\n  tspec.ts.toml\n  tspec-opt.ts.toml\n```\n\nTests: workspace discovery, `tspec build -p`, `tspec test -w`, compare across packages.\n\n### Phase 3 (dev3): POWS fixture\n\n```\ntests/fixtures/pows/\n  Cargo.toml              # [workspace] members=[\"pows-app\",\"pows-lib\"], NO [package]\n  Cargo.lock\n  pows-app/\n    Cargo.toml            # [package] name=\"fixture-pows-app\"\n    src/main.rs\n    tspec.ts.toml\n  pows-lib/\n    Cargo.toml            # [package] name=\"fixture-pows-lib\"\n    src/lib.rs\n```\n\nTests: all-packages mode, per-package specs, `tspec build -w`.\n\n## Implementation sequence\n\n1. **Commit 1** (marker): `chore: Start feat-test-fixture-workspace branch, v0.15.0-dev1`\n   - Create branch, bump version, chores entry, update todo\n\n2. **Commit 2**: `feat: Add POP test fixture and integration test infrastructure`\n   - Create `tests/fixtures/pop/` with all files\n   - Update root `Cargo.toml` workspace exclude, `.gitignore`\n   - Generate `Cargo.lock` by running cargo in the fixture\n   - Create `tests/fixture.rs` helper and `tests/integration_test.rs` with POP tests\n   - All tests `#[ignore]`\n   - Verify: `tspec test -p tspec` passes, `cargo test --test integration_test -- --ignored` passes\n   - Install and verify\n\n3. **Commit 3**: `feat: Add POP+workspace test fixture, v0.15.0-dev2`\n   - Create `tests/fixtures/pop-ws/`, add tests\n\n4. **Commit 4**: `feat: Add POWS test fixture, v0.15.0-dev3`\n   - Create `tests/fixtures/pows/`, add tests\n\n5. **Commit 5** (release): `chore: Release v0.15.0`\n   - Remove -dev, update notes\n\n## Files to create/modify\n\n- `Cargo.toml` — add workspace `exclude`, bump version\n- `.gitignore` — add fixture target dirs\n- `tests/fixtures/pop/` — POP fixture files (new)\n- `tests/fixtures/pop-ws/` — POP+WS fixture files (new, dev2)\n- `tests/fixtures/pows/` — POWS fixture files (new, dev3)\n- `tests/fixture.rs` — copy helper (new)\n- `tests/integration_test.rs` — integration tests (new, expanded each dev)\n- `notes/todo.md` — move item to In Progress\n- `notes/chores-6.md` — new chores file\n\n## Verification (each step)\n\n```bash\ntspec test -p tspec                              #
