     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};
     7→use crate::all::{print_test_summary, test_all};
     8→use crate::cargo_build::test_package;
     9→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    10→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    11→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    12→
    13→/// Test package(s) with a translation spec
    14→#[derive(Args)]
    15→pub struct TestCmd {
    16→    /// Package to test (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    17→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    18→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    19→    /// Package to test (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    20→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    21→    pub package: Option<String>,
    22→    /// Test all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    24→    pub workspace: bool,
    25→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to test with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    26→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    27→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    28→    /// Release build
    29→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    30→    pub release: bool,
    31→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    32→    #[arg(long)]
    33→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    34→    /// Stop on first failure
    35→    #[arg(short, long)]
    36→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    37→}
    38→
    39→impl TestCmd {
    40→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    41→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    42→            Some(p)
    43→        } else if self.release {
    44→            Some("release")
    45→        } else {
    46→            None
    47→        }
    48→    }
    49→}
    50→
    51→impl Execute for TestCmd {
    52→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    53→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    54→
    55→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    56→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    57→            None
    58→        } else {
    59→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    60→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    61→                None => current_package_name(),
    62→            }
    63→        };
    64→
    65→        match resolved {
    66→            None => {
    67→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    68→                let results = test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, flags);
    69→                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    70→            }
    71→            Some(name) => {
    72→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    73→                    test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, flags)?;
    74→                } else {
    75→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    76→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    77→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    78→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    79→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    80→                        test_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, flags)?;
    81→                    }
    82→                }
    83→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    84→            }
    85→        }
    86→    }
    87→}
    88→
    89→#[cfg(test)]
    90→mod tests {
    91→    use super::*;
    92→    use crate::cli::Cli;
    93→    use clap::Parser;
    94→
    95→    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> TestCmd {
    96→        let mut full = vec!["tspec", "test"];
    97→        full.extend_from_slice(args);
    98→        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(full).unwrap();
    99→        match cli.command {
   100→            crate::cli::Commands::Test(cmd) => cmd,
   101→            _ => panic!("expected Test command"),
   102→        }
   103→    }
   104→
   105→    #[test]
   106→    fn package_optional() {
   107→        let cmd = parse(&[]);
   108→        assert!(cmd.package.is_none());
   109→    }
   110→
   111→    #[test]
   112→    fn package_explicit() {
   113→        let cmd = parse(&["-p", "myapp"]);
   114→        assert_eq!(cmd.package.as_deref(), Some("myapp"));
   115→    }
   116→
   117→    #[test]
   118→    fn tspec_empty_by_default() {
   119→        let cmd = parse(&[]);
   120→        assert!(cmd.tspec.is_empty());
   121→    }
   122→
   123→    #[test]
   124→    fn tspec_single_file() {
   125→        let cmd = parse(&["-t", "foo.ts.toml"]);
   126→        assert_eq!(cmd.tspec, vec!["foo.ts.toml"]);
   127→    }
   128→
   129→    #[test]

    13→
    14→/// Build package(s) with a translation spec
    15→#[derive(Args)]
    16→pub struct BuildCmd {
    17→    /// Package to build (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    18→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    19→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    20→    /// Package to build (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    21→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    22→    pub package: Option<String>,
    23→    /// Build all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    24→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    25→    pub workspace: bool,
    26→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to build with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    27→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    28→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    29→    /// Release build
    30→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    31→    pub release: bool,
    32→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    33→    #[arg(long)]
    34→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    35→    /// Strip symbols from binary after build
    36→    #[arg(short, long)]
    37→    pub strip: bool,
    38→    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    39→    #[arg(short, long)]
    40→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    41→}
    42→
    43→impl BuildCmd {
    44→    /// Resolve the effective profile from --release / --profile flags.
    45→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    46→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    47→            Some(p)
    48→        } else if self.release {
    49→            Some("release")
    50→        } else {
    51→            None
    52→        }
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→impl Execute for BuildCmd {
    57→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    58→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    59→
    60→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    61→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    62→            None
    63→        } else {
    64→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    65→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    66→                None => current_package_name(),
    67→            }
    68→        };
    69→
    70→        match resolved {
    71→            None => {
    72→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    73→                let results = build_all(
    74→                    &workspace,
    75→                    &self.tspec,
    76→                    cli_profile,
    77→                    self.strip,
    78→                    self.fail_fast,
    79→                    flags,
    80→                );
    81→                Ok(print_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    82→            }
    83→            Some(name) => {
    84→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    85→                    let result = build_package(&name, None, cli_profile, flags)?;
    86→                    if self.strip {
    87→                        strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    88→                    }
    89→                } else {
    90→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    91→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    92→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    93→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    94→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    95→                        let result = build_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, flags)?;
    96→                        if self.strip {
    97→                            strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    98→                        }
    99→                    }
   100→                }
   101→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   102→            }
   103→        }
   104→    }
   105→}

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use std::fs;
     3→use std::io::Write;
     4→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     5→use std::process::Command;
     6→
     7→use crate::find_paths::{
     8→    find_package_dir, find_project_root, find_tspec, get_binary_path, get_binary_path_simple,
     9→    get_package_name,
    10→};
    11→use crate::tspec::{expand_target_dir, load_spec, spec_name_from_path};
    12→use crate::types::{CargoFlags, Spec, Verbosity, flatten_config};
    13→
    14→const TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER: &str = "// Generated by tspec - do not edit";
    15→
    16→/// Built-in cargo profiles that don't require a `[profile.<name>]` definition.
    17→const BUILTIN_PROFILES: &[&str] = &["dev", "debug", "release", "test", "bench"];
    18→
    19→/// Validate that a custom profile is defined in the workspace Cargo.toml.
    20→/// Built-in profiles (dev, release, test, bench) are always valid.
    21→/// Custom profiles must have a `[profile.<name>]` section with `inherits`.
    22→pub fn validate_profile(profile: &str, workspace: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    23→    if BUILTIN_PROFILES.contains(&profile) {
    24→        return Ok(());
    25→    }
    26→    let cargo_toml_path = workspace.join("Cargo.toml");
    27→    let content = fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml_path)
    28→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml_path.display()))?;
    29→    let doc: toml::Value =
    30→        toml::from_str(&content).context("failed to parse workspace Cargo.toml")?;
    31→    if let Some(profiles) = doc.get("profile").and_then(|p| p.as_table())
    32→        && profiles.contains_key(profile)
    33→    {
    34→        return Ok(());
    35→    }
    36→    bail!(
    37→        "profile `{}` is not defined in {}. Custom profiles must be defined with \
    38→         `[profile.{}]` and an `inherits` field (e.g., `inherits = \"release\"`).",
    39→        profile,
    40→        cargo_toml_path.display(),
    41→        profile,
    42→    )
    43→}
    44→/// Check for spec settings that are likely misconfigurations.
    45→/// Returns a list of warning messages (printed at top and bottom of output).
    46→pub fn check_spec_misconfigurations(pkg_name: &str, spec: &Spec, pkg_dir: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
    47→    let mut warnings = Vec::new();
    48→    let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
    49→    let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
    50→
    51→    // -static with non-musl target (glibc + -static segfaults)
    52→    if has_linker_args {
    53→        let has_static = spec.linker.args.iter().any(|a| a == "-static");
    54→        let is_musl = spec
    55→            .cargo
    56→            .target_triple
    57→            .as_ref()
    58→            .is_some_and(|t| t.contains("musl"));
    59→        if has_static && !is_musl {
    60→            warnings.push(format!(
    61→                "Warning: -static linker arg without musl target for {}. \
    62→                 glibc + -static often segfaults; consider using a musl target triple.",
    63→                pkg_name
    64→            ));
    65→        }
    66→    }
    67→
    68→    // linker.args on lib-only package
    69→    if has_linker_args && !has_bin_target {
    70→        warnings.push(format!(
    71→            "Warning: linker.args ignored for {} (no binary target)",
    72→            pkg_name
    73→        ));
    74→    }
    75→
    76→    warnings
    77→}
    78→
    79→/// Print collected warnings again so they're visible after cargo output.
    80→pub fn reprint_warnings(warnings: &[String]) {
    81→    for w in warnings {
    82→        println!("{}", w);
    83→    }
    84→}
    85→
    86→pub fn warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale: bool) {
    87→    if had_stale {
    88→        println!("Warning: a stale build.rs was deleted, likely from a previous interrupted build");
    89→    }
    90→}
    91→
    92→/// Check if a build.rs file was generated by tspec.
    93→/// Matches either the marker comment or a file that only contains
    94→/// `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=` println lines (marker-less variant).
    95→fn is_tspec_generated_build_rs(content: &str) -> bool {
    96→    if content.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER) {
    97→        return true;
    98→    }
    99→    // Fallback: file only contains fn main, cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin printlns, and braces
   100→    let dominated_by_link_args = content
   101→        .lines()
   102→        .map(|l| l.trim())
   103→        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())
   104→        .all(|l| {
   105→            l == "fn main() {"
   106→                || l == "}"
   107→                || l.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER)
   108→                || l.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
   109→        });
   110→    dominated_by_link_args && content.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
   111→}
   112→
   113→/// Remove a stale tspec-generated build.rs left behind by an interrupted build.
   114→/// Returns true if a stale file was removed. Caller should print a warning after the build.
   115→pub fn remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {
   116→    let build_rs = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   117→    if let Ok(content) = fs::read_to_string(&build_rs)
   118→        && is_tspec_generated_build_rs(&content)
   119→    {
   120→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs);
   121→        return true;
   122→    }
   123→    false
   124→}
   125→
   126→/// Result of a successful build
   127→pub struct BuildResult {
   128→    pub binary_path: PathBuf,
   129→    /// The effective target directory base (e.g., "target/foo" if target_dir was set)
   130→    pub target_base: PathBuf,
   131→}
   132→
   133→/// Unified cargo runner for build and test operations.
   134→///
   135→/// Handles spec loading, build.rs generation, command construction, and cleanup.
   136→/// `cli_profile` is the profile from the CLI (e.g., "release", "release-small").
   137→/// `None` means debug (default). `Some("release")` is equivalent to `--release`.
   138→pub fn run_cargo(
   139→    mode: CargoMode,
   140→    pkg_name: &str,
   141→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   142→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   143→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   144→) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   145→    let verbosity = flags.verbosity;
   146→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   147→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
   148→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;
   149→
   150→    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)
   151→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
   152→
   153→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
   154→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
   155→
   156→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
   157→    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   158→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
   159→
   160→    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir
   161→    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
   162→        let s = load_spec(path)?;
   163→        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);
   164→        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;
   165→        (Some(s), td)
   166→    } else {
   167→        (None, None)
   168→    };
   169→
   170→    // Compute paths
   171→    let target_base = match &expanded_td {
   172→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   173→        None => workspace.join("target"),
   174→    };
   175→    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   176→        get_binary_path(
   177→            &workspace,
   178→            &pkg_name,
   179→            spec,
   180→            cli_profile,
   181→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   182→        )
   183→    } else {
   184→        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, cli_profile)
   185→    };
   186→
   187→    // Validate the effective profile exists before invoking cargo
   188→    let effective_profile = spec
   189→        .as_ref()
   190→        .and_then(|s| s.cargo.profile.as_deref())
   191→        .or(cli_profile);
   192→    if let Some(profile) = effective_profile {
   193→        validate_profile(profile, &workspace)?;
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    // Check for misconfigurations before running cargo
   197→    let spec_warnings = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   198→        check_spec_misconfigurations(&pkg_name, spec, &pkg_dir)
   199→    } else {
   200→        Vec::new()
   201→    };
   202→
   203→    let verb = match mode {
   204→        CargoMode::Build => "Building",
   205→        CargoMode::Test => "Testing",
   206→    };
   207→
   208→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo subcommand
   209→    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   210→        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();
   211→        println!("{} {} with spec {}", verb, pkg_name, path.display());
   212→
   213→        // -vv: print spec resolution details
   214→        if verbosity >= Verbosity::Debug {
   215→            let rel_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path);
   216→            println!("[debug] spec: {}", rel_path.display());
   217→            let tc = if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
   218→                format!("{} (from spec)", tc)
   219→            } else if requires_nightly(spec, mode) {
   220→                "nightly (auto-detected)".to_string()
   221→            } else {
   222→                "default".to_string()
   223→            };
   224→            println!("[debug] toolchain: {}", tc);
   225→            if let Some(td) = &expanded_td {
   226→                println!("[debug] target_dir: {} (expanded from spec)", td);
   227→            }
   228→            let eff = effective_profile.unwrap_or("debug");
   229→            let source = if spec.cargo.profile.is_some() {
   230→                "from spec"
   231→            } else if cli_profile.is_some() {
   232→                "from CLI"
   233→            } else {
   234→                "default"
   235→            };
   236→            println!("[debug] effective profile: {} ({})", eff, source);
   237→        }
   238→
   239→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
   240→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
   241→        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
   242→        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {
   243→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;
   244→        }
   245→
   246→        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec, mode)?;
   247→        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());
   248→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   249→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   250→
   251→        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs
   252→        cmd.env("TSPEC_SPEC_FILE", path.as_os_str());
   253→
   254→        apply_spec_to_command(
   255→            &mut cmd,
   256→            spec,
   257→            &workspace,
   258→            cli_profile,
   259→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   260→        )?;
   261→
   262→        // For test mode, append -Zpanic_abort_tests to RUSTFLAGS if needed
   263→        if mode == CargoMode::Test && needs_panic_abort_tests(spec) {
   264→            let existing = cmd
   265→                .get_envs()
   266→                .find(|(k, _)| k == &"RUSTFLAGS")
   267→                .and_then(|(_, v)| v)
   268→                .map(|v| v.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   269→                .unwrap_or_default();
   270→            let new_flags = if existing.is_empty() {
   271→                "-Zpanic_abort_tests".to_string()
   272→            } else {
   273→                format!("{} -Zpanic_abort_tests", existing)
   274→            };
   275→            cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", new_flags);
   276→        }
   277→
   278→        // Pass global flags through to cargo (-v/-vv, -j N, etc.)
   279→        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   280→
   281→        // -v: print command line and env vars
   282→        print_verbose_command(&cmd, verbosity);
   283→
   284→        cmd.status()
   285→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", mode.subcommand()))?
   286→    } else {
   287→        println!("{} {} (no tspec)", verb, pkg_name);
   288→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   289→        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());
   290→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   291→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   292→        if let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   293→            match p {
   294→                "debug" | "dev" => {}
   295→                _ => {
   296→                    cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   297→                }
   298→            }
   299→        }
   300→
   301→        // Pass global flags through to cargo (-v/-vv, -j N, etc.)
   302→        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   303→
   304→        // -v: print command line even without spec
   305→        print_verbose_command(&cmd, verbosity);
   306→
   307→        cmd.status()
   308→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", mode.subcommand()))?
   309→    };
   310→
   311→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
   312→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
   313→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
   314→    }
   315→
   316→    if !status.success() {
   317→        match &tspec_path {
   318→            Some(path) => {
   319→                let display_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path).display();
   320→                bail!(
   321→                    "cargo {} failed for `{}` with spec {}",
   322→                    mode.subcommand(),
   323→                    pkg_name,
   324→                    display_path
   325→                )
   326→            }
   327→            None => bail!("cargo {} failed for `{}`", mode.subcommand(), pkg_name),
   328→        }
   329→    }
   330→
   331→    if mode == CargoMode::Build {
   332→        println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
   333→    }
   334→    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);
   335→    reprint_warnings(&spec_warnings);
   336→    Ok(BuildResult {
   337→        binary_path,
   338→        target_base,
   339→    })
   340→}
   341→
   342→/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success.
   343→pub fn build_package(
   344→    pkg_name: &str,
   345→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   346→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   347→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   348→) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   349→    run_cargo(CargoMode::Build, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)
   350→}
   351→
   352→/// Test a package with a spec.
   353→pub fn test_package(
   354→    pkg_name: &str,
   355→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   356→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   357→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   358→) -> Result<()> {
   359→    run_cargo(CargoMode::Test, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)?;
   360→    Ok(())
   361→}
   362→
   363→/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.
   364→/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.
   365→pub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   366→    build_package(pkg_name, None, Some("release"), flags)
   367→}
   368→
   369→/// Generate a temporary build.rs with scoped linker flags from tspec.toml
   370→pub fn generate_build_rs(path: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec) -> Result<()> {
   371→    let mut lines = vec![TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER.to_string(), "fn main() {".to_string()];
   372→
   373→    for arg in &spec.linker.args {
   374→        lines.push(format!(
   375→            "    println!(\"cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={}={}\");",
   376→            crate_name, arg
   377→        ));
   378→    }
   379→
   380→    lines.push("}".to_string());
   381→    fs::write(path, lines.join("\n")).context("failed to write build.rs")?;
   382→    Ok(())
   383→}
   384→
   385→/// Cargo operation mode
   386→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
   387→pub enum CargoMode {
   388→    Build,
   389→    Test,
   390→}
   391→
   392→impl CargoMode {
   393→    fn subcommand(&self) -> &'static str {
   394→        match self {
   395→            CargoMode::Build => "build",
   396→            CargoMode::Test => "test",
   397→        }
   398→    }
   399→}
   400→
   401→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain.
   402→/// For Test mode, panic=abort also needs nightly because `-Zpanic_abort_tests` is nightly-only.
   403→fn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> bool {
   404→    let panic_needs_nightly = match mode {
   405→        CargoMode::Test => spec
   406→            .panic
   407→            .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())
   408→            .unwrap_or(false),
   409→        CargoMode::Build => spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false),
   410→    };
   411→
   412→    let has_build_std = !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty();
   413→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
   414→
   415→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
   416→}
   417→
   418→/// Check if spec uses an abort-like panic mode that needs `-Zpanic_abort_tests` for testing.
   419→fn needs_panic_abort_tests(spec: &Spec) -> bool {
   420→    spec.panic
   421→        .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())
   422→        .unwrap_or(false)
   423→}
   424→
   425→/// Print command details at verbose level.
   426→fn print_verbose_command(cmd: &Command, verbosity: Verbosity) {
   427→    if verbosity >= Verbosity::Verbose {
   428→        let prog = cmd.get_program().to_string_lossy();
   429→        let args: Vec<String> = cmd
   430→            .get_args()
   431→            .map(|a| a.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   432→            .collect();
   433→        println!("[verbose] {} {}", prog, args.join(" "));
   434→        for (key, val) in cmd.get_envs() {
   435→            if let Some(v) = val {
   436→                println!(
   437→                    "[verbose] {}={}",
   438→                    key.to_string_lossy(),
   439→                    v.to_string_lossy()
   440→                );
   441→            }
   442→        }
   443→    }
   444→}
   445→
   446→/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)
   447→fn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> Result<Command> {
   448→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   449→
   450→    if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
   451→        cmd.arg(format!("+{}", tc));
   452→    } else if requires_nightly(spec, mode) {
   453→        cmd.arg("+nightly");
   454→    }
   455→
   456→    Ok(cmd)
   457→}
   458→
   459→/// Apply spec parameters to a cargo command.
   460→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   461→pub fn apply_spec_to_command(
   462→    cmd: &mut Command,
   463→    spec: &Spec,
   464→    workspace: &Path,
   465→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   466→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   467→) -> Result<()> {
   468→    // Set custom target directory if specified
   469→    if let Some(td) = expanded_target_dir {
   470→        cmd.arg("--target-dir")
   471→            .arg(workspace.join("target").join(td));
   472→    }
   473→
   474→    // Handle high-level panic mode (cargo -Z flag)
   475→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   476→        && let Some(z_flag) = panic_mode.cargo_z_flag()
   477→    {
   478→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(z_flag);
   479→    }
   480→
   481→    // Handle cargo config
   482→    let has_profile = spec.cargo.profile.is_some();
   483→    if let Some(ref profile) = spec.cargo.profile {
   484→        match profile.as_str() {
   485→            "debug" | "dev" => {
   486→                // Debug/dev is default, no flag needed
   487→            }
   488→            _ => {
   489→                cmd.arg("--profile").arg(profile);
   490→            }
   491→        }
   492→    }
   493→
   494→    if let Some(ref triple) = spec.cargo.target_triple {
   495→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(triple);
   496→    }
   497→
   498→    if let Some(ref path) = spec.cargo.target_json {
   499→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg("json-target-spec");
   500→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(path);
   501→    }
   502→
   503→    for flag in &spec.cargo.unstable {
   504→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(flag);
   505→    }
   506→
   507→    // Inject --config key=value pairs (flattened from potentially nested config)
   508→    for (key, value) in flatten_config(&spec.cargo.config) {
   509→        cmd.arg("--config").arg(format!("{}={}", key, value));
   510→    }
   511→
   512→    // If no profile in spec, fall back to CLI profile
   513→    if !has_profile && let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   514→        match p {
   515→            "debug" | "dev" => {} // default, no flag needed
   516→            _ => {
   517→                cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   518→            }
   519→        }
   520→    }
   521→
   522→    // Collect rustc flags
   523→    let mut rustc_flags: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   524→
   525→    // Handle high-level panic mode (rustc -C flag)
   526→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   527→        && let Some(panic_value) = panic_mode.rustc_panic_value()
   528→    {
   529→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", panic_value));
   530→    }
   531→
   532→    // Handle high-level strip mode
   533→    if let Some(strip_mode) = spec.strip
   534→        && let Some(strip_value) = strip_mode.rustc_strip_value()
   535→    {
   536→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C strip={}", strip_value));
   537→    }
   538→
   539→    if !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty() {
   540→        // -Z build-std is a cargo flag, not rustc
   541→        let crates_str = spec.cargo.build_std.join(",");
   542→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(format!("build-std={}", crates_str));
   543→    }
   544→
   545→    for flag in &spec.rustflags {
   546→        rustc_flags.push(flag.clone());
   547→    }
   548→
   549→    // Handle version script (generates file and adds linker arg)
   550→    if let Some(vs) = &spec.linker.version_script {
   551→        let vs_dir = match expanded_target_dir {
   552→            Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   553→            None => workspace.join("target"),
   554→        };
   555→        let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&vs_dir);
   556→        let version_script_path = vs_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
   557→
   558→        // Generate version script: { global: sym1; sym2; local: *; };
   559→        let globals = vs.global.join("; ");
   560→        let content = format!("{{ global: {}; local: {}; }};", globals, vs.local);
   561→
   562→        let mut f =
   563→            fs::File::create(&version_script_path).context("failed to create version script")?;
   564→        writeln!(f, "{}", content)?;
   565→
   566→        rustc_flags.push(format!(
   567→            "-C link-arg=-Wl,--version-script={}",
   568→            version_script_path.display()
   569→        ));
   570→    }
   571→
   572→    // Apply rustc flags (linker args from Args handled by generated build.rs)
   573→    if !rustc_flags.is_empty() {
   574→        cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", rustc_flags.join(" "));
   575→    }
   576→
   577→    Ok(())
   578→}

    23→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
    24→use std::ffi::OsString;
    25→use std::path::Path;
    26→use std::process::ExitCode;
    27→
    28→use crate::find_paths::{find_package_dir, find_project_root, get_package_name, is_pop};
    29→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    30→
    31→/// Trait for command execution.
    32→pub trait Execute {
    33→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    34→}
    35→
    36→/// Resolve a `-p` argument (path or name) to the actual cargo package name.
    37→/// Returns Some(name) for a package, None if it resolves to a workspace root
    38→/// with no `[package]` section (meaning "operate on all packages").
    39→pub(crate) fn resolve_package_arg(pkg: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    40→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    41→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg)?;
    42→    match get_package_name(&pkg_dir) {
    43→        Ok(name) => Ok(Some(name)),
    44→        Err(_) => Ok(None),
    45→    }
    46→}
    47→
    48→/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.
    49→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(
    50→    subcommand: &str,
    51→    args: &[OsString],
    52→    project_root: &Path,
    53→    flags: &CargoFlags,
    54→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    55→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    56→    cmd.arg(subcommand);
    57→    cmd.args(args);
    58→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
    59→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
    60→    let status = cmd
    61→        .status()
    62→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", subcommand))?;
    63→    if status.success() {
    64→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    65→    } else {
    66→        bail!("cargo {} failed", subcommand);
    67→    }
    68→}
    69→
    70→/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package]).
    71→/// Returns None at a workspace root so that commands default to all-packages mode.
    72→pub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {
    73→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
    74→    // At a workspace root, don't treat it as a single package
    75→    if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() && !is_pop(&cwd) {
    76→        return None;
    77→    }
    78→    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()
    79→}
    80→
```

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Test Infrastructure — Extra Test Args + Fixture Workspaces

## Context

tspec has 255 unit tests but zero integration tests that actually build real packages. The todo list's top item is "Add a permanent test workspace for integration testing." To run integration tests via tspec itself (dogfooding), we first need `tspec test` to support selecting test targets and passing args to the test harness.

tspec supports three project structures that all need test coverage:
1. **POP** (Plain Old Package) — single `Cargo.toml`, no `[workspace]`
2. **POP + embedded workspace** — root has both `[package]` and `[workspace]` (like tspec itself)
3. **POWS** (Pure Old Workspace) — workspace root with only `[workspace]`, no `[package]`

## dev1: Add `--test` and trailing args to `tspec test`

Enable: `tspec test -p tspec --test integration_test -- --ignored`

### Changes

**`src/cmd/test.rs`** — Add two fields to `TestCmd`:
```rust
/// Select a specific test target (maps to cargo test --test <NAME>)
#[arg(long)]
pub test_target: Option<String>,
/// Extra arguments passed after -- to the test binary
#[arg(last = true)]
pub test_args: Vec<String>,
```

Thread `test_target` and `test_args` through `execute()` → `test_package()` → `run_cargo()`.

**`src/cargo_build.rs`** — Add `extra_args: &[String]` parameter to `run_cargo()` and `test_package()`:
- `--test <name>` appended to cargo args before the `--` separator
- Trailing args appended after `--`
- `build_package()` passes empty `&[]` (no extra args for builds)

**`src/all.rs`** — Update `test_all()` to accept and pass `extra_args`.

**`src/cmd/build.rs`**, **`src/cmd/run.rs`** — Update `build_package()`/`run_cargo()` calls with empty extra args.

**`src/compare.rs`** — Update calls to pass empty extra args.

### Tests

Add unit tests to `cmd/test.rs`:
- `test_target_flag` — `--test integration_test` parses correctly
- `trailing_args` — `-- --ignored` captured
- `both_together` — `--test foo -- --ignored --nocapture` works

## dev2: POP fixture + integration tests

### Fixture: `tests/fixtures/pop/`

```
tests/fixtures/pop/
  Cargo.toml              # [package] name="fixture-pop", edition="2024"
  Cargo.lock              # committed for reproducibility
  src/main.rs             # fn main() { println!("hello"); }
  tspec.ts.toml           # default: profile=release, strip=symbols
  tspec-opt.ts.toml       # optimized: + opt-level=z, lto, codegen-units=1
```

### Root changes

- `Cargo.toml` `[workspace]`: add `exclude = ["tests/fixtures"]`
- `.gitignore`: add `tests/fixtures/*/target/`

### Test helper: `tests/fixture.rs`

- `copy_fixture(name: &str) -> (TempDir, PathBuf)` — recursive copy, skips `target/`
- `copy_pop() -> (TempDir, PathBuf)` — convenience

### Integration tests: `tests/integration_test.rs`

All `#[ignore]`. POP-specific tests:
1. `cargo build` succeeds on copy
2. Spec loading returns expected values
3. `tspec build .` produces `target/release/fixture-pop`
4. `tspec compare .` succeeds
5. Mutation: remove specs, verify debug fallback

Run via: `tspec test -p tspec --test integration_test -- --ignored`

## dev3: POP+WS fixture

```
tests/fixtures/pop-ws/
  Cargo.toml              # [workspace] + [package] name="fixture-pop-ws"
  Cargo.lock
  src/main.rs             # uses fixture_pop_ws_lib::add()
  pop-ws-lib/
    Cargo.toml            # [package] name="fixture-pop-ws-lib"
    src/lib.rs            # pub fn add() + #[test]
  tspec.ts.toml
  tspec-opt.ts.toml
```

Tests: workspace discovery, `tspec build -p`, `tspec test -w`, cross-package compare.

## dev4: POWS fixture

```

  Cargo.toml              # [workspace] only, NO [package]
  Cargo.lock

    src/main.rs
    tspec.ts.toml
  pows-lib/
    Cargo.toml            # [package] name="fixture-pows-lib"
    src/lib.rs
```

Tests: all-packages mode, per-package specs, `tspec build -w`.

    55→### Key Modules
    56→
    57→- `cmd/` - Command implementations (one file per command)
    58→  - `mod.rs` - Execute trait, `execute_cargo_subcommand` helper, `resolve_package_arg()` (resolves paths like "." to package names), `current_package_name()` (workspace-aware), re-exports
    59→  - `build.rs`, `clean.rs`, `clippy.rs`, `compare.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `install.rs`, `run.rs`, `test.rs`, `version.rs` - Individual commands
    60→- `cli.rs` - Clap CLI definitions
    61→- `types.rs` - Spec types (CargoConfig, LinkerConfig)
    62→- `tspec.rs` - Spec loading/saving/hashing, `copy_spec_snapshot()` for byte-for-byte backups
    63→- `cargo_build.rs` - Package build orchestration with spec application
    64→- `workspace.rs` - Workspace package discovery
    65→- `all.rs` - Batch operations (build_all, test_all, run_all)
    66→- `ts_cmd/edit.rs` - `toml_edit` helpers: field registry, `set_field()`, `unset_field()`, `add_items()`, `remove_items_by_value()`, `remove_item_by_index()`, validation
    67→- `ts_cmd/set.rs` - `ts set` command (scalar or replace entire array)
    68→- `ts_cmd/unset.rs` - `ts unset` command (remove field entirely)
    69→- `ts_cmd/add.rs` - `ts add` command (append or insert at position)
    70→- `ts_cmd/remove.rs` - `ts remove` command (by value or by index)
    71→- `tspec-build/` - Library crate for build.rs integration (reads `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE` env var)
    72→
    73→### Three Write Strategies
    74→
    75→| Operation | Strategy | Why |
    76→|---|---|---|
    77→| load, hash, build | serde (`toml`) | Need typed `Spec` struct for build logic |
    78→| set, unset, add, remove | `toml_edit` (`DocumentMut`) | Surgical edit preserving comments/formatting |
    79→| backup, restore, new -f | raw `fs::copy` | Exact byte-for-byte preservation |
    80→
    81→### Translation Spec Structure
    82→
    83→Specs are TOML files (`*.ts.toml`) with top-level fields and two sections:
    84→- Top-level: `panic`, `strip`, `rustflags`
```

```
     1→# tspec - Translation Spec Build System
     2→
     3→A spec-driven build system for Rust that wraps cargo with configurable target triples, compiler flags, and linker options. Each tspec applies to a Cargo **package** — the unit with a `Cargo.toml`. If different crates need different compilation settings, they should be in separate packages.
     4→
     5→**Status:** Active development. Works with both workspaces and single-package projects (i.e. Plain Old Packages, POPs).
     6→
     7→## Installation
     8→
     9→```bash
    10→cargo install --path .
    11→```
    12→
    13→Or run directly:
    14→```bash
    15→cargo run -- build
    16→```
    17→
    18→## Contributing
    19→
    20→**All non-trivial changes must be on a feature branch** — never commit directly to `main`. This applies even for solo development.
    21→
    22→```bash
    23→git checkout -b feat-my-feature    # Create a branch
    24→# ... make changes, commit ...
    25→git checkout main                  # Switch back
    26→git merge --no-ff feat-my-feature  # Merge with merge commit (or squash-merge)
    27→git push
    28→```
    29→
    30→Branch naming: `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`.
    31→
    32→**Protect main on GitHub:** Enable branch protection (Settings > Branches) to block force pushes and optionally require PRs.
    33→
    34→For the full feature workflow (version bumps, notes, merge steps, Claude Code conventions), see [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md#feature-workflow).
    35→
    36→## Usage
    37→
    38→```bash
    39→tspec build                                # Build current package (or all in workspace) with tspec.ts.toml if present
    40→tspec build -p myapp                       # Build specific package with tspec.ts.toml if present
    41→tspec build myapp                          # Same, using positional argument
    42→tspec build .                              # Build the package in current directory
    43→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt          # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    44→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt.ts.toml  # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    45→tspec build -p myapp -r -s                 # Build release, strip symbols with tspec.ts.toml if present
    46→tspec run -p myapp                         # Build and run with tspec.ts.toml if present
    47→tspec test -p myapp                        # Build and test with tspec.ts.toml if present
    48→tspec build -w                             # Build all packages (even from inside a package dir)
    49→tspec build -t 'tspec*'                    # Build all packages using matching specs (quote the glob!)
    50→tspec build -t 'tspec-opt*'               # Build all packages with tspec-opt specs only
    51→tspec compare -p myapp                     # Compare binary sizes for a package
    52→tspec compare -p myapp -t *.ts.toml        # Compare using shell-expanded glob
    53→tspec compare                              # Compare all packages (workspace mode)
    54→tspec compare -w                           # Force workspace mode from inside a package
    55→tspec compare -w -f                        # Workspace mode, stop on first failure
    56→tspec -v build .                           # Verbose: show cargo command/env + cargo -v
    57→tspec -vv build .                          # Debug: also show spec details + cargo -vv
    58→tspec -j4 build .                          # Limit cargo to 4 parallel jobs
    59→```
    60→
    61→The `-p` flag or positional argument specifies a package by name or path (defaults to current directory if in a package, otherwise all packages). Paths like `.` are resolved to the actual cargo package name. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` means "all packages."
    62→Use `-w, --workspace` to force all-packages mode even when inside a package directory.
    63→The `-t` flag selects a tspec file; if omitted and `tspec.ts.toml` exists, it's used automatically. In workspace mode (no `-p`, or with `-w`), `-t` patterns are resolved per-package — each sub-package is searched for matching specs, and packages with no matches are skipped.
    64→
    65→**Global flags** apply to all commands and are passed through to cargo: `-v` (cargo `-v`, shows rustc invocations), `-vv` (cargo `-vv`, full command details), and `-j N` (parallel jobs). tspec's own verbose output (command line, env vars, spec resolution) is also controlled by `-v`/`-vv`.
    66→
    67→**Important: quote glob patterns with `-t`.** The shell expands unquoted globs *before* tspec sees them. At a workspace root, `tspec build -t tspec*` may expand to literal filenames from the root directory (or worse, match `target/`), rather than being matched per-package. Always quote: `-t 'tspec*'`. tspec will warn if it detects shell-expanded non-tspec arguments.
    68→
    69→**Note on `cmd` vs `cmd .`:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), while `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata files. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    70→
    71→## tspec Files
    72→
    73→Translation specs are TOML files (conventionally `*.ts.toml`) that configure builds:
    74→
    75→```toml
    76→# tspec.ts.toml - all supported fields shown
    77→
    78→# Top-level high-level options (expand to lower-level cargo/rustc flags)
    79→panic = "abort"                            # "unwind" (default), "abort", "immediate-abort" (nightly)
    80→strip = "symbols"                          # "none" (default), "debuginfo", "symbols"
    81→toolchain = "nightly"                      # override toolchain (e.g., "nightly", "stable", "1.75")
    82→rustflags = ["-C", "relocation-model=static"]  # raw flags passed through to RUSTFLAGS
    83→
    84→[cargo]
    85→profile = "release"                        # "debug" (default), "release", or custom profile
    86→target_triple = "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl"
    87→target_json = "path/to/custom-target.json" # custom target spec (auto-adds -Z json-target-spec)
    88→unstable = ["panic-immediate-abort"]       # -Z flags (nightly only)
    89→target_dir = "{name}"                      # per-spec target dir; supports {name} and {hash} placeholders
    90→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]              # crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly)
    91→
    92→[cargo.config.profile.release]             # nested tables under [cargo.config] become individual
    93→opt-level = "z"                            # --config KEY=VALUE args to cargo; any valid cargo
    94→lto = true                                 # config key works (profile, build, target, etc.)
    95→codegen-units = 1
    96→
    97→[linker]
    98→args = ["-static", "-nostartfiles"]
    99→
   100→[linker.version_script]                    # symbol visibility control (enables --gc-sections)
   101→global = ["_start"]                        # symbols to keep
   102→local = "*"                                # default: "*" (hide everything else)
   103→```
   104→
   105→The top-level `panic` sets both cargo `-Z` and rustc `-C` flags automatically.
   106→
   107→### Key Concepts
   108→
   109→- **One tspec per package** - A tspec applies to all crates (targets) within a Cargo package. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
   110→- **tspec.ts.toml is optional** - Packages without one get plain `cargo build/test`
   111→- **Generated build.rs** - tspec generates temporary build.rs for scoped linker flags
   112→- **Spec comparison** - Compare binary sizes across different specs; always shows both `cargo --release` and `cargo --release-strip` baselines. Supports `-w` for all-packages mode — per-package tables are collected and printed together at the end. With a single package, only its comparison table is shown
   113→
   114→### How Linker Flags Work
   115→
   116→tspec generates a temporary `build.rs` to apply linker flags with binary-scoped directives:
   117→
   118→```rust
   119→// Generated by tspec - deleted after build
   120→fn main() {
   121→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-static");
   122→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-nostdlib");
   123→}
   124→```
   125→
   126→The only other alternative is to add these linker flags to RUSTFLAGS, but that
   127→affects ALL compilations (including dependency build scripts), causing crashes
   128→with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated build.rs scopes flags to just
   129→the binary.
   130→
   131→### Cargo Config Passthrough (`cargo.config`)
   132→
   133→The `[cargo.config]` section is a general-purpose passthrough to cargo's `--config KEY=VALUE` mechanism. Any nested tables under `[cargo.config]` are flattened into dotted keys and passed as `--config` args. This lets you override any [cargo configuration](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html) value at the command line.
   134→
   135→**Profile settings** are the most common use case:
   136→
   137→```toml
   138→[cargo.config.profile.release]
   139→opt-level = "z"
   140→codegen-units = 1
   141→lto = true
   142→```
   143→
   144→This produces: `cargo --config profile.release.opt-level="z" --config profile.release.codegen-units=1 --config profile.release.lto=true`
   145→
   146→**Other cargo config keys** work too:
   147→
   148→```toml
   149→[cargo.config.target.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu]
   150→linker = "clang"
   151→runner = "qemu-x86_64"
   152→```
   153→
   154→**Note:** Do not use `build.rustflags` in `[cargo.config]`. tspec sets the `RUSTFLAGS` env var (from top-level `rustflags`, `panic`, and `strip`), which takes precedence over `--config build.rustflags` per cargo's precedence rules. Use the top-level `rustflags` array instead.
   155→
   156→**Custom profiles:** Any profile defined in your workspace `Cargo.toml` (with `inherits`) can be used. tspec validates that custom profiles exist before invoking cargo. Use `cargo.profile` in the spec or `--profile` on the CLI.
   157→
   158→**Flat dotted keys** also work — TOML treats them equivalently:
   159→
   160→```toml
   161→[cargo.config]
   162→"profile.release.opt-level" = "z"
   163→```
   164→
   165→## tspec Management (ts subcommand)
   166→
   167→Manage spec files without manual TOML editing. All commands that modify files preserve comments and formatting.
   168→
   169→| Command | Description |
   170→|---------|-------------|
   171→| `ts list` | List tspec files found in the workspace or a specific package |
   172→| `ts show` | Display the TOML contents of a tspec file |
   173→| `ts hash` | Print the content hash (used in backup filenames) of a tspec |
   174→| `ts new` | Create a new tspec with defaults, or copy an existing one with `-f` (byte-for-byte) |
   175→| `ts set` | Set a scalar value or replace an entire array |
   176→| `ts unset` | Remove a field (scalar or array) from a tspec entirely |
   177→| `ts add` | Add items to an array field (append or insert at position) |
   178→| `ts remove` | Remove items from an array field (by value or by index) |
   179→| `ts backup` | Create a versioned backup copy (`name-NNN-hash.ts.toml`) |
   180→| `ts restore` | Restore a tspec by copying a versioned backup back to its original name |
   181→
   182→```bash
   183→# List / show / hash
   184→tspec ts list                            # List all tspec files
   185→tspec ts list -p myapp                   # List tspec files for a package
   186→tspec ts show -p myapp                   # Show all tspec contents
   187→tspec ts show -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show specific tspec-opt.ts.toml
   188→tspec ts hash -p myapp                   # Show content hash of tspec.ts.toml
   189→
   190→# Create new tspec files
   191→tspec ts new -p myapp                    # Create tspec.ts.toml with defaults
   192→tspec ts new experiment -p myapp         # Create experiment.ts.toml
   193→tspec ts new opt2 -p myapp -f tspec-opt  # Copy from existing spec (byte-for-byte)
   194→
   195→# Set scalar values
   196→tspec ts set strip symbols -p myapp              # Set a scalar field
   197→tspec ts set cargo.profile release               # No quoting needed
   198→
   199→# Set (replace) entire arrays — each element is a separate arg
   200→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib        # Replace array
   201→tspec ts set cargo.build_std core alloc           # Replace array
   202→tspec ts set rustflags -C relocation-model=static # Replace array
   203→
   204→# Add items to arrays (append by default)
   205→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections        # Append one item
   206→tspec ts add linker.args -nostdlib -pie            # Append multiple items
   207→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles        # Insert at position 0
   208→
   209→# Remove items from arrays
   210→tspec ts remove linker.args -nostdlib              # Remove by value
   211→tspec ts remove linker.args -static -pie           # Remove multiple by value
   212→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args                   # Remove by index
   213→
   214→# Remove a field entirely
   215→tspec ts unset strip -p myapp                      # Remove scalar field
   216→tspec ts unset linker.args -t tspec-opt            # Remove array field
   217→
   218→# Backup and restore (byte-for-byte copies)
   219→tspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Backup tspec.ts.toml → tspec-001-abcd1234.ts.toml
   220→tspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup tspec-opt.ts.toml
   221→tspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup
   222→```
   223→
   224→Backups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.
   225→
   226→`ts set`, `ts unset`, `ts add`, and `ts remove` use `toml_edit` for surgical editing — comments and formatting are preserved.
   227→`ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` use byte-for-byte file copy — comments are preserved exactly.
   228→
   229→### Supported keys
   230→
   231→Keys use two forms: `FIELD` for top-level fields (`panic`, `strip`, `rustflags`) or `TABLE.FIELD` for nested fields (`cargo.profile`, `linker.args`).
   232→
   233→| Key | Type | Values |
   234→|-----|------|--------|
   235→| `panic` | scalar | `unwind`, `abort`, `immediate-abort` |
   236→| `strip` | scalar | `none`, `debuginfo`, `symbols` |
   237→| `toolchain` | scalar | e.g. `nightly`, `stable`, `nightly-2025-01-15`, `1.75` |
   238→| `rustflags` | array | e.g. `-C relocation-model=static` |
   239→| `cargo.profile` | scalar | `debug`, `release`, or any custom profile defined in `Cargo.toml` |
   240→| `cargo.target_triple` | scalar | any string |
   241→| `cargo.target_json` | scalar | any path |
   242→| `cargo.target_dir` | scalar | any string (supports `{name}`, `{hash}`) |
   243→| `cargo.unstable` | array | e.g. `panic-immediate-abort` |
   244→| `cargo.config` | table | arbitrary `--config KEY=VALUE` passthrough; supports nested tables (e.g. `[cargo.config.profile.release]`) |
   245→| `cargo.build_std` | array | e.g. `core alloc` |
   246→| `linker.args` | array | e.g. `-static -nostdlib` |
   247→
   248→### Array operations
   249→
   250→Four orthogonal commands operate on arrays at two levels:
   251→
   252→| Level | Command | Purpose |
   253→|-------|---------|---------|
   254→| Field-level | `ts set` | Replace entire array with new values |
   255→| Field-level | `ts unset` | Remove the array field entirely |
   256→| Item-level | `ts add` | Append items (or insert at position with `-i`) |
   257→| Item-level | `ts remove` | Remove items by value (or by index with `-i`) |
   258→
   259→```bash
   260→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib   # replace all with these
   261→tspec ts unset linker.args                   # clear the field
   262→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections   # append (skips duplicates)
   263→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles  # insert at position
   264→tspec ts remove linker.args -static          # remove by value
   265→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args             # remove by index
   266→```
   267→
   268→Values starting with `-` work without quoting thanks to `allow_hyphen_values`.
   269→Use `--` as an escape hatch if a value collides with a flag like `-p`, `-t`, or `-i`.
   270→
   271→Note: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for "tspec management").
   272→
   273→## Testing
   274→
   275→```bash
   276→tspec test -p tspec           # Run tspec tests
   277→tspec test -p tspec-build     # Run tspec-build tests
   278→```
   279→
   280→## Project Structure
   281→
   282→```
   283→tspec/                  # Workspace root (also the main tspec package)
   284→  Cargo.toml            # [workspace] + [package] — workspace with root package
   285→  src/
   286→    lib.rs              # Library root, exposes modules
   287→    main.rs             # Entry point, dispatch
   288→    cli.rs              # Clap CLI definitions
   289→    types.rs            # Spec parameter types
   290→    tspec.rs            # Spec loading/saving/hashing
   291→    find_paths.rs       # Project/package/tspec/binary path discovery
   292→    workspace.rs        # Workspace package discovery
   293→    cargo_build.rs      # Unified cargo runner (build + test) + generated build.rs
   294→    run.rs              # Run command implementation
   295→    compare.rs          # Compare command (size comparison)
   296→    all.rs              # Batch operations (build_all, run_all, test_all, compare_all)
   297→    cmd/                # Command implementations (one file per command)
   298→    ts_cmd/             # Tspec management subcommands
   299→    binary.rs           # Binary operations (strip, size)
   300→  tests/
   301→    data/               # Test fixtures (TOML specs)
   302→    tspec_test.rs       # Integration tests
   303→  tspec-build/          # Library crate for build.rs integration
   304→    src/lib.rs          # Reads TSPEC_SPEC_FILE env var at build time
   305→  notes/                # Design docs and todo tracking
   306→```
   307→
   308→## Development
   309→
   310→See [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md) for current tasks.
   311→
   312→### Verification
   313→
   314→```bash
   315→tspec test -p tspec
   316→tspec test -p tspec-build
   317→tspec clippy
   318→tspec fmt --check
   319→```
   320→
   321→## Origin
   322→
   323→Originally developed as `xt` within the [rlibc-x](https://github.com/user/rlibc-x) project, then extracted to a standalone tool. The git history was preserved using `git subtree split`.

    86→- `[linker]` - args, version_script
    87→
    88→## Conventions
    89→
    90→- **Rust Edition:** 2024
    91→- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:). Append `, vX.Y.Z` to the subject when the commit sets a release version — e.g. `feat: Add glob support for -t, v0.11.3`. No "bump" — the version number speaks for itself.
    92→- **Naming:** POP (Plain Old Package) refers to single-package projects (no workspace); tspec treats them as trivial workspaces
    93→- **Granularity:** tspec operates at the Cargo package level, not the crate level. "Package" = directory with Cargo.toml. A package may contain multiple crates (targets), but they all share one tspec.
    94→- **Package argument pattern:** All commands accept both `-p <name-or-path>` and a positional `<PACKAGE>` argument. Paths (like `.`) are resolved to the actual cargo package name via `resolve_package_arg()`. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` resolves to None (all-packages mode). Resolution order: positional > `-p` > current directory > all packages.
    95→- **`cmd` vs `cmd .` for passthroughs:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), but `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    96→- **Markdown refs:** Multiple references use `[1],[2]` not `[1,2]` or `[1][2]` (both break in markdown)
    97→
    98→## Feature Workflow
    99→
   100→**All non-trivial changes must be on a feature branch — never commit directly to main.** Even "quick" changes tend to grow; start on a branch and it's always safe to amend, rebase, or force-push without risk to main.
   101→
   102→**Before starting feature or fix work:**
   103→1. Create a branch: `git checkout -b <type>-<short-description>` (e.g., `fix-compare-optional-p`)
   104→2. Create a dated entry in `notes/chores-N.md` with context and plan
   105→3. Update `notes/todo.md` to move items to In Progress
   106→4. Bump version in `Cargo.toml` (always required — use `X.Y.Z-dev` for multi-step, direct bump for single-step)
   107→5. For plans: recommend single-step (one commit) vs multi-step (`-devN` series) and get user approval
   108→6. Commit the above as a chore marker commit before starting code changes
   109→7. Use todo list to track progress during implementation
   110→
   111→**On completion:**
   112→1. Update `notes/chores-N.md` with result
   113→2. Move items from In Progress to Done in `notes/todo.md`
   114→3. Remove `-dev` from version in `Cargo.toml`
   115→4. Commit as a release chore (include `Cargo.lock` if changed)
```
