     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace packages
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→
     5→use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
     6→use std::process::ExitCode;
     7→
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
    11→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, test_package};
    12→use crate::compare::{SpecResult, compare_specs, print_comparison};
    13→use crate::find_paths::find_tspecs;
    14→use crate::run::run_binary;
    15→use crate::tspec::spec_name_from_path;
    16→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    17→use crate::workspace::{PackageKind, WorkspaceInfo};
    18→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
    19→
    20→/// Normalize tspec patterns for per-package matching in all-packages mode.
    21→///
    22→/// Strips directory components so shell-expanded paths from the workspace root
    23→/// don't leak into sub-packages. Filters out non-tspec shell expansions
    24→/// (e.g. `target/`, `tools/`) — only `.ts.toml` files and glob patterns are kept.
    25→///
    26→/// Returns `None` if all patterns were filtered out (likely shell expansion of a
    27→/// glob that matched non-tspec entries). The caller should warn about quoting.
    28→fn normalize_tspec_patterns(patterns: &[String]) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    29→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    30→        return Some(Vec::new());
    31→    }
    32→    let filenames: Vec<String> = patterns
    33→        .iter()
    34→        .filter_map(|p| {
    35→            let name = Path::new(p)
    36→                .file_name()
    37→                .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
    38→                .unwrap_or_else(|| p.clone());
    39→            // Keep glob patterns (unexpanded) and actual tspec files
    40→            let is_glob = name.contains('*') || name.contains('?') || name.contains('[');
    41→            let is_tspec = name.ends_with(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    42→            if is_glob || is_tspec {
    43→                Some(name)
    44→            } else {
    45→                None
    46→            }
    47→        })
    48→        .collect();
    49→    if filenames.is_empty() && !patterns.is_empty() {
    50→        None // all patterns were filtered — likely shell expansion
    51→    } else {
    52→        Some(filenames)
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Warn that shell glob expansion likely ate the tspec pattern.
    57→pub fn warn_shell_glob_expansion(patterns: &[String]) {
    58→    eprintln!(
    59→        "Warning: -t arguments ({}) don't look like tspec files.",
    60→        patterns.join(", ")
    61→    );
    62→    eprintln!("  The shell likely expanded your glob before tspec could see it.");
    63→    eprintln!("  Quote the pattern to prevent shell expansion: -t 'pattern*'");
    64→}
    65→
    66→/// Resolve tspec patterns for a workspace member.
    67→///
    68→/// Returns the matching spec paths, or an empty vec if no patterns match.
    69→/// When `patterns` is empty, returns an empty vec (caller should use default behavior).
    70→fn resolve_specs_for_member(member_path: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    71→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    72→        return Vec::new();
    73→    }
    74→    find_tspecs(member_path, patterns).unwrap_or_default()
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
    78→fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    79→    match tspec {
    80→        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
    81→        None => String::new(),
    82→    }
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
    86→pub struct OpResult {
    87→    pub name: String,
    88→    pub spec: String,
    89→    pub success: bool,
    90→    pub message: String,
    91→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    92→}
    93→
    94→/// Build all workspace packages (excluding build tools)
    95→///
    96→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
    97→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
    98→pub fn build_all(
    99→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   100→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   101→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   102→    strip: bool,
   103→    fail_fast: bool,
   104→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   105→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   106→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   107→        Some(n) => n,
   108→        None => {
   109→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   110→            return Vec::new();
   111→        }
   112→    };
   113→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   114→
   115→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   116→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   117→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   118→            continue;
   119→        }
   120→
   121→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   122→
   123→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   124→            vec![None]
   125→        } else {
   126→            specs
   127→                .into_iter()
   128→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   129→                .collect()
   130→        };
   131→
   132→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   133→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   134→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   135→                Ok(build_result) => {
   136→                    if strip
   137→                        && member.has_binary
   138→                        && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
   139→                    {
   140→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   141→                    }
   142→                    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
   143→                    OpResult {
   144→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   145→                        spec,
   146→                        success: true,
   147→                        message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
   148→                        size,
   149→                    }
   150→                }
   151→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   152→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   153→                    spec,
   154→                    success: false,
   155→                    message: e.to_string(),
   156→                    size: None,
   157→                },
   158→            };
   159→
   160→            let failed = !result.success;
   161→            results.push(result);
   162→
   163→            if failed && fail_fast {
   164→                return results;
   165→            }
   166→        }
   167→    }
   168→
   169→    results
   170→}
   171→
   172→/// Run all app packages sequentially
   173→///
   174→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   175→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   176→pub fn run_all(
   177→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   178→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   179→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   180→    strip: bool,
   181→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   182→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   183→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   184→        Some(n) => n,
   185→        None => {
   186→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   187→            return Vec::new();
   188→        }
   189→    };
   190→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   191→
   192→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
   193→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   194→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   195→            continue;
   196→        }
   197→
   198→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   199→
   200→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   201→            vec![None]
   202→        } else {
   203→            specs
   204→                .into_iter()
   205→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   206→                .collect()
   207→        };
   208→
   209→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   210→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   211→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   212→                Ok(build_result) => {
   213→                    if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
   214→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   215→                    }
   216→                    match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
   217→                        Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
   218→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   219→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   220→                            success: true,
   221→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   222→                            size: None,
   223→                        },
   224→                        Err(e) => OpResult {
   225→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   226→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   227→                            success: false,
   228→                            message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   229→                            size: None,
   230→                        },
   231→                    }
   232→                }
   233→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   234→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   235→                    spec,
   236→                    success: false,
   237→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   238→                    size: None,
   239→                },
   240→            };
   241→
   242→            results.push(result);
   243→        }
   244→    }
   245→
   246→    results
   247→}
   248→
   249→/// Test all workspace packages
   250→///
   251→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   252→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   253→pub fn test_all(
   254→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   255→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   256→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   257→    fail_fast: bool,
   258→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   259→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   260→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   261→        Some(n) => n,
   262→        None => {
   263→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   264→            return Vec::new();
   265→        }
   266→    };
   267→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   268→
   269→    // Test regular packages (excluding Test kind which needs special handling)
   270→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   271→        if member.kind == PackageKind::Test {
   272→            continue; // Handle test packages separately
   273→        }
   274→
   275→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   276→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   277→            continue;
   278→        }
   279→
   280→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   281→
   282→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   283→            vec![None]
   284→        } else {
   285→            specs
   286→                .into_iter()
   287→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   288→                .collect()
   289→        };
   290→
   291→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   292→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   293→            let result = match test_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   294→                Ok(()) => OpResult {
   295→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   296→                    spec,
   297→                    success: true,
   298→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   299→                    size: None,
   300→                },
   301→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   302→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   303→                    spec,
   304→                    success: false,
   305→                    message: e.to_string(),
   306→                    size: None,
   307→                },
   308→            };
   309→
   310→            let failed = !result.success;
   311→            results.push(result);
   312→
   313→            if failed && fail_fast {
   314→                return results;
   315→            }
   316→        }
   317→    }
   318→
   319→    // Handle test packages (like rlibc-x2-tests) - build and run all test binaries
   320→    for member in workspace.test_members() {
   321→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   322→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   323→            continue;
   324→        }
   325→
   326→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   327→
   328→        // Build the test package (builds all binaries)
   329→        let build_tspec = specs.first().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
   330→        let spec = spec_label(&build_tspec);
   331→        let build_result =
   332→            match build_package(&member.name, build_tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   333→                Ok(r) => r,
   334→                Err(e) => {
   335→                    results.push(OpResult {
   336→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   337→                        spec,
   338→                        success: false,
   339→                        message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   340→                        size: None,
   341→                    });
   342→                    if fail_fast {
   343→                        return results;
   344→                    }
   345→                    continue;
   346→                }
   347→            };
   348→
   349→        // Find and run all test binaries in the target directory
   350→        let profile_dir = crate::types::profile_dir_name(cli_profile.unwrap_or("debug"));
   351→        let target_dir = build_result.target_base.join(profile_dir);
   352→
   353→        // Look for binaries that end with "-tests" in the target directory
   354→        let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
   355→            .into_iter()
   356→            .flatten()
   357→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   358→            .filter(|e| {
   359→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   360→                let is_file = e.path().is_file();
   361→                let is_test = name.ends_with("-tests");
   362→                let is_executable = e
   363→                    .metadata()
   364→                    .map(|m| m.permissions().mode() & 0o111 != 0)
   365→                    .unwrap_or(false);
   366→                is_file && is_test && is_executable
   367→            })
   368→            .collect();
   369→
   370→        if test_binaries.is_empty() {
   371→            results.push(OpResult {
   372→                name: member.name.clone(),
   373→                spec: spec.clone(),
   374→                success: false,
   375→                message: "no test binaries found".to_string(),
   376→                size: None,
   377→            });
   378→            continue;
   379→        }
   380→
   381→        for entry in test_binaries {
   382→            let path = entry.path();
   383→            let bin_name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   384→
   385→            print!("  Running {}... ", bin_name);
   386→
   387→            let result = match run_binary(&path, &[]) {
   388→                Ok(exit_code) => {
   389→                    if exit_code == 0 {
   390→                        println!("ok");
   391→                        OpResult {
   392→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   393→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   394→                            success: true,
   395→                            message: "ok".to_string(),
   396→                            size: None,
   397→                        }
   398→                    } else {
   399→                        println!("FAILED (exit {})", exit_code);
   400→                        OpResult {
   401→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   402→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   403→                            success: false,
   404→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   405→                            size: None,
   406→                        }
   407→                    }
   408→                }
   409→                Err(e) => {
   410→                    println!("FAILED");
   411→                    OpResult {
   412→                        name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   413→                        spec: spec.clone(),
   414→                        success: false,
   415→                        message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   416→                        size: None,
   417→                    }
   418→                }
   419→            };
   420→
   421→            let failed = !result.success;
   422→            results.push(result);
   423→
   424→            if failed && fail_fast {
   425→                return results;
   426→            }
   427→        }
   428→    }
   429→
   430→    results
   431→}
   432→
   433→/// A row for the summary table: package name, spec, and pre-formatted detail string.
   434→struct SummaryRow {
   435→    name: String,
   436→    spec: String,
   437→    detail: String,
   438→}
   439→
   440→/// Print a summary table with consistent formatting across all operations.
   441→///
   442→/// - `ws_name` — workspace/package name for the header
   443→/// - `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   444→/// - `detail_header` — column header for the detail column (e.g. "Status", "Status    Size", "Exit")
   445→/// - `rows` — pre-formatted rows
   446→/// - `footer` — footer line (e.g. "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed")
   447→fn print_summary_table(
   448→    ws_name: &str,
   449→    cmd: &str,
   450→    detail_header: &str,
   451→    rows: &[SummaryRow],
   452→    footer: &str,
   453→) {
   454→    let max_name_len = rows.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(7).max(7);
   455→    let has_spec = rows.iter().any(|r| !r.spec.is_empty());
   456→    let max_spec_len = if has_spec {
   457→        rows.iter().map(|r| r.spec.len()).max().unwrap_or(4).max(4)
   458→    } else {
   459→        0
   460→    };
   461→
   462→    println!();
   463→    print_header!(format!("{ws_name} {cmd} SUMMARY"));
   464→    if has_spec {
   465→        println!(
   466→            "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {detail_header}",
   467→            "Package",
   468→            "Spec",
   469→            nw = max_name_len,
   470→            sw = max_spec_len
   471→        );
   472→    } else {
   473→        println!(
   474→            "  {:width$}  {detail_header}",
   475→            "Package",
   476→            width = max_name_len
   477→        );
   478→    }
   479→
   480→    for row in rows {
   481→        if has_spec {
   482→            println!(
   483→                "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {}",
   484→                row.name,
   485→                row.spec,
   486→                row.detail,
   487→                nw = max_name_len,
   488→                sw = max_spec_len
   489→            );
   490→        } else {
   491→            println!(
   492→                "  {:width$}  {}",
   493→                row.name,
   494→                row.detail,
   495→                width = max_name_len
   496→            );
   497→        }
   498→    }
   499→
   500→    println!();
   501→    if !footer.is_empty() {
   502→        println!("  {footer}");
   503→    }
   504→    print_hline!();
   505→    println!();
   506→}
   507→
   508→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   509→pub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   510→    let mut passed = 0;
   511→    let mut failed = 0;
   512→
   513→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   514→        .iter()
   515→        .map(|r| {
   516→            let detail = if r.success {
   517→                passed += 1;
   518→                "[PASS]".to_string()
   519→            } else {
   520→                failed += 1;
   521→                "[FAIL]".to_string()
   522→            };
   523→            SummaryRow {
   524→                name: r.name.clone(),
   525→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   526→                detail,
   527→            }
   528→        })
   529→        .collect();
   530→
   531→    print_summary_table(
   532→        name,
   533→        "TEST",
   534→        "Status",
   535→        &rows,
   536→        &format!("Test: {passed} passed, {failed} failed"),
   537→    );
   538→
   539→    if failed > 0 {
   540→        ExitCode::from(1)
   541→    } else {
   542→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   543→    }
   544→}
   545→
   546→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   547→pub fn print_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   548→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   549→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   550→
   551→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   552→        .iter()
   553→        .map(|r| {
   554→            let status = if r.success {
   555→                ok_count += 1;
   556→                "[ OK ]"
   557→            } else {
   558→                failed_count += 1;
   559→                "[FAIL]"
   560→            };
   561→            let size_str = r.size.map(format_size).unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   562→            SummaryRow {
   563→                name: r.name.clone(),
   564→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   565→                detail: format!("{status}  {size_str:>6}"),
   566→            }
   567→        })
   568→        .collect();
   569→
   570→    print_summary_table(
   571→        name,
   572→        "BUILD",
   573→        "Status    Size",
   574→        &rows,
   575→        &format!("Build: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   576→    );
   577→
   578→    if failed_count > 0 {
   579→        ExitCode::from(1)
   580→    } else {
   581→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   582→    }
   583→}
   584→
   585→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   586→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   587→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   588→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   589→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   590→    } else {
   591→        format!("{}", bytes)
   592→    }
   593→}
   594→
   595→/// Result of a compare operation on a single package
   596→pub struct CompareResult {
   597→    pub op: OpResult,
   598→    pub specs: Vec<SpecResult>,
   599→}
   600→
   601→/// Compare all workspace packages that have binaries
   602→///
   603→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package discovers its own specs via default glob.
   604→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   605→pub fn compare_all(
   606→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   607→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   608→    fail_fast: bool,
   609→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   610→) -> Vec<CompareResult> {
   611→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   612→        Some(n) => n,
   613→        None => {
   614→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   615→            return Vec::new();
   616→        }
   617→    };
   618→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   619→
   620→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   621→        if !member.has_binary {
   622→            continue;
   623→        }
   624→
   625→        let spec_paths = if normalized.is_empty() {
   626→            find_tspecs(&member.path, &[]).unwrap_or_default()
   627→        } else {
   628→            let resolved = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   629→            if resolved.is_empty() {
   630→                continue;
   631→            }
   632→            resolved
   633→        };
   634→
   635→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   636→
   637→        let (op, specs) = match compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths, flags) {
   638→            Ok(spec_results) => (
   639→                OpResult {
   640→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   641→                    spec: String::new(),
   642→                    success: true,
   643→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   644→                    size: None,
   645→                },
   646→                spec_results,
   647→            ),
   648→            Err(e) => (
   649→                OpResult {
   650→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   651→                    spec: String::new(),
   652→                    success: false,
   653→                    message: e.to_string(),
   654→                    size: None,
   655→                },
   656→                Vec::new(),
   657→            ),
   658→        };
   659→
   660→        let failed = !op.success;
   661→        results.push(CompareResult { op, specs });
   662→
   663→        if failed && fail_fast {
   664→            break;
   665→        }
   666→    }
   667→
   668→    results
   669→}
   670→
   671→/// Print a summary for compare operations
   672→///
   673→/// With a single package, just prints its comparison table.
   674→/// With multiple packages, reprints all per-package tables then an overall OK/FAIL summary.
   675→pub fn print_compare_summary(name: &str, results: &[CompareResult]) -> ExitCode {
   676→    let has_failure = results.iter().any(|r| !r.op.success);
   677→
   678→    // Reprint per-package comparison tables together
   679→    for result in results {
   680→        if !result.specs.is_empty() {
   681→            print_comparison(&result.op.name, &result.specs);
   682→        }
   683→    }
   684→
   685→    // Only show overall COMPARE SUMMARY when there are multiple packages
   686→    if results.len() > 1 {
   687→        let mut ok_count = 0;
   688→        let mut failed_count = 0;
   689→
   690→        let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   691→            .iter()
   692→            .map(|r| {
   693→                let detail = if r.op.success {
   694→                    ok_count += 1;
   695→                    "[ OK ]".to_string()
   696→                } else {
   697→                    failed_count += 1;
   698→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   699→                };
   700→                SummaryRow {
   701→                    name: r.op.name.clone(),
   702→                    spec: r.op.spec.clone(),
   703→                    detail,
   704→                }
   705→            })
   706→            .collect();
   707→
   708→        print_summary_table(
   709→            name,
   710→            "COMPARE",
   711→            "Status",
   712→            &rows,
   713→            &format!("Compare: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   714→        );
   715→    }
   716→
   717→    if has_failure {
   718→        ExitCode::from(1)
   719→    } else {
   720→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   721→    }
   722→}
   723→
   724→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   725→pub fn print_run_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   726→    let mut error_count = 0;
   727→
   728→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   729→        .iter()
   730→        .map(|r| {
   731→            let detail = if r.success {
   732→                let code = r.message.strip_prefix("exit code: ").unwrap_or(&r.message);
   733→                format!("{code:>4}")
   734→            } else {
   735→                error_count += 1;
   736→                format!("ERROR: {}", r.message)
   737→            };
   738→            SummaryRow {
   739→                name: r.name.clone(),
   740→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   741→                detail,
   742→            }
   743→        })
   744→        .collect();
   745→
   746→    let footer = if error_count > 0 {
   747→        format!("Run: {error_count} error(s)")
   748→    } else {
   749→        String::new()
   750→    };
   751→
   752→    print_summary_table(name, "RUN", "Exit", &rows, &footer);
   753→
   754→    if error_count > 0 {
   755→        ExitCode::from(1)
   756→    } else {
   757→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   758→    }
   759→}
   760→

Stopping. What's up?

> DEVELOPER

It feels like test_args could be field in cargo_args, test_package more parameters isn't better, thoughts

> AGENT

You're right. One `extra_args: &[String]` is cleaner — the caller builds the full arg list including `--` if needed. `run_cargo()` just appends them. No special knowledge of test harness args in the lower layers.

So TestCmd builds `["--test", "integration_test", "--", "--ignored"]` and passes it as a single slice. `run_cargo()` does `cmd.args(extra_args)`. Done.

`build_package()` stays unchanged (passes `&[]` internally). Only `test_package()` gets the one extra param.

Want me to proceed with that?

> DEVELOPER

So no new parameters to test_package the info would be part of CargoFlags?

> AGENT

That's cleaner. `CargoFlags` is already "stuff passed through to cargo" and it's threaded everywhere. Add `extra_args: Vec<String>` to it — TestCmd populates it, everyone else gets empty via `Default`. `apply_to_command()` appends them. Zero signature changes to `test_package()` or `run_cargo()`.

The only question: `CargoFlags` is currently constructed once in `main.rs` and passed as `&CargoFlags`. TestCmd would need to either:
- Clone and modify: `let mut flags = flags.clone(); flags.extra_args = ...;`
- Or `main.rs` builds it with the test args already included (but main.rs doesn't know about subcommand-specific args)

Clone-and-modify in TestCmd seems right — it's the only command that needs it.

Shall I proceed with that approach?

> DEVELOPER

sure, let's see how it goes

  "new_string": "/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success.\npub fn build_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    run_cargo(CargoMode::Build, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)\n}\n\n/// Test a package with a spec.\npub fn test_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<()> {\n    run_cargo(CargoMode::Test, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)?;\n    Ok(())\n}"
}

Build extra_args from --test and trailing args\n        let flags = if self.test_target.is_some() || !self.test_args.is_empty() {\n            let mut f = flags.clone();\n            if let Some(ref target) = self.test_target {\n                f.extra_args.push(\"--test\".to_string());\n                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());\n            }\n            if !self.test_args.is_empty() {\n                f.extra_args.push(\"--\".to_string());\n                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());\n            }\n            f\n        } else {\n            flags.clone()\n        };\n\n        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all\n        let resolved = if self.workspace {\n            None\n        } else {\n            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {\n                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,\n                None => current_package_name(),\n            }\n        };\n\n        match resolved {\n            None => {\n                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;\n                let results =\n                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);\n                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))\n            }\n            Some(name) => {\n                if self.tspec.is_empty() {\n                    test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;\n                } else {\n                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;\n                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;\n                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;\n                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {\n                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();\n                        test_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, &flags)?;\n                    }\n                }\n                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}"

{

  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn profile_and_release_conflict() {\n        let result = Cli::try_parse_from([\"tspec\", \"test\", \"-r\", \"--profile\", \"custom\"]);\n        assert!(result.is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_target_flag() {\n        let cmd = parse(&[\"--test\", \"integration_test\"]);\n        assert_eq!(cmd.test_target.as_deref(), Some(\"integration_test\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn trailing_args() {\n        let cmd = parse(&[\"--\", \"--ignored\"]);\n        assert_eq!(cmd.test_args, vec![\"--ignored\"]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_target_and_trailing_args() {\n        let cmd = parse(&[\"--test\", \"integration_test\", \"--\", \"--ignored\", \"--nocapture\"]);\n        assert_eq!(cmd.test_target.as_deref(), Some(\"integration_test\"));\n        assert_eq!(cmd.test_args, vec![\"--ignored\", \"--nocapture\"]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_target_with_package() {\n        let cmd = parse(&[\"-p\", \"tspec\", \"--test\", \"integration_test\", \"--\", \"--ignored\"]);\n        assert_eq!(cmd.package.as_deref(), Some(\"tspec\"));\n        assert_eq!(cmd.test_target.as_deref(), Some(\"integration_test\"));\n        assert_eq!(cmd.test_args, vec![\"--ignored\"]);\n    }\n}"
}

> AGENT

Here's the picture:

**Test selection in cargo works at two levels:**

1. **Target selection** (`--test <NAME>`, `--lib`, `--bin <NAME>`, `--tests`, etc.) — selects which test *binary/target* to run. `--test` takes a single name, no globs. You can repeat it: `--test foo --test bar`.

2. **Test name filter** (`TESTNAME` positional arg) — filters *within* a test binary by test function name substring. So `cargo test my_func` runs only tests containing "my_func". This supports no globs either, just substring match.

3. **After `--`** — args to the test harness itself: `--ignored`, `--nocapture`, `--exact`, `--include-ignored` (runs both normal AND ignored), `--skip <PATTERN>` (excludes tests matching pattern).

So to answer your questions:

- **Yes**, without `--ignored` you can select tests: `tspec test -p tspec --test integration_test` runs only the `integration_test` target (normal tests only).
- **No**, cargo doesn't support globs for `--test` — it's an exact name. But you can pass `--tests` to run all test targets.
- **For include/exclude lists**: `cargo test -- --skip pattern` skips tests matching "pattern". And `cargo test -- --include-ignored` runs both normal and ignored tests.

Currently our `--test` only takes one name. We could support multiple `--test` values. And we might want to expose `--lib`, `--tests`, `--bins` too — but those could just go through `extra_args` via the `--` mechanism.

Want me to allow multiple `--test` values, or is single enough for now?

> DEVELOPER

I assume multiple is "trivial" if so add it if not we'll add it later. One or Q, in tspec and cargo "--tests" is the default workspace wide tests, correct?

> AGENT

Multiple is trivial — just change `Option<String>` to `Vec<String>`.

On your question: **no**, `--tests` is not the default. In cargo:

- **`cargo test`** (no flags) — runs everything: lib tests, bin tests, integration tests, AND doctests
- **`cargo test --tests`** — runs all targets with `test = true` but **excludes doctests**
- **`cargo test --test foo`** — runs only the specific integration test target `foo`

So `--tests` is actually a *narrower* selection than the default (it drops doctests). The default is "run everything."

In tspec, `tspec test -p tspec` maps to `cargo test -p tspec` — the full default. And `tspec test -w` iterates workspace members, each getting `cargo test -p <name>`.

Let me add multiple `--test` support now.

> AGENT

Good to know — `tspec test` with no args and `tspec test -w` both end up in all-packages mode. That's because `current_package_name()` returns `None` at a workspace root (or POP+WS root like tspec/rlibc-x), which triggers the `None` branch in `execute()` — same as `-w`.

The difference would only show if you're `cd`-ed into a member subdirectory: `tspec test` would test just that package, while `tspec test -w` forces all-packages mode.

Committed `0e7bd59`.

Remember to commit `.claude/` session files.

> AGENT

So here's the picture:

**`cargo test --test <NAME>`** — `<NAME>` is a **test target** (integration test file), not a package. For tspec, the only integration test target is `tspec_test` (from `tests/tspec_test.rs`). When we add `tests/integration_test.rs`, there'll be a second one.

**Listing test targets:** `cargo test --no-run` shows the compiled test binaries (targets). **Listing test functions:** `cargo test -- --list` lists every test function name.

Both of these already work through our `--` passthrough:
```bash
tspec test -p tspec -- --list          # list all test functions
```

And `--no-run` could go through `--test` or just be passed after `--`... actually `--no-run` is a cargo arg, not a test harness arg. It would need to go before `--`. Right now our `extra_args` handles that since `--test` goes there too.

Do you want to add a dedicated `tspec test --list` convenience flag, or is `tspec test -p tspec -- --list` sufficient for now? A dedicated flag could combine `--no-run` (show targets) and `-- --list` (show functions) into a single nice output.

> DEVELOPER

I'd like `tspec test --list`, so I can easily know what tests there are and verify `tspec test --test <name>` works. Although how do I use cargo to run a particular test, I used `cargo test -- --lists` and here is one of them `types::tests::spec_default_is_empty: test` but do I run that one test?

> AGENT

To run a single test by name with cargo:

```bash
cargo test -p tspec types::tests::spec_default_is_empty        # substring match
cargo test -p tspec -- --exact types::tests::spec_default_is_empty  # exact match
```

The first positional arg to `cargo test` is a **substring filter** — it runs all tests whose name contains that string. `--exact` (after `--`) makes it match the full name only.

So with tspec today you could do:

```bash
tspec test -p tspec -- types::tests::spec_default_is_empty      # substring (works now via trailing args... actually no)
```

Hmm, actually that won't work — the test name filter is a cargo positional arg (before `--`), not a test harness arg (after `--`). Our current `extra_args` could handle it, but the user would need to know the distinction.

For `tspec test --list` — should it show:
1. **Test targets** only (like `--no-run` output: `tspec_test`, `lib`, `bin`) — quick
2. **Test functions** only (`-- --list` output: `types::tests::spec_default_is_empty: test`) — detailed
3. **Both** — targets as headers, functions nested under each

Which do you prefer? And do you want this in the same dev1 commit (amend) or as a separate step?

wink@3900x 26-02-20T01:11:29.152Z:~/data/prgs/rust/tspec (feat-test-fixtures)


If we do support that I'd sure like to only show the one result and not all the noise, I know it possible, but is it practical/worth it, your opinion?

> AGENT

Honest opinion:

**`tspec test --list`** — practical and high value. We run `cargo test -p <name> -- --list` and format the output (group by target, clean headers). Worth doing now.

**Quiet single-test execution** — desirable but harder than it looks. The noise comes from cargo running every test binary even when only one matches. To suppress it, tspec would need to:
1. Run `-- --list` first to discover which target contains the test
2. Then run `--test <that_target> -- --exact <name>`

That's two cargo invocations and output parsing. Doable, but it's a feature in itself — not a quick addition. And it could have edge cases (test name appears in multiple targets, etc.).

**My suggestion:** Add `--list` now (dev1 scope, it's just formatting cargo's output). Note the quiet single-test execution as a future todo item. For now, users who want less noise can manually narrow with `--test <target>`:

```bash
tspec test -p tspec --test tspec_test -- --exact config_kv_hash_is_stable
```

That only runs one binary, so no "running 0 tests" spam. The `--list` output would tell them which target to use.

Want me to add `--list` to dev1?

> DEVELOPER

Good thoughts but one other option is instead of running twice we capture the output and filter it we do that to get the results at the bottom of other commands like compare and others

> AGENT

Good point — capture-and-filter is simpler than two invocations and we already do it elsewhere. For a single test run, capture stdout, drop the "running 0 tests" sections, show only the target that matched. One invocation, clean output.

That said, both `--list` and quiet-single-test are separate from the core dev1 goal (fixture infrastructure). Want to:

1. Add both to dev1 scope (getting bigger but all test-UX related)
2. Add `--list` to dev1, note quiet-single-test as a todo
3. Note both as todos, move to dev2 (POP fixture)

I'd lean toward option 2 — `--list` is quick and immediately useful for verifying `--test` works, and quiet-single-test is a meatier change. But your call.

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};
     7→use crate::all::{print_test_summary, test_all};
     8→use crate::cargo_build::test_package;
     9→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    10→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    11→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    12→
    13→/// Test package(s) with a translation spec
    14→#[derive(Args)]
    15→pub struct TestCmd {
    16→    /// Package to test (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    17→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    18→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    19→    /// Package to test (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    20→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    21→    pub package: Option<String>,
    22→    /// Test all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    24→    pub workspace: bool,
    25→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to test with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    26→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    27→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    28→    /// Release build
    29→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    30→    pub release: bool,
    31→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    32→    #[arg(long)]
    33→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    34→    /// Stop on first failure
    35→    #[arg(short, long)]
    36→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    37→    /// Select specific test target(s) (passed as cargo test --test <NAME>; repeatable)
    38→    #[arg(long = "test")]
    39→    pub test_target: Vec<String>,
    40→    /// Extra arguments passed after -- to the test binary (e.g., --ignored)
    41→    #[arg(last = true)]
    42→    pub test_args: Vec<String>,
    43→}
    44→
    45→impl TestCmd {
    46→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    47→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    48→            Some(p)
    49→        } else if self.release {
    50→            Some("release")
    51→        } else {
    52→            None
    53→        }
    54→    }
    55→}
    56→
    57→impl Execute for TestCmd {
    58→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    59→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    60→
    61→        // Build extra_args from --test and trailing args
    62→        let flags = if !self.test_target.is_empty() || !self.test_args.is_empty() {
    63→            let mut f = flags.clone();
    64→            for target in &self.test_target {
    65→                f.extra_args.push("--test".to_string());
    66→                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());
    67→            }
    68→            if !self.test_args.is_empty() {
    69→                f.extra_args.push("--".to_string());
    70→                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());
    71→            }
    72→            f
    73→        } else {
    74→            flags.clone()
    75→        };
    76→
    77→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    78→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    79→            None
    80→        } else {
    81→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    82→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    83→                None => current_package_name(),
    84→            }
    85→        };
    86→
    87→        match resolved {
    88→            None => {
    89→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    90→                let results =
    91→                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);
    92→                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    93→            }
    94→            Some(name) => {
    95→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    96→                    test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
    97→                } else {
    98→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    99→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
   100→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
   101→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
   102→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
   103→                        test_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, &flags)?;
   104→                    }
   105→                }
   106→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   107→            }
   108→        }
   109→    }
   110→}
   111→
   112→#[cfg(test)]
   113→mod tests {
   114→    use super::*;
   115→    use crate::cli::Cli;
   116→    use clap::Parser;
   117→
   118→    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> TestCmd {
   119→        let mut full = vec!["tspec", "test"];
   120→        full.extend_from_slice(args);
   121→        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(full).unwrap();
   122→        match cli.command {
   123→            crate::cli::Commands::Test(cmd) => cmd,
   124→            _ => panic!("expected Test command"),
   125→        }
   126→    }
   127→
   128→    #[test]
   129→    fn package_optional() {
   130→        let cmd = parse(&[]);
   131→        assert!(cmd.package.is_none());
   132→    }
   133→
   134→    #[test]
   135→    fn package_explicit() {
   136→        let cmd = parse(&["-p", "myapp"]);
   137→        assert_eq!(cmd.package.as_deref(), Some("myapp"));
   138→    }
   139→
   140→    #[test]
   141→    fn tspec_empty_by_default() {
   142→        let cmd = parse(&[]);
   143→        assert!(cmd.tspec.is_empty());
   144→    }
   145→
   146→    #[test]
   147→    fn tspec_single_file() {
   148→        let cmd = parse(&["-t", "foo.ts.toml"]);
   149→        assert_eq!(cmd.tspec, vec!["foo.ts.toml"]);
   150→    }
   151→
   152→    #[test]
   153→    fn tspec_multiple_values_one_flag() {
   154→        let cmd = parse(&["-t", "a.ts.toml", "b.ts.toml"]);
   155→        assert_eq!(cmd.tspec, vec!["a.ts.toml", "b.ts.toml"]);
   156→    }
   157→
   158→    #[test]
   159→    fn tspec_multiple_flags() {
   160→        let cmd = parse(&["-t", "a.ts.toml", "-t", "b.ts.toml"]);
   161→        assert_eq!(cmd.tspec, vec!["a.ts.toml", "b.ts.toml"]);
   162→    }
   163→
   164→    #[test]
   165→    fn all_flags_together() {
   166→        let cmd = parse(&["-p", "myapp", "-t", "spec.ts.toml"]);
   167→        assert_eq!(cmd.package.as_deref(), Some("myapp"));
   168→        assert_eq!(cmd.tspec, vec!["spec.ts.toml"]);
   169→    }
   170→
   171→    #[test]
   172→    fn workspace_flag_short() {
   173→        let cmd = parse(&["-w"]);
   174→        assert!(cmd.workspace);
   175→    }
   176→
   177→    #[test]
   178→    fn workspace_flag_long() {
   179→        let cmd = parse(&["--workspace"]);
   180→        assert!(cmd.workspace);
   181→    }
   182→
   183→    #[test]
   184→    fn workspace_default_false() {
   185→        let cmd = parse(&[]);
   186→        assert!(!cmd.workspace);
   187→    }
   188→
   189→    #[test]
   190→    fn fail_fast_flag() {
   191→        let cmd = parse(&["-w", "-f"]);
   192→        assert!(cmd.workspace);
   193→        assert!(cmd.fail_fast);
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    #[test]
   197→    fn fail_fast_long() {
   198→        let cmd = parse(&["--workspace", "--fail-fast"]);
   199→        assert!(cmd.workspace);
   200→        assert!(cmd.fail_fast);
   201→    }
   202→
   203→    #[test]
   204→    fn release_flag() {
   205→        let cmd = parse(&["-r"]);
   206→        assert!(cmd.release);
   207→    }
   208→
   209→    #[test]
   210→    fn profile_flag() {
   211→        let cmd = parse(&["--profile", "release-small"]);
   212→        assert_eq!(cmd.profile.as_deref(), Some("release-small"));
   213→        assert_eq!(cmd.effective_profile(), Some("release-small"));
   214→    }
   215→
   216→    #[test]
   217→    fn profile_and_release_conflict() {
   218→        let result = Cli::try_parse_from(["tspec", "test", "-r", "--profile", "custom"]);
   219→        assert!(result.is_err());

Now add the `list_tests` function before the `#[cfg(test)]` module:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01C3zq73WZWaydp56kpt2uEX
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cmd/test.rs",
  "old_string": "#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {",
  "new_string": "/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output.\n///\n/// Groups test functions under their target headers, showing counts per target\n/// and a total. Skips targets with zero tests.\nfn list_tests(_project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n\n    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n    cmd.arg(\"test\");\n    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);\n    cmd.args([\"--\", \"--list\"]);\n    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n\n    let output = cmd.output().context(\"failed to run cargo test -- --list\")?;\n\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);\n        eprintln!(\"{}\", stderr);\n        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));\n    }\n\n    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);\n    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);\n\n    // Parse target headers from stderr (\"Running ...\" and \"Doc-tests ...\" lines)\n    let targets: Vec<&str> = stderr\n        .lines()\n        .filter_map(|line| {\n            let trimmed = line.trim();\n            if trimmed.starts_with(\"Running \") {\n                // \"Running unittests src/lib.rs (target/...)\" or \"Running tests/foo.rs (target/...)\"\n                Some(trimmed.strip_prefix(\"Running \").unwrap())\n            } else if trimmed.starts_with(\"Doc-tests \") {\n                Some(trimmed)\n            } else {\n                None\n            }\n        })\n        .collect();\n\n    // Parse stdout into groups split by \"N tests, M benchmarks\" summary lines\n    let mut groups: Vec<Vec<&str>> = Vec::new();\n    let mut current: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();\n    for line in stdout.lines() {\n        if line.contains(\"tests,\") && line.contains(\"benchmarks\") {\n            groups.push(std::mem::take(&mut current));\n        } else if line.ends_with(\": test\") || line.ends_with(\": bench\") {\n            current.push(line);\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Print grouped output\n    let mut total = 0;\n    for (i, tests) in groups.iter().enumerate() {\n        if tests.is_empty() {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let header = targets.get(i).unwrap_or(&\"unknown target\");\n        println!(\"{} ({} tests)\", header, tests.len());\n        for test in tests {\n            // Strip \": test\" / \": bench\" suffix for cleaner output\n            let name = test\n                .strip_suffix(\": test\")\n                .or_else(|| test.strip_suffix(\": bench\"))\n                .unwrap_or(test);\n            println!(\"  {}\", name);\n        }\n        total += tests.len();\n        println!();\n    }\n\n    println!(\"{} tests total\", total);\n    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cmd/test.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output.\n///\n/// Groups test functions under their target headers, showing counts per target\n/// and a total. Skips targets with zero tests.\nfn list_tests(_project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n\n    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n    cmd.arg(\"test\");\n    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);\n    cmd.args([\"--\", \"--list\"]);\n    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);",
  "new_string": "/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output.\n///\n/// Groups test functions under their target headers, showing counts per target\n/// and a total. Skips targets with zero tests.\nfn list_tests(package: Option<&str>, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n\n    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n    cmd.arg(\"test\");\n    if let Some(pkg) = package {\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(pkg);\n    }\n    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);\n    cmd.args([\"--\", \"--list\"]);\n    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);"
}

```
     1→# tspec - Translation Spec Build System
     2→
     3→A spec-driven build system for Rust that wraps cargo with configurable target triples, compiler flags, and linker options. Each tspec applies to a Cargo **package** — the unit with a `Cargo.toml`. If different crates need different compilation settings, they should be in separate packages.
     4→
     5→**Status:** Active development. Works with both workspaces and single-package projects (i.e. Plain Old Packages, POPs).
     6→
     7→## Installation
     8→
     9→```bash
    10→cargo install --path .
    11→```
    12→
    13→Or run directly:
    14→```bash
    15→cargo run -- build
    16→```
    17→
    18→## Contributing
    19→
    20→**All non-trivial changes must be on a feature branch** — never commit directly to `main`. This applies even for solo development.
    21→
    22→```bash
    23→git checkout -b feat-my-feature    # Create a branch
    24→# ... make changes, commit ...
    25→git checkout main                  # Switch back
    26→git merge --no-ff feat-my-feature  # Merge with merge commit (or squash-merge)
    27→git push
    28→```
    29→
    30→Branch naming: `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`.
    31→
    32→**Versioning:** Single-step changes bump directly (e.g., `0.14.0` → `0.15.0`). Multi-step features use a `-devN` series: `-dev0` for the marker/planning commit, then `-dev1`, `-dev2`, etc. for each code step, dropping the suffix on release.
    33→
    34→**Protect main on GitHub:** Enable branch protection (Settings > Branches) to block force pushes and optionally require PRs.
    35→
    36→For the full feature workflow (version bumps, notes, merge steps, Claude Code conventions), see [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md#feature-workflow).
    37→
    38→## Usage
    39→
    40→```bash
    41→tspec build                                # Build current package (or all in workspace) with tspec.ts.toml if present
    42→tspec build -p myapp                       # Build specific package with tspec.ts.toml if present
    43→tspec build myapp                          # Same, using positional argument
    44→tspec build .                              # Build the package in current directory
    45→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt          # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    46→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt.ts.toml  # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    47→tspec build -p myapp -r -s                 # Build release, strip symbols with tspec.ts.toml if present
    48→tspec run -p myapp                         # Build and run with tspec.ts.toml if present
    49→tspec test -p myapp                        # Build and test with tspec.ts.toml if present
    50→tspec build -w                             # Build all packages (even from inside a package dir)
    51→tspec build -t 'tspec*'                    # Build all packages using matching specs (quote the glob!)
    52→tspec build -t 'tspec-opt*'               # Build all packages with tspec-opt specs only
    53→tspec compare -p myapp                     # Compare binary sizes for a package
    54→tspec compare -p myapp -t *.ts.toml        # Compare using shell-expanded glob
    55→tspec compare                              # Compare all packages (workspace mode)
    56→tspec compare -w                           # Force workspace mode from inside a package
    57→tspec compare -w -f                        # Workspace mode, stop on first failure
    58→tspec -v build .                           # Verbose: show cargo command/env + cargo -v
    59→tspec -vv build .                          # Debug: also show spec details + cargo -vv
    60→tspec -j4 build .                          # Limit cargo to 4 parallel jobs
    61→```
    62→
    63→The `-p` flag or positional argument specifies a package by name or path (defaults to current directory if in a package, otherwise all packages). Paths like `.` are resolved to the actual cargo package name. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` means "all packages."
    64→Use `-w, --workspace` to force all-packages mode even when inside a package directory.
    65→The `-t` flag selects a tspec file; if omitted and `tspec.ts.toml` exists, it's used automatically. In workspace mode (no `-p`, or with `-w`), `-t` patterns are resolved per-package — each sub-package is searched for matching specs, and packages with no matches are skipped.
    66→
    67→**Global flags** apply to all commands and are passed through to cargo: `-v` (cargo `-v`, shows rustc invocations), `-vv` (cargo `-vv`, full command details), and `-j N` (parallel jobs). tspec's own verbose output (command line, env vars, spec resolution) is also controlled by `-v`/`-vv`.
    68→
    69→**Important: quote glob patterns with `-t`.** The shell expands unquoted globs *before* tspec sees them. At a workspace root, `tspec build -t tspec*` may expand to literal filenames from the root directory (or worse, match `target/`), rather than being matched per-package. Always quote: `-t 'tspec*'`. tspec will warn if it detects shell-expanded non-tspec arguments.
    70→
    71→**Note on `cmd` vs `cmd .`:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), while `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata files. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    72→
    73→## tspec Files
    74→
    75→Translation specs are TOML files (conventionally `*.ts.toml`) that configure builds:
    76→
    77→```toml
    78→# tspec.ts.toml - all supported fields shown
    79→
    80→# Top-level high-level options (expand to lower-level cargo/rustc flags)
    81→panic = "abort"                            # "unwind" (default), "abort", "immediate-abort" (nightly)
    82→strip = "symbols"                          # "none" (default), "debuginfo", "symbols"
    83→toolchain = "nightly"                      # override toolchain (e.g., "nightly", "stable", "1.75")
    84→rustflags = ["-C", "relocation-model=static"]  # raw flags passed through to RUSTFLAGS
    85→
    86→[cargo]
    87→profile = "release"                        # "debug" (default), "release", or custom profile
    88→target_triple = "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl"
    89→target_json = "path/to/custom-target.json" # custom target spec (auto-adds -Z json-target-spec)
    90→unstable = ["panic-immediate-abort"]       # -Z flags (nightly only)
    91→target_dir = "{name}"                      # per-spec target dir; supports {name} and {hash} placeholders
    92→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]              # crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly)
    93→
    94→[cargo.config.profile.release]             # nested tables under [cargo.config] become individual
    95→opt-level = "z"                            # --config KEY=VALUE args to cargo; any valid cargo
    96→lto = true                                 # config key works (profile, build, target, etc.)
    97→codegen-units = 1
    98→
    99→[linker]
   100→args = ["-static", "-nostartfiles"]
   101→
   102→[linker.version_script]                    # symbol visibility control (enables --gc-sections)
   103→global = ["_start"]                        # symbols to keep
   104→local = "*"                                # default: "*" (hide everything else)
   105→```
   106→
   107→The top-level `panic` sets both cargo `-Z` and rustc `-C` flags automatically.
   108→
   109→### Key Concepts
   110→
   111→- **One tspec per package** - A tspec applies to all crates (targets) within a Cargo package. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
   112→- **tspec.ts.toml is optional** - Packages without one get plain `cargo build/test`
   113→- **Generated build.rs** - tspec generates temporary build.rs for scoped linker flags
   114→- **Spec comparison** - Compare binary sizes across different specs; always shows both `cargo --release` and `cargo --release-strip` baselines. Supports `-w` for all-packages mode — per-package tables are collected and printed together at the end. With a single package, only its comparison table is shown
   115→
   116→### How Linker Flags Work
   117→
   118→tspec generates a temporary `build.rs` to apply linker flags with binary-scoped directives:
   119→
   120→```rust
   121→// Generated by tspec - deleted after build
   122→fn main() {
   123→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-static");
   124→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-nostdlib");
   125→}
   126→```
   127→
   128→The only other alternative is to add these linker flags to RUSTFLAGS, but that
   129→affects ALL compilations (including dependency build scripts), causing crashes
   130→with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated build.rs scopes flags to just
   131→the binary.
   132→
   133→### Cargo Config Passthrough (`cargo.config`)
   134→
   135→The `[cargo.config]` section is a general-purpose passthrough to cargo's `--config KEY=VALUE` mechanism. Any nested tables under `[cargo.config]` are flattened into dotted keys and passed as `--config` args. This lets you override any [cargo configuration](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html) value at the command line.
   136→
   137→**Profile settings** are the most common use case:
   138→
   139→```toml
   140→[cargo.config.profile.release]
   141→opt-level = "z"
   142→codegen-units = 1
   143→lto = true
   144→```
   145→
   146→This produces: `cargo --config profile.release.opt-level="z" --config profile.release.codegen-units=1 --config profile.release.lto=true`
   147→
   148→**Other cargo config keys** work too:
   149→
   150→```toml
   151→[cargo.config.target.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu]
   152→linker = "clang"
   153→runner = "qemu-x86_64"
   154→```
   155→
   156→**Note:** Do not use `build.rustflags` in `[cargo.config]`. tspec sets the `RUSTFLAGS` env var (from top-level `rustflags`, `panic`, and `strip`), which takes precedence over `--config build.rustflags` per cargo's precedence rules. Use the top-level `rustflags` array instead.
   157→
   158→**Custom profiles:** Any profile defined in your workspace `Cargo.toml` (with `inherits`) can be used. tspec validates that custom profiles exist before invoking cargo. Use `cargo.profile` in the spec or `--profile` on the CLI.
   159→
   160→**Flat dotted keys** also work — TOML treats them equivalently:
   161→
   162→```toml
   163→[cargo.config]
   164→"profile.release.opt-level" = "z"
   165→```
   166→
   167→## tspec Management (ts subcommand)
   168→
   169→Manage spec files without manual TOML editing. All commands that modify files preserve comments and formatting.
   170→
   171→| Command | Description |
   172→|---------|-------------|
   173→| `ts list` | List tspec files found in the workspace or a specific package |
   174→| `ts show` | Display the TOML contents of a tspec file |
   175→| `ts hash` | Print the content hash (used in backup filenames) of a tspec |
   176→| `ts new` | Create a new tspec with defaults, or copy an existing one with `-f` (byte-for-byte) |
   177→| `ts set` | Set a scalar value or replace an entire array |
   178→| `ts unset` | Remove a field (scalar or array) from a tspec entirely |
   179→| `ts add` | Add items to an array field (append or insert at position) |
   180→| `ts remove` | Remove items from an array field (by value or by index) |
   181→| `ts backup` | Create a versioned backup copy (`name-NNN-hash.ts.toml`) |
   182→| `ts restore` | Restore a tspec by copying a versioned backup back to its original name |
   183→
   184→```bash
   185→# List / show / hash
   186→tspec ts list                            # List all tspec files
   187→tspec ts list -p myapp                   # List tspec files for a package
   188→tspec ts show -p myapp                   # Show all tspec contents
   189→tspec ts show -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show specific tspec-opt.ts.toml
   190→tspec ts hash -p myapp                   # Show content hash of tspec.ts.toml
   191→
   192→# Create new tspec files
   193→tspec ts new -p myapp                    # Create tspec.ts.toml with defaults
   194→tspec ts new experiment -p myapp         # Create experiment.ts.toml
   195→tspec ts new opt2 -p myapp -f tspec-opt  # Copy from existing spec (byte-for-byte)
   196→
   197→# Set scalar values
   198→tspec ts set strip symbols -p myapp              # Set a scalar field
   199→tspec ts set cargo.profile release               # No quoting needed
   200→
   201→# Set (replace) entire arrays — each element is a separate arg
   202→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib        # Replace array
   203→tspec ts set cargo.build_std core alloc           # Replace array
   204→tspec ts set rustflags -C relocation-model=static # Replace array
   205→
   206→# Add items to arrays (append by default)
   207→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections        # Append one item
   208→tspec ts add linker.args -nostdlib -pie            # Append multiple items
   209→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles        # Insert at position 0
   210→
   211→# Remove items from arrays
   212→tspec ts remove linker.args -nostdlib              # Remove by value
   213→tspec ts remove linker.args -static -pie           # Remove multiple by value
   214→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args                   # Remove by index
   215→
   216→# Remove a field entirely
   217→tspec ts unset strip -p myapp                      # Remove scalar field
   218→tspec ts unset linker.args -t tspec-opt            # Remove array field
   219→
   220→# Backup and restore (byte-for-byte copies)
   221→tspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Backup tspec.ts.toml → tspec-001-abcd1234.ts.toml
   222→tspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup tspec-opt.ts.toml
   223→tspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup
   224→```
   225→
   226→Backups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.
   227→
   228→`ts set`, `ts unset`, `ts add`, and `ts remove` use `toml_edit` for surgical editing — comments and formatting are preserved.
   229→`ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` use byte-for-byte file copy — comments are preserved exactly.
   230→
   231→### Supported keys
   232→
   233→Keys use two forms: `FIELD` for top-level fields (`panic`, `strip`, `rustflags`) or `TABLE.FIELD` for nested fields (`cargo.profile`, `linker.args`).
   234→
   235→| Key | Type | Values |
   236→|-----|------|--------|
   237→| `panic` | scalar | `unwind`, `abort`, `immediate-abort` |
   238→| `strip` | scalar | `none`, `debuginfo`, `symbols` |
   239→| `toolchain` | scalar | e.g. `nightly`, `stable`, `nightly-2025-01-15`, `1.75` |
   240→| `rustflags` | array | e.g. `-C relocation-model=static` |
   241→| `cargo.profile` | scalar | `debug`, `release`, or any custom profile defined in `Cargo.toml` |
   242→| `cargo.target_triple` | scalar | any string |
   243→| `cargo.target_json` | scalar | any path |
   244→| `cargo.target_dir` | scalar | any string (supports `{name}`, `{hash}`) |
   245→| `cargo.unstable` | array | e.g. `panic-immediate-abort` |
   246→| `cargo.config` | table | arbitrary `--config KEY=VALUE` passthrough; supports nested tables (e.g. `[cargo.config.profile.release]`) |
   247→| `cargo.build_std` | array | e.g. `core alloc` |
   248→| `linker.args` | array | e.g. `-static -nostdlib` |
   249→
   250→### Array operations
   251→
   252→Four orthogonal commands operate on arrays at two levels:
   253→
   254→| Level | Command | Purpose |
   255→|-------|---------|---------|
   256→| Field-level | `ts set` | Replace entire array with new values |
   257→| Field-level | `ts unset` | Remove the array field entirely |
   258→| Item-level | `ts add` | Append items (or insert at position with `-i`) |
   259→| Item-level | `ts remove` | Remove items by value (or by index with `-i`) |
   260→
   261→```bash
   262→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib   # replace all with these
   263→tspec ts unset linker.args                   # clear the field
   264→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections   # append (skips duplicates)
   265→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles  # insert at position
   266→tspec ts remove linker.args -static          # remove by value
   267→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args             # remove by index
   268→```
   269→
   270→Values starting with `-` work without quoting thanks to `allow_hyphen_values`.
   271→Use `--` as an escape hatch if a value collides with a flag like `-p`, `-t`, or `-i`.
   272→
   273→Note: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for "tspec management").
   274→
   275→## Testing
   276→
   277→```bash
   278→tspec test -p tspec           # Run tspec tests
   279→tspec test -p tspec-build     # Run tspec-build tests
   280→```
   281→
   282→## Project Structure
   283→
   284→```
   285→tspec/                  # Workspace root (also the main tspec package)
   286→  Cargo.toml            # [workspace] + [package] — workspace with root package
   287→  src/
   288→    lib.rs              # Library root, exposes modules
   289→    main.rs             # Entry point, dispatch
   290→    cli.rs              # Clap CLI definitions
   291→    types.rs            # Spec parameter types
   292→    tspec.rs            # Spec loading/saving/hashing
   293→    find_paths.rs       # Project/package/tspec/binary path discovery
   294→    workspace.rs        # Workspace package discovery
   295→    cargo_build.rs      # Unified cargo runner (build + test) + generated build.rs
   296→    run.rs              # Run command implementation
   297→    compare.rs          # Compare command (size comparison)
   298→    all.rs              # Batch operations (build_all, run_all, test_all, compare_all)
   299→    cmd/                # Command implementations (one file per command)
   300→    ts_cmd/             # Tspec management subcommands
   301→    binary.rs           # Binary operations (strip, size)
   302→  tests/
   303→    data/               # Test fixtures (TOML specs)
   304→    tspec_test.rs       # Integration tests
   305→  tspec-build/          # Library crate for build.rs integration
   306→    src/lib.rs          # Reads TSPEC_SPEC_FILE env var at build time
   307→  notes/                # Design docs and todo tracking
   308→```
   309→
   310→## Development
   311→
   312→See [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md) for current tasks.
   313→
   314→### Verification
   315→
   316→```bash
   317→tspec test -p tspec
   318→tspec test -p tspec-build
   319→tspec clippy
   320→tspec fmt --check
   321→```

    64→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    65→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    66→
    67→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    68→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    69→            None
    70→        } else {
    71→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    72→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    73→                None => current_package_name(),
    74→            }
    75→        };
    76→
    77→        if self.list {
    78→            return list_tests(resolved.as_deref(), flags);
    79→        }
    80→
    81→        // Build extra_args from --test and trailing args
    82→        let flags = if !self.test_target.is_empty() || !self.test_args.is_empty() {
    83→            let mut f = flags.clone();
    84→            for target in &self.test_target {
    85→                f.extra_args.push("--test".to_string());
    86→                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());
    87→            }
    88→            if !self.test_args.is_empty() {
    89→                f.extra_args.push("--".to_string());
    90→                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());
    91→            }
    92→            f
    93→        } else {
    94→            flags.clone()
    95→        };
    96→
    97→        match resolved {
    98→            None => {
    99→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
   100→                let results =
   101→                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);
   102→                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
   103→            }
   104→            Some(name) => {
   105→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
   106→                    test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
   107→                } else {
   108→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
   109→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
   110→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
   111→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
   112→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
   113→                        test_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, &flags)?;
   114→                    }
   115→                }
   116→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   117→            }
   118→        }

> AGENT

Good question. In cargo, the test name filter **is** positional:

```
cargo test TESTNAME [-- args]
```

But in tspec, the positional slot is already taken by PACKAGE (consistent across all commands — build, test, run, compare). So we have a conflict:

```
tspec test myapp          # PACKAGE (existing)
tspec test my_test_fn     # TESTNAME? ambiguous!
```

The `--name`/`-n` flag avoids ambiguity:

```
tspec test -p tspec-build -n spec_default     # clear: package + test filter
tspec test tspec-build -n spec_default        # also clear: positional package + named filter
```

The alternative would be to flip convention and make the positional be TESTNAME (like cargo), but that would:
1. Break consistency with build/run/compare which all use positional for PACKAGE
2. Force `-p` for all package selection in test (different from other commands)

I think `--name`/`-n` is the right tradeoff — it's one extra flag but keeps the PACKAGE positional consistent across all commands. Does that reasoning make sense, or would you prefer a different approach?

    41→        profile,

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cmd/test.rs",
  "old_string": "fn list_tests(package: Option<&str>, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n\n    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n    cmd.arg(\"test\");\n    if let Some(pkg) = package {\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(pkg);\n    }\n    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);\n    cmd.args([\"--\", \"--list\"]);",
  "new_string": "fn list_tests(\n    package: Option<&str>,\n    name_filter: Option<&str>,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n\n    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n    cmd.arg(\"test\");\n    if let Some(pkg) = package {\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(pkg);\n    }\n    if let Some(name) = name_filter {\n        cmd.arg(name);\n    }\n    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);\n    cmd.args([\"--\", \"--list\"]);"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/README.md",
  "old_string": "## Testing\n\n```bash\ntspec test -p tspec           # Run tspec tests\ntspec test -p tspec-build     # Run tspec-build tests\n```",
  "new_string": "## Testing\n\n```bash\ntspec test -p tspec                        # Run tspec tests\ntspec test -p tspec-build                  # Run tspec-build tests\ntspec test -w                              # Run all workspace tests\ntspec test -p tspec --list                 # List all test targets and functions\ntspec test -p tspec -n flatten_config      # Filter tests by name (substring match)\ntspec test -p tspec -n flatten --list      # List tests matching a name filter\ntspec test --test integration_test         # Run a specific test target\ntspec test --test foo --test bar           # Run multiple test targets\ntspec test -- --ignored                    # Pass args to the test harness\ntspec test --test integration_test -- --ignored --nocapture  # Combined\n```\n\n**Test flags:**\n- `-n, --name <NAME>` — filter tests by name (substring match, like cargo's TESTNAME positional)\n- `--test <TARGET>` — select a specific test target (repeatable; maps to `cargo test --test`)\n- `--list` / `-l` — list test targets and functions instead of running them\n- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing arguments to the test binary (e.g., `--ignored`, `--exact`, `--nocapture`)"
}
