> AGENT

Good point. Let me change `name_filter` from `Option<String>` to `Vec<String>` with `--names`/`-n`, allowing one or more values. In cargo, TESTNAME is a single substring, but since we're wrapping it, we can support multiple by running cargo once per name or by joining them with `|` as a regex.

Actually, cargo test's TESTNAME is a single string that acts as a substring filter. It doesn't support multiple. But we could accept multiple `-n` values and pass them as separate cargo invocations... that's heavyweight. 

The simpler approach: cargo's TESTNAME supports regex patterns — `cargo test "foo|bar"` matches both. So we can join multiple `-n` values with `|`.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cmd/test.rs",
  "old_string": "        // Build extra_args from --name, --test, and trailing args\n        let has_extra = self.name_filter.is_some()\n            || !self.test_target.is_empty()\n            || !self.test_args.is_empty();\n        let flags = if has_extra {\n            let mut f = flags.clone();\n            // name_filter is cargo's TESTNAME positional (must come before --test and --)\n            if let Some(ref name) = self.name_filter {\n                f.extra_args.push(name.clone());\n            }",
  "new_string": "        // Build extra_args from --names, --test, and trailing args\n        let has_extra = !self.name_filter.is_empty()\n            || !self.test_target.is_empty()\n            || !self.test_args.is_empty();\n        let flags = if has_extra {\n            let mut f = flags.clone();\n            // name_filter becomes cargo's TESTNAME positional (before --test and --)\n            // Multiple names are joined with | as a regex pattern\n            if !self.name_filter.is_empty() {\n                f.extra_args.push(self.name_filter.join(\"|\"));\n            }"
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "fn list_tests(\n    package: Option<&str>,\n    name_filter: Option<&str>,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n\n    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n    cmd.arg(\"test\");\n    if let Some(pkg) = package {\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(pkg);\n    }\n    if let Some(name) = name_filter {\n        cmd.arg(name);\n    }",
  "new_string": "fn list_tests(\n    package: Option<&str>,\n    name_filter: &[String],\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n\n    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n    cmd.arg(\"test\");\n    if let Some(pkg) = package {\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(pkg);\n    }\n    if !name_filter.is_empty() {\n        cmd.arg(name_filter.join(\"|\"));\n    }"
}
```

> AGENT

`[FILTERS...]` — multiple filters are supported natively! They go after `--`. So the right approach is to pass multiple names as separate test harness filters after `--`, not joined with `|`.

Let me rethink the design. The filters are test harness arguments (after `--`), not cargo arguments (before `--`). So:

```
cargo test -p tspec-build -- resolve_spec emit --list
```

Multiple filters after `--` work as OR match. So the correct approach: `--names` values should be passed as test harness filters after `--`, not before it. Let me update the implementation:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01DzikAaR38v5yHKX814v4T6
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cmd/test.rs",
  "old_string": "        // Build extra_args from --names, --test, and trailing args\n        let has_extra = !self.name_filter.is_empty()\n            || !self.test_target.is_empty()\n            || !self.test_args.is_empty();\n        let flags = if has_extra {\n            let mut f = flags.clone();\n            // name_filter becomes cargo's TESTNAME positional (before --test and --)\n            // Multiple names are joined with | as a regex pattern\n            if !self.name_filter.is_empty() {\n                f.extra_args.push(self.name_filter.join(\"|\"));\n            }\n            for target in &self.test_target {\n                f.extra_args.push(\"--test\".to_string());\n                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());\n            }\n            if !self.test_args.is_empty() {\n                f.extra_args.push(\"--\".to_string());\n                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());\n            }\n            f\n        } else {\n            flags.clone()\n        };",
  "new_string": "        // Build extra_args from --names, --test, and trailing args\n        let has_extra = !self.name_filter.is_empty()\n            || !self.test_target.is_empty()\n            || !self.test_args.is_empty();\n        let flags = if has_extra {\n            let mut f = flags.clone();\n            for target in &self.test_target {\n                f.extra_args.push(\"--test\".to_string());\n                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());\n            }\n            // Name filters and test_args both go after --\n            // The test harness accepts [FILTERS...] as OR-matched substrings\n            if !self.name_filter.is_empty() || !self.test_args.is_empty() {\n                f.extra_args.push(\"--\".to_string());\n                f.extra_args.extend(self.name_filter.clone());\n                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());\n            }\n            f\n        } else {\n            flags.clone()\n        };"
}
```

> AGENT

Committed `8134354`.

Remember to commit `.claude/` session files.

**Summary of dev1:** Added four new flags to `tspec test`:
- `--test <TARGET>` (repeatable) — select specific test targets
- `-n/--names <NAME>...` — filter by name (multiple OR-matched via test harness)
- `-l/--list` — list test targets and functions, grouped with counts
- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing args to test binary

264 tests pass, clippy/fmt clean, installed and smoke-tested.

> DEVELOPER

I'm confused, what's a `--test <TARGET>` as opposed to `--names t1 t2`?

> AGENT

They operate at different levels:

- **`--test <TARGET>`** selects which test **binary** to run — maps to `cargo test --test <name>`. Each `tests/*.rs` file is a separate test target. For example, `--test integration_test` runs only `tests/integration_test.rs`, skipping unit tests and doctests entirely.

- **`--names t1 t2`** filters which test **functions** to run within the selected targets — maps to `cargo test -- t1 t2`. These are substring matches against function names like `tests::resolve_spec_path_with_explicit_path`.

Example with tspec itself — `tspec test -p tspec --list` shows targets like:

```
unittests src/lib.rs (263 tests)
tests/tspec_test.rs (8 tests)
Doc-tests tspec (0 tests)
```

`--test tspec_test` would run only the 8 integration tests. `--names flatten_config` would run only functions matching "flatten_config" across all targets. You can combine them: `--test tspec_test -n load_spec` runs only matching functions in that one target.

wink@3900x 26-02-20T05:27:47.414Z:~/data/prgs/rust/rlibc-x (main)


In that help it says; `--test [<NAME>]     Test only the specified test target` but that's not very clear is <NAME> a test name like `ts_cmd::edit::tests::unset_nonexistent_is_ok` or full path to a test file, just a basename of a file in `tests/`, confusing

> AGENT

Yeah, cargo's help is unclear. Here's what `--test [<NAME>]` actually means:

**NAME is the stem of a file in `tests/`** — not a test function, not a full path. So for tspec's structure:

```
tests/
  tspec_test.rs    →  --test tspec_test
```

It selects which **test binary** to compile and run. Each `tests/*.rs` becomes a separate binary. It has nothing to do with individual test functions like `ts_cmd::edit::tests::unset_nonexistent_is_ok`.

The mental model:
- `--test tspec_test` → "only run the binary built from `tests/tspec_test.rs`"
- `TESTNAME` / `--names` → "only run functions whose name contains this substring"

Right now tspec only has one integration test file, so `--test` isn't very useful yet. It'll matter more in dev2+ when we add `tests/integration_test.rs` with `#[ignore]` tests — then `--test integration_test -- --ignored` skips the 264 unit tests entirely and only runs the slow integration tests.

That said — is `--test` pulling its weight as a tspec flag, or would you rather drop it and just use `-- --test tspec_test` via trailing args when needed?

> DEVELOPER

If it's "common" then tspec should implement it the same way, personally I've not used it but that means nothing.

> AGENT

It's standard cargo — any project with multiple `tests/*.rs` files uses it to run just one (e.g., `cargo test --test api` vs `cargo test --test cli`). We'll use it ourselves once we add integration tests. It's already implemented and working, so let's keep it.

Ready to amend the commit now?

Looking at cargo's -h doc for "Target Selection":
Target Selection:
      --lib               Test only this package's library
      --bins              Test all binaries
      --bin [<NAME>]      Test only the specified binary
      --examples          Test all examples
      --example [<NAME>]  Test only the specified example
      --tests             Test all targets that have `test = true` set

      --doc               Test only this library's documentation


A number of "targets" have [<NAME>] are all of these mean "basename" in our current terminology?

> AGENT

Yes, in the common case — cargo auto-discovers them from conventional directories:

| Flag | Directory | `--bin foo` = `src/bin/foo.rs` |
|------|-----------|-------------------------------|
| `--bin` | `src/bin/` | basename of `.rs` file |
| `--example` | `examples/` | basename of `.rs` file |
| `--test` | `tests/` | basename of `.rs` file |
| `--bench` | `benches/` | basename of `.rs` file |

The one exception: any of these can be explicitly named in `Cargo.toml` via `[[bin]]`, `[[test]]`, `[[example]]`, `[[bench]]` sections with a `name` field that overrides the basename. But that's uncommon — most projects rely on auto-discovery.

So "basename without `.rs`" is accurate for the vast majority of usage. Want me to add a brief note in our docs that the name can also come from an explicit `[[test]]` declaration in `Cargo.toml`, or keep it simple?

> DEVELOPER

That exception is huge and surprising, what's the presidence, I'm guessing the name field in the section, in which case using the basename ain't going to work at all :(

> AGENT

You're right to flag it, but in practice it's less scary than it sounds:

1. If there are **no `[[test]]` sections** in Cargo.toml (the common case), cargo auto-discovers from `tests/` and the name **is** the basename.

2. If there **is** a `[[test]]` section, the `name` field wins — but people almost always set it to match the basename anyway:
   ```toml
   [[test]]
   name = "integration_test"   # matches tests/integration_test.rs
   harness = false              # ← this is why you'd add the section
   ```

3. The edge case where `name` differs from the basename is vanishingly rare.

The good news: `tspec test --list` already shows the actual names cargo knows about, so users can discover them without guessing.

I think the right fix for our docs is to say "the test name as shown by `--list`" and note it defaults to the basename. Something like:

> `--test <NAME>` — run only the named test (use `--list` to see available names; defaults to the basename of files in `tests/` without `.rs`)

That way we're accurate without overcomplicating it. Sound good?

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

Ha! Yeah, that's genuinely confusing. Let me untangle it:

- `binary::tests::binary_size_empty_file` — `binary` here is the **module path** inside `src/lib.rs`. It's `mod binary` in the library, not a test target. All 264 of those tests live in one binary (`unittests src/lib.rs`).

- `--test` only works with **integration test files** in `tests/` — that's why `--test binary` fails with "no test target named `binary`" and helpfully says `available test targets: tspec_test`.

- `-n binary` matches any test **function name** containing "binary" as a substring — so it catches both `binary::tests::*` and `find_paths::tests::get_binary_path_*`.

So there are really three different "name" concepts:

| What | Example | Used with |
|------|---------|-----------|
| Module path in function name | `binary::tests::...` | `-n` (substring match) |
| Integration test file | `tspec_test` (from `tests/tspec_test.rs`) | `--test` |
| Library unit tests | `unittests src/lib.rs` | not selectable individually by `--test` |

This makes me think the `--list` output should clearly distinguish these. Let me improve the headers to show the `--test` name when applicable, and label the unit tests differently so it's obvious `--test` doesn't apply to them.

```
   140→    cmd.arg("test");
   141→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   142→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   143→    }
   144→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   145→    cmd.arg("--");
   146→    cmd.args(name_filter);
   147→    cmd.arg("--list");
   148→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   149→
   150→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test -- --list")?;
   151→
   152→    if !output.status.success() {
   153→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   154→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   155→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   156→    }
   157→
   158→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   159→    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
   160→
   161→    // Parse target headers from stderr ("Running ..." and "Doc-tests ..." lines)
   162→    let targets: Vec<&str> = stderr
   163→        .lines()
   164→        .filter_map(|line| {
   165→            let trimmed = line.trim();
   166→            if trimmed.starts_with("Running ") {
   167→                // "Running unittests src/lib.rs (target/...)" or "Running tests/foo.rs (target/...)"
   168→                Some(trimmed.strip_prefix("Running ").unwrap())
   169→            } else if trimmed.starts_with("Doc-tests ") {
   170→                Some(trimmed)
   171→            } else {
   172→                None
   173→            }
   174→        })
   175→        .collect();
   176→
   177→    // Parse stdout into groups split by "N tests, M benchmarks" summary lines
   178→    let mut groups: Vec<Vec<&str>> = Vec::new();
   179→    let mut current: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
   180→    for line in stdout.lines() {
   181→        if line.contains("tests,") && line.contains("benchmarks") {
   182→            groups.push(std::mem::take(&mut current));
   183→        } else if line.ends_with(": test") || line.ends_with(": bench") {
   184→            current.push(line);
   185→        }
   186→    }
   187→
   188→    // Print grouped output
   189→    let mut total = 0;
   190→    for (i, tests) in groups.iter().enumerate() {
   191→        if tests.is_empty() {
   192→            continue;
   193→        }
   194→        let header = targets.get(i).unwrap_or(&"unknown target");
   195→        println!("{} ({} tests)", header, tests.len());
   196→        for test in tests {
   197→            // Strip ": test" / ": bench" suffix for cleaner output
   198→            let name = test
   199→                .strip_suffix(": test")
   200→                .or_else(|| test.strip_suffix(": bench"))
   201→                .unwrap_or(test);
   202→            println!("  {}", name);
   203→        }
   204→        total += tests.len();
   205→        println!();
   206→    }
   207→
   208→    println!("{} tests total", total);
   209→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
```

```json
  "new_string": "/// Format a cargo test target header into a human-friendly label.\n///\n/// Cargo emits headers like:\n/// - `unittests src/lib.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec-abc123)` → unit tests (no --test name)\n/// - `tests/tspec_test.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec_test-abc123)` → integration test\n///\n/// For integration tests, we show the `--test` usable name prominently.\nfn format_target_header(raw: &str) -> String {\n    // Strip the \"(target/...)\" suffix for cleaner output\n    let label = if let Some(idx) = raw.find(\" (\") {\n        &raw[..idx]\n    } else {\n        raw\n    };\n\n    if label.starts_with(\"unittests \") {\n        // \"unittests src/lib.rs\" → unit tests, not selectable with --test\n        format!(\"unit tests ({})\", label.strip_prefix(\"unittests \").unwrap())\n    } else if label.starts_with(\"tests/\") {\n        // \"tests/tspec_test.rs\" → extract basename for --test\n        let basename = label\n            .strip_prefix(\"tests/\")\n            .and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(\".rs\"))\n            .unwrap_or(label);\n        format!(\"{} (--test {})\", label, basename)\n    } else {\n        label.to_string()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output."
}
```

Good idea. A dedicated `--list-targets` (or similar) that shows just the `--test`-usable names would be much clearer than hunting through the full `--list` output with hundreds of function names.

How about:

```
$ tspec test -p tspec --list-targets
Available --test targets:
  tspec_test    (tests/tspec_test.rs)
```

Short, focused, and directly tells you what to pass to `--test`. The flag name `--list-targets` makes the connection explicit.

Alternatively `--list-tests` but that's confusable with `--list` (which shows test functions). I think `--list-targets` is clearest since it's specifically listing the `--test` targets. Thoughts?

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cmd/test.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output.",
  "new_string": "/// List available target names for `--test`.\n///\n/// Runs `cargo test --no-run` and parses stderr for \"Running tests/...\" lines,\n/// extracting the basename (without .rs) as the target name.\nfn list_target_names(package: Option<&str>, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n\n    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(\"cargo\");\n    cmd.arg(\"test\").arg(\"--no-run\");\n    if let Some(pkg) = package {\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(pkg);\n    }\n    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);\n    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n\n    let output = cmd.output().context(\"failed to run cargo test --no-run\")?;\n\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);\n        eprintln!(\"{}\", stderr);\n        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));\n    }\n\n    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);\n    let mut found = false;\n\n    println!(\"Available target names for --test:\");\n    for line in stderr.lines() {\n        let trimmed = line.trim();\n        // Cargo emits \"Running tests/foo.rs (target/...)\" for integration tests\n        if let Some(rest) = trimmed.strip_prefix(\"Running tests/\") {\n            let file = if let Some(idx) = rest.find(\" (\") {\n                &rest[..idx]\n            } else {\n                rest\n            };\n            if let Some(name) = file.strip_suffix(\".rs\") {\n                println!(\"  {}    (tests/{})\", name, file);\n                found = true;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if !found {\n        println!(\"  (none — no integration test files in tests/)\");\n    }\n\n    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)\n}\n\n/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output."
}
```

> AGENT

Right — `tspec test --test tspec_test` runs in workspace mode and passes `--test tspec_test` to every package, including `tspec-build` which doesn't have that target.

Two options:

1. **Require `-p` with `--test`** — add a `conflicts_with = "workspace"` and error if no package specified. Simple, explicit.
2. **Skip packages that don't have the target** — in workspace mode, treat "no test target named X" as a skip rather than a failure. More forgiving but harder to implement (need to parse cargo's error output).

I'd lean toward option 1 — if you know the target name, you know which package it's in. And it avoids hiding real errors. Thoughts?

> DEVELOPER

Hmm, if --test-target knows then we can know to, rather than skipping can we know and only do the "right" thing?

> AGENT

Good idea — we can check the filesystem. If `--test foo` is specified, just look for `tests/foo.rs` in each package directory. No extra cargo invocations needed.

```
     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace packages
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→
     5→use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
     6→use std::process::ExitCode;
     7→
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
    11→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, test_package};
    12→use crate::compare::{SpecResult, compare_specs, print_comparison};
    13→use crate::find_paths::find_tspecs;
    14→use crate::run::run_binary;
    15→use crate::tspec::spec_name_from_path;
    16→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    17→use crate::workspace::{PackageKind, WorkspaceInfo};
    18→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
    19→
    20→/// Normalize tspec patterns for per-package matching in all-packages mode.
    21→///
    22→/// Strips directory components so shell-expanded paths from the workspace root
    23→/// don't leak into sub-packages. Filters out non-tspec shell expansions
    24→/// (e.g. `target/`, `tools/`) — only `.ts.toml` files and glob patterns are kept.
    25→///
    26→/// Returns `None` if all patterns were filtered out (likely shell expansion of a
    27→/// glob that matched non-tspec entries). The caller should warn about quoting.
    28→fn normalize_tspec_patterns(patterns: &[String]) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    29→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    30→        return Some(Vec::new());
    31→    }
    32→    let filenames: Vec<String> = patterns
    33→        .iter()
    34→        .filter_map(|p| {
    35→            let name = Path::new(p)
    36→                .file_name()
    37→                .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
    38→                .unwrap_or_else(|| p.clone());
    39→            // Keep glob patterns (unexpanded) and actual tspec files
    40→            let is_glob = name.contains('*') || name.contains('?') || name.contains('[');
    41→            let is_tspec = name.ends_with(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    42→            if is_glob || is_tspec {
    43→                Some(name)
    44→            } else {
    45→                None
    46→            }
    47→        })
    48→        .collect();
    49→    if filenames.is_empty() && !patterns.is_empty() {
    50→        None // all patterns were filtered — likely shell expansion
    51→    } else {
    52→        Some(filenames)
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Warn that shell glob expansion likely ate the tspec pattern.
    57→pub fn warn_shell_glob_expansion(patterns: &[String]) {
    58→    eprintln!(
    59→        "Warning: -t arguments ({}) don't look like tspec files.",
    60→        patterns.join(", ")
    61→    );
    62→    eprintln!("  The shell likely expanded your glob before tspec could see it.");
    63→    eprintln!("  Quote the pattern to prevent shell expansion: -t 'pattern*'");
    64→}
    65→
    66→/// Resolve tspec patterns for a workspace member.
    67→///
    68→/// Returns the matching spec paths, or an empty vec if no patterns match.
    69→/// When `patterns` is empty, returns an empty vec (caller should use default behavior).
    70→fn resolve_specs_for_member(member_path: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    71→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    72→        return Vec::new();
    73→    }
    74→    find_tspecs(member_path, patterns).unwrap_or_default()
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
    78→fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    79→    match tspec {
    80→        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
    81→        None => String::new(),
    82→    }
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
    86→pub struct OpResult {
    87→    pub name: String,
    88→    pub spec: String,
    89→    pub success: bool,
    90→    pub message: String,
    91→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    92→}
    93→
    94→/// Build all workspace packages (excluding build tools)
    95→///
    96→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
    97→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
    98→pub fn build_all(
    99→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   100→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   101→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   102→    strip: bool,
   103→    fail_fast: bool,
   104→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   105→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   106→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   107→        Some(n) => n,
   108→        None => {
   109→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   110→            return Vec::new();
   111→        }
   112→    };
   113→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   114→
   115→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   116→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   117→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   118→            continue;
   119→        }
   120→
   121→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   122→
   123→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   124→            vec![None]
   125→        } else {
   126→            specs
   127→                .into_iter()
   128→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   129→                .collect()
   130→        };
   131→
   132→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   133→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   134→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   135→                Ok(build_result) => {
   136→                    if strip
   137→                        && member.has_binary
   138→                        && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
   139→                    {
   140→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   141→                    }
   142→                    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
   143→                    OpResult {
   144→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   145→                        spec,
   146→                        success: true,
   147→                        message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
   148→                        size,
   149→                    }
   150→                }
   151→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   152→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   153→                    spec,
   154→                    success: false,
   155→                    message: e.to_string(),
   156→                    size: None,
   157→                },
   158→            };
   159→
   160→            let failed = !result.success;
   161→            results.push(result);
   162→
   163→            if failed && fail_fast {
   164→                return results;
   165→            }
   166→        }
   167→    }
   168→
   169→    results
   170→}
   171→
   172→/// Run all app packages sequentially
   173→///
   174→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   175→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   176→pub fn run_all(
   177→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   178→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   179→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   180→    strip: bool,
   181→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   182→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   183→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   184→        Some(n) => n,
   185→        None => {
   186→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   187→            return Vec::new();
   188→        }
   189→    };
   190→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   191→
   192→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
   193→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   194→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   195→            continue;
   196→        }
   197→
   198→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   199→
   200→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   201→            vec![None]
   202→        } else {
   203→            specs
   204→                .into_iter()
   205→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   206→                .collect()
   207→        };
   208→
   209→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   210→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   211→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   212→                Ok(build_result) => {
   213→                    if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
   214→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   215→                    }
   216→                    match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
   217→                        Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
   218→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   219→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   220→                            success: true,
   221→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   222→                            size: None,
   223→                        },
   224→                        Err(e) => OpResult {
   225→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   226→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   227→                            success: false,
   228→                            message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   229→                            size: None,
   230→                        },
   231→                    }
   232→                }
   233→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   234→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   235→                    spec,
   236→                    success: false,
   237→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   238→                    size: None,
   239→                },
   240→            };
   241→
   242→            results.push(result);
   243→        }
   244→    }
   245→
   246→    results
   247→}
   248→
   249→/// Test all workspace packages
   250→///
   251→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   252→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   253→pub fn test_all(
   254→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   255→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   256→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   257→    fail_fast: bool,
   258→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   259→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   260→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   261→        Some(n) => n,
   262→        None => {
   263→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   264→            return Vec::new();
   265→        }
   266→    };
   267→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   268→
   269→    // Test regular packages (excluding Test kind which needs special handling)
   270→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   271→        if member.kind == PackageKind::Test {
   272→            continue; // Handle test packages separately
   273→        }
   274→
   275→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   276→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   277→            continue;
   278→        }
   279→
   280→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   281→
   282→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   283→            vec![None]
   284→        } else {
   285→            specs
   286→                .into_iter()
   287→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   288→                .collect()
   289→        };
   290→
   291→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   292→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   293→            let result = match test_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   294→                Ok(()) => OpResult {
   295→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   296→                    spec,
   297→                    success: true,
   298→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   299→                    size: None,
   300→                },
   301→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   302→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   303→                    spec,
   304→                    success: false,
   305→                    message: e.to_string(),
   306→                    size: None,
   307→                },
   308→            };
   309→
   310→            let failed = !result.success;
   311→            results.push(result);
   312→
   313→            if failed && fail_fast {
   314→                return results;
   315→            }
   316→        }
   317→    }
   318→
   319→    // Handle test packages (like rlibc-x2-tests) - build and run all test binaries
   320→    for member in workspace.test_members() {
   321→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   322→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   323→            continue;
   324→        }
   325→
   326→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   327→
   328→        // Build the test package (builds all binaries)
   329→        let build_tspec = specs.first().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
   330→        let spec = spec_label(&build_tspec);
   331→        let build_result =
   332→            match build_package(&member.name, build_tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   333→                Ok(r) => r,
   334→                Err(e) => {
   335→                    results.push(OpResult {
   336→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   337→                        spec,
   338→                        success: false,
   339→                        message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   340→                        size: None,
   341→                    });
   342→                    if fail_fast {
   343→                        return results;
   344→                    }
   345→                    continue;
   346→                }
   347→            };
   348→
   349→        // Find and run all test binaries in the target directory
   350→        let profile_dir = crate::types::profile_dir_name(cli_profile.unwrap_or("debug"));
   351→        let target_dir = build_result.target_base.join(profile_dir);
   352→
   353→        // Look for binaries that end with "-tests" in the target directory
   354→        let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
   355→            .into_iter()
   356→            .flatten()
   357→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   358→            .filter(|e| {
   359→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   360→                let is_file = e.path().is_file();
   361→                let is_test = name.ends_with("-tests");
   362→                let is_executable = e
   363→                    .metadata()
   364→                    .map(|m| m.permissions().mode() & 0o111 != 0)
   365→                    .unwrap_or(false);
   366→                is_file && is_test && is_executable
   367→            })
   368→            .collect();
   369→
   370→        if test_binaries.is_empty() {
   371→            results.push(OpResult {
   372→                name: member.name.clone(),
   373→                spec: spec.clone(),
   374→                success: false,
   375→                message: "no test binaries found".to_string(),
   376→                size: None,
   377→            });
   378→            continue;
   379→        }
   380→
   381→        for entry in test_binaries {
   382→            let path = entry.path();
   383→            let bin_name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   384→
   385→            print!("  Running {}... ", bin_name);
   386→
   387→            let result = match run_binary(&path, &[]) {
   388→                Ok(exit_code) => {
   389→                    if exit_code == 0 {
   390→                        println!("ok");
   391→                        OpResult {
   392→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   393→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   394→                            success: true,
   395→                            message: "ok".to_string(),
   396→                            size: None,
   397→                        }
   398→                    } else {
   399→                        println!("FAILED (exit {})", exit_code);
   400→                        OpResult {
   401→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   402→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   403→                            success: false,
   404→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   405→                            size: None,
   406→                        }
   407→                    }
   408→                }
   409→                Err(e) => {
   410→                    println!("FAILED");
   411→                    OpResult {
   412→                        name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   413→                        spec: spec.clone(),
   414→                        success: false,
   415→                        message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   416→                        size: None,
   417→                    }
   418→                }
   419→            };
   420→
   421→            let failed = !result.success;
   422→            results.push(result);
   423→
   424→            if failed && fail_fast {
   425→                return results;
   426→            }
   427→        }
   428→    }
   429→
   430→    results
   431→}
   432→
   433→/// A row for the summary table: package name, spec, and pre-formatted detail string.
   434→struct SummaryRow {
   435→    name: String,
   436→    spec: String,
   437→    detail: String,
   438→}
   439→
   440→/// Print a summary table with consistent formatting across all operations.
   441→///
   442→/// - `ws_name` — workspace/package name for the header
   443→/// - `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   444→/// - `detail_header` — column header for the detail column (e.g. "Status", "Status    Size", "Exit")
   445→/// - `rows` — pre-formatted rows
   446→/// - `footer` — footer line (e.g. "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed")
   447→fn print_summary_table(
   448→    ws_name: &str,
   449→    cmd: &str,
   450→    detail_header: &str,
   451→    rows: &[SummaryRow],
   452→    footer: &str,
   453→) {
   454→    let max_name_len = rows.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(7).max(7);
   455→    let has_spec = rows.iter().any(|r| !r.spec.is_empty());
   456→    let max_spec_len = if has_spec {
   457→        rows.iter().map(|r| r.spec.len()).max().unwrap_or(4).max(4)
   458→    } else {
   459→        0
   460→    };
   461→
   462→    println!();
   463→    print_header!(format!("{ws_name} {cmd} SUMMARY"));
   464→    if has_spec {
   465→        println!(
   466→            "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {detail_header}",
   467→            "Package",
   468→            "Spec",
   469→            nw = max_name_len,
   470→            sw = max_spec_len
   471→        );
   472→    } else {
   473→        println!(
   474→            "  {:width$}  {detail_header}",
   475→            "Package",
   476→            width = max_name_len
   477→        );
   478→    }
   479→
   480→    for row in rows {
   481→        if has_spec {
   482→            println!(
   483→                "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {}",
   484→                row.name,
   485→                row.spec,
   486→                row.detail,
   487→                nw = max_name_len,
   488→                sw = max_spec_len
   489→            );
   490→        } else {
   491→            println!(
   492→                "  {:width$}  {}",
   493→                row.name,
   494→                row.detail,
   495→                width = max_name_len
   496→            );
   497→        }
   498→    }
   499→
   500→    println!();
   501→    if !footer.is_empty() {
   502→        println!("  {footer}");
   503→    }
   504→    print_hline!();
   505→    println!();
   506→}
   507→
   508→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   509→pub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   510→    let mut passed = 0;
   511→    let mut failed = 0;
   512→
   513→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   514→        .iter()
   515→        .map(|r| {
   516→            let detail = if r.success {
   517→                passed += 1;
   518→                "[PASS]".to_string()
   519→            } else {
   520→                failed += 1;
   521→                "[FAIL]".to_string()
   522→            };
   523→            SummaryRow {
   524→                name: r.name.clone(),
   525→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   526→                detail,
   527→            }
   528→        })
   529→        .collect();
   530→
   531→    print_summary_table(
   532→        name,
   533→        "TEST",
   534→        "Status",
   535→        &rows,
   536→        &format!("Test: {passed} passed, {failed} failed"),
   537→    );
   538→
   539→    if failed > 0 {
   540→        ExitCode::from(1)
   541→    } else {
   542→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   543→    }
   544→}
   545→
   546→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   547→pub fn print_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   548→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   549→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   550→
   551→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   552→        .iter()
   553→        .map(|r| {
   554→            let status = if r.success {
   555→                ok_count += 1;
   556→                "[ OK ]"
   557→            } else {
   558→                failed_count += 1;
   559→                "[FAIL]"
   560→            };
   561→            let size_str = r.size.map(format_size).unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   562→            SummaryRow {
   563→                name: r.name.clone(),
   564→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   565→                detail: format!("{status}  {size_str:>6}"),
   566→            }
   567→        })
   568→        .collect();
   569→
   570→    print_summary_table(
   571→        name,
   572→        "BUILD",
   573→        "Status    Size",
   574→        &rows,
   575→        &format!("Build: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   576→    );
   577→
   578→    if failed_count > 0 {
   579→        ExitCode::from(1)
   580→    } else {
   581→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   582→    }
   583→}
   584→
   585→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   586→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   587→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   588→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   589→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   590→    } else {
   591→        format!("{}", bytes)
   592→    }
   593→}
   594→
   595→/// Result of a compare operation on a single package
   596→pub struct CompareResult {
   597→    pub op: OpResult,
   598→    pub specs: Vec<SpecResult>,
   599→}
   600→
   601→/// Compare all workspace packages that have binaries
   602→///
   603→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package discovers its own specs via default glob.
   604→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   605→pub fn compare_all(
   606→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   607→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   608→    fail_fast: bool,
   609→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   610→) -> Vec<CompareResult> {
   611→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   612→        Some(n) => n,
   613→        None => {
   614→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   615→            return Vec::new();
   616→        }
   617→    };
   618→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   619→
   620→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   621→        if !member.has_binary {
   622→            continue;
   623→        }
   624→
   625→        let spec_paths = if normalized.is_empty() {
   626→            find_tspecs(&member.path, &[]).unwrap_or_default()
   627→        } else {
   628→            let resolved = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   629→            if resolved.is_empty() {
   630→                continue;
   631→            }
   632→            resolved
   633→        };
   634→
   635→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   636→
   637→        let (op, specs) = match compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths, flags) {
   638→            Ok(spec_results) => (
   639→                OpResult {
   640→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   641→                    spec: String::new(),
   642→                    success: true,
   643→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   644→                    size: None,
   645→                },
   646→                spec_results,
   647→            ),
   648→            Err(e) => (
   649→                OpResult {
   650→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   651→                    spec: String::new(),
   652→                    success: false,
   653→                    message: e.to_string(),
   654→                    size: None,
   655→                },
   656→                Vec::new(),
   657→            ),
   658→        };
   659→
   660→        let failed = !op.success;
   661→        results.push(CompareResult { op, specs });
   662→
   663→        if failed && fail_fast {
   664→            break;
   665→        }
   666→    }
   667→
   668→    results
   669→}
   670→
   671→/// Print a summary for compare operations
   672→///
   673→/// With a single package, just prints its comparison table.
   674→/// With multiple packages, reprints all per-package tables then an overall OK/FAIL summary.
   675→pub fn print_compare_summary(name: &str, results: &[CompareResult]) -> ExitCode {
   676→    let has_failure = results.iter().any(|r| !r.op.success);
   677→
   678→    // Reprint per-package comparison tables together
   679→    for result in results {
   680→        if !result.specs.is_empty() {
   681→            print_comparison(&result.op.name, &result.specs);
   682→        }
   683→    }
   684→
   685→    // Only show overall COMPARE SUMMARY when there are multiple packages
   686→    if results.len() > 1 {
   687→        let mut ok_count = 0;
   688→        let mut failed_count = 0;
   689→
   690→        let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   691→            .iter()
   692→            .map(|r| {
   693→                let detail = if r.op.success {
   694→                    ok_count += 1;
   695→                    "[ OK ]".to_string()
   696→                } else {
   697→                    failed_count += 1;
   698→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   699→                };
   700→                SummaryRow {
   701→                    name: r.op.name.clone(),
   702→                    spec: r.op.spec.clone(),
   703→                    detail,
   704→                }
   705→            })
   706→            .collect();
   707→
   708→        print_summary_table(
   709→            name,
   710→            "COMPARE",
   711→            "Status",
   712→            &rows,
   713→            &format!("Compare: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   714→        );
   715→    }
   716→
   717→    if has_failure {
   718→        ExitCode::from(1)
   719→    } else {
   720→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   721→    }
   722→}
   723→
   724→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   725→pub fn print_run_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   726→    let mut error_count = 0;
   727→
   728→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   729→        .iter()
   730→        .map(|r| {
   731→            let detail = if r.success {
   732→                let code = r.message.strip_prefix("exit code: ").unwrap_or(&r.message);
   733→                format!("{code:>4}")
   734→            } else {
   735→                error_count += 1;
   736→                format!("ERROR: {}", r.message)
   737→            };
   738→            SummaryRow {
   739→                name: r.name.clone(),
   740→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   741→                detail,
   742→            }
   743→        })
   744→        .collect();
   745→
   746→    let footer = if error_count > 0 {
   747→        format!("Run: {error_count} error(s)")
   748→    } else {
   749→        String::new()
   750→    };
   751→
   752→    print_summary_table(name, "RUN", "Exit", &rows, &footer);
   753→
   754→    if error_count > 0 {
   755→        ExitCode::from(1)
   756→    } else {
   757→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   758→    }
   759→}
   760→
```

> AGENT

I see the approach. The simplest fix: in `TestCmd::execute()`, when in workspace mode with `--test` targets, check each package's `tests/` directory for matching files and skip packages that don't have them. This happens in `execute()` before calling `test_all`, by switching to a manual loop.

Actually, even simpler — I can add the filtering right in `test_all` since `CargoFlags.extra_args` carries the `--test` names and each member has a `path`. But that couples `test_all` to knowing about `--test` semantics.

The cleanest approach: filter in `execute()`. When workspace mode + `--test` targets, discover matching packages and run them individually instead of `test_all`.

```
    66→impl Execute for TestCmd {
    67→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    68→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    69→
    70→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    71→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    72→            None
    73→        } else {
    74→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    75→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    76→                None => current_package_name(),
    77→            }
    78→        };
    79→
    80→        if self.target_names {
    81→            return list_target_names(resolved.as_deref(), flags);
    82→        }
    83→
    84→        if self.list {
    85→            return list_tests(resolved.as_deref(), &self.name_filter, flags);
    86→        }
    87→
    88→        // Build extra_args from --names, --test, and trailing args
    89→        let has_extra = !self.name_filter.is_empty()
    90→            || !self.test_target.is_empty()
    91→            || !self.test_args.is_empty();
    92→        let flags = if has_extra {
    93→            let mut f = flags.clone();
    94→            for target in &self.test_target {
    95→                f.extra_args.push("--test".to_string());
    96→                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());
    97→            }
    98→            // Name filters and test_args both go after --
    99→            // The test harness accepts [FILTERS...] as OR-matched substrings
   100→            if !self.name_filter.is_empty() || !self.test_args.is_empty() {
   101→                f.extra_args.push("--".to_string());
   102→                f.extra_args.extend(self.name_filter.clone());
   103→                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());
   104→            }
   105→            f
   106→        } else {
   107→            flags.clone()
   108→        };
   109→
   110→        match resolved {
   111→            None => {
   112→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
   113→                let results =
   114→                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);
   115→                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
   116→            }
   117→            Some(name) => {
   118→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
   119→                    test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
   120→                } else {
   121→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
   122→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
   123→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
   124→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
   125→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
   126→                        test_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, &flags)?;
   127→                    }
   128→                }
   129→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   130→            }
   131→        }
   132→    }
   133→}
   134→
   135→/// Format a cargo test target header into a human-friendly label.
```

Now I'll add the filtering logic. When in workspace mode with `--test` targets, check each package for `tests/<name>.rs` and only include matching packages:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01DCnW2A59CcVBmTn8YPEpsc
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cmd/test.rs",
  "old_string": "        match resolved {\n            None => {\n                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;\n                let results =\n                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);\n                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))\n            }",
  "new_string": "        match resolved {\n            None => {\n                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;\n\n                // When --test targets are specified in workspace mode, only test\n                // packages that actually have matching files in tests/\n                if !self.test_target.is_empty() {\n                    let matching: Vec<_> = workspace\n                        .buildable_members()\n                        .iter()\n                        .filter(|m| {\n                            self.test_target\n                                .iter()\n                                .any(|t| m.path.join(\"tests\").join(format!(\"{t}.rs\")).exists())\n                        })\n                        .cloned()\n                        .collect();\n\n                    if matching.is_empty() {\n                        eprintln!(\n                            \"No packages contain test targets: {}\",\n                            self.test_target.join(\", \")\n                        );\n                        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));\n                    }\n\n                    for member in &matching {\n                        println!(\"=== {} ===\", member.name);\n                        test_package(&member.name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;\n                    }\n                    return Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS);\n                }\n\n                let results =\n                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);\n                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))\n            }"
}
```

All flags can be combined freely:\n`tspec test --test tspec_test -n load_spec config_kv`\n\nAll flags flow through `CargoFlags.extra_args` via clone-and-modify in\nTestCmd::execute() — no new parameters needed on test_package()/run_cargo().\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",
```json
  "description": "Amend dev1 commit"
}
```
